ROOT CAUSE: `lowerAwaitExpr` → `InstrAwait{Dst, Task}` → async-normalize `InstrPoll{Task, ReadyBB}`; the awaitable `rt_task` (spawned + refcounted by `checkpoint()`/`sleep()`, registered in the task table) is polled but never `task_release`d on the ready path (`ReadyBB`). Not cancel-specific — normal completion leaks the same per-await. Making the await's value operand consume=true is a VERIFIED NO-OP (async codegen regenerates the poll independent of it). FIX (async analog of RV2-DEBT-040): give `Task` a drop-leaf (`rt_task_release`/`free_task` — the cluster exists in rt_async_state.c), emit it at `ReadyBB` in async-normalize, and make the await CONSUME the awaitable so move-tracking prevents a NAMED handle (`let t = spawn f(); t.await()`, where `t` is also dropped by its scope) from double-releasing → UAF. Bounded (~4 spots) but real double-free risk; verify valgrind-clean + differential across all 8 async showcases. ROOT CAUSE CORRECTED ON FIX: the awaitable `rt_task` was NOT the leak — `rt_task_poll` already `task_release`s the target on the DONE path. The 48+40-byte pair per iteration was the async RESUME FRAME: the state struct box (`{__pc, __payload}`) plus the payload union box, allocated fresh at every suspend (`buildAsyncPendingBlocks` → `rt_async_yield`) and abandoned after the entry dispatch unpacked it (`__task_state` nulls `task->state`; `mark_done` also drops the final state on the floor). The small per-poll block was the discarded awaited `TaskResult` box: `drop` of a boxed union whose payloads own no heap was a backend no-op. FIXED: (1) `AsyncStateFreeBuiltin` (`__async_state_free`) emitted at the head of every pending block and before `async_return` (success only — a cancelled return's state can be re-parked by `apply_poll_outcome` while scope children drain, so its pair stays alive, one bounded pair per cancelled task); LLVM lowers it to shallow `rt_free` of both boxes + null-store, the VM treats it as a no-op (GC). (2) `emitInstrDrop` now frees non-heap-owning boxed unions through the same null-checked drop glue. Verified: `busy(1000)` awaited to completion 107,125 bytes / 3,068 blocks lost → 101 bytes / 5 blocks, iteration-independent (10 vs 5000 identical); async/05 megabytes → 16 bytes; zero invalid frees (named-handle double-await is rejected by sema — "task is neither awaited nor returned" — so no scope-drop double-release surface exists). Residual: a task cancelled mid-await leaks its final resume-frame pair + the parked awaitable `rt_task` (bounded per cancelled task, not per iteration). | Closed (resume-frame release + union-box drop) | — | `for`/`while` loops with `await` are allocation-balanced (valgrind definitely-lost independent of iteration count); named-handle awaits do not double-free. Done. |
| RV2-DEBT-045 | Directory-mode diagnostics are NONDETERMINISTIC for the type-identity mismatch class: `surge diag stdlib/` flaps between 4 and 8 errors across identical runs (even with `--jobs 1`), all of the shape "expected X, got X" (`field total_duration expects Duration, got Duration` in directives/benchmark+time, `expected &JsonValue, got &JsonValue` in http/context+response, `expected &string, got &&string` in fs). Two distinct TypeIDs for the same nominal type surface depending on module load order — a cross-module type-interning identity bug. Pre-existing (reproduces on clean HEAD with the pre-2026-07-16 binary); surfaced while differential-testing the reference-containment work; NOT caused by a stale installed stdlib (reproduced 2026-07-16 with a fresh install AND with SURGE_STDLIB pinned to the repo). CHARACTERIZED over 4 pinned runs: the identity-mismatch class splits into a STABLE subset (http/response.sg `&JsonValue vs &JsonValue` — 4/4 runs, a deterministic reproducer to debug first) and a FLAPPING subset (directives/benchmark+time `Duration vs Duration`, http/context — 2-3/4 runs, load-order dependent). Two unrelated stable bugs ride along and should be split out: fs/fs.sg:54 `expected &string, got &&string` (likely a plain fs.sg code bug — double-borrow at a call site) and the `saturating_cast` module importing a nonexistent `bounded` module (+ duplicate max_value/min_value declarations). Risks: flaky CI if stdlib diag is ever gated; user-hostile phantom errors in multi-module projects. CLOSED 2026-07-16. ROOT CAUSE (instrumented: Duration held TWO TypeIDs, 1508 and 1685, in one shared interner): one physical module compiled into TWO records because the walk keys it relative to the walk root ("time") while imports spell it from the repo root ("stdlib/time") — records were deduped by path STRING, so the module compiled twice and re-registered its exported types; consumers then bound the field type through one registration and a function signature through the other. FIX (driver): module records dedupe by PHYSICAL IDENTITY — directory for directory-shaped modules, file for file-shaped modules (sibling file-modules in one directory must not collapse; the first dir-only attempt aliased module_multitest's `import point` to main itself) — in BOTH graphs (`resolveDirModuleGraph`, `runModuleGraph`); every importer's import path is rewritten to the owning record and alias keys are threaded into `collectModuleExports` so alternate spellings see the exports AS the owner compiles (adding them after all compiles left consumers with "module has no exports"). Also (sema) the three `for key := range tc.exports` suffix-match fallbacks now pick deterministically (`moduleKeyByAliasSuffix`: exact key, then smallest suffix match) instead of relying on Go map order. Rode-along stdlib bugs fixed the same day: fs.sg double-borrow at a projection call site, and saturating_cast's dangling `import ./bounded` (commit f923bea4 had moved min_value/max_value into core intrinsics and deleted bounded.sg — the stale import caused both the missing-module AND duplicate-declaration errors; note saturating_cast has zero in-tree importers, a cull candidate for the post-v2 stdlib refresh). | Closed (dedupe by physical module identity + deterministic alias resolution) | — | `surge diag stdlib/` returns the IDENTICAL, empty error set across repeated runs with parallel jobs (verified 5/5 ×0 errors); goldens regenerate byte-identical; module_multitest sibling file-modules keep compiling. |
| RV2-DEBT-046 | async/02_fanout_fanin intermittently HANGS on the LLVM backend (futex-parked, all 66 threads sleeping, no ready task): ~1-in-20 standalone runs on the reference host, measured 2026-07-16 at 1/20 on clean HEAD (`a51940be`, pre-RV2-DEBT-044-fix binary) AND 1/20 with the DEBT-044 fix — PRE-EXISTING, rate unchanged by the resume-frame reclamation. Shape: 8 `spawn job(i)` into a `Task<int>[]` array, sequential `raw_task.await()` fan-in over `sleep((x*5):uint)` jobs; the hang parks forever instead of panicking "async deadlock", so it looks like a lost wake in the sleep-timer/join path under multi-worker scheduling (same family as the RV2-DEBT-002 lost-wake class, which was fixed for channel keys specifically). `scripts/run_showcases.sh` has NO per-run timeout, so one hung run stalls the whole differential suite indefinitely (interacts with RV2-DEBT-006's timeout-ownership debt). CLOSED 2026-07-16. ROOT CAUSE (gdb state dump of a caught hang: joiner task TASK_WAITING with park_key=JOIN(sleep-task), sleep task DONE, EVERY waiter store empty): the deferred stale-key removal in `wake_task_with_policy` is generation-qualified only for CHANNEL keys (the RV2-DEBT-002 fix); for a JOIN key the removal is UNQUALIFIED and `remove_waiter_from_store_seq(seq=0)` sweeps EVERY (key, task) match. A spurious wake consumes the joiner's park and defers the removal; the joiner re-polls and re-parks on the SAME join key in the post-unlock window; the removal then eats the fresh registration too. Join wakes are store-driven (mark_done -> wake_key_all pops the join store), so the joiner never wakes — parked forever, no deadlock panic. FIX: join keys are exempt from the deferred removal (`waker_seq0_retry_is_terminal_drained`, rt_waiter.h); a stale join entry is self-cleaning — the target completes exactly once, its drain pops every entry for the key, and the stale pop is absorbed as one spurious wake by the wake token. Timer keys stay removable (their wake is by-id from the sleep deadline index, never store-driven); channel keys keep the generation-qualified removal. PROOF: deterministic sync-point harness (`SP_WAKE_BEFORE_STALE_REMOVAL` + `debt046-join-stale-removal-proof`, positive at SURGE_SHARDS=1/2/8, negative control `RV2_DEBT_046_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` strands with the expected message); empirically 0 hangs / 1000 consecutive standalone runs (baseline ~1 in 20-30). run_showcases.sh now bounds every run (`SHOWCASE_RUN_TIMEOUT`, default 120s; exit 124 fails the case with a TIMEOUT note instead of stalling the suite). Runtime C debug objects now build with -g (target/debug profile), which made the gdb state-dump diagnosis possible. | Closed (join keys exempt from deferred removal; sync-point proof + 1000-run gate) | — | Fan-in survives >=1000 consecutive runs (was >=200 required); `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleDebt046JoinStaleRemovalProof` + negative control gate the window; run_showcases.sh fails hung runs in bounded time. |
| RV2-DEBT-047 | The transport shutdown drain can PANIC on valid queued crossing messages: `rt_remote_spawn_fail_all_pending` (`runtime/native/rt_remote_spawn.c`) drains every shard's inbound queue at shutdown behind an allowlist that admits only `REMOTE_SPAWN_*`/`REMOTE_TASK_*`/`NONE` kinds — any queued `IMMEDIATE_ON_*`, `FAR_CHANNEL_*` (create/share/select request+reply), or `CREDIT_CONTROL` message hits `panic_msg("remote spawn: unsupported transport message kind during shutdown")` BEFORE the release helpers run, even though `rt_remote_task_release_msg_payload` (`runtime/native/rt_remote_task_completion.c`) already knows how to release exactly those payload kinds. The allowlist predates the immediate-on (Epic 13) and far-channel/select (Epics 14/17) message families and was never widened. Found 2026-07-17 during the Epic 20 draft review (codex second opinion, confirmed by direct code read). Reachability window: a message enqueued after the last steady-state drain and before shutdown drain; no observed in-suite failure yet — a deterministic repro row is part of the fix. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 5 row 1 (commit `9f27d86d` + the harness row): the allowlist is now a kind-complete release switch (every production kind falls through to `remote_spawn_release_msg_payload` + `rt_remote_task_release_msg_payload`; only truly unknown kinds fail closed with "unknown transport message kind during shutdown"), and the switch itself is the static exhaustiveness guard (`-Wswitch` fails the build until a new kind takes a release stance). Proof: `shutdown-queued-kinds` harness mode (enqueues IMMEDIATE_ON execute/reply, FAR_CHANNEL create/share/select request+reply, CREDIT_CONTROL, then calls `rt_remote_spawn_fail_all_pending` — no panic, queues drained) + updated static-shape assertions pinning the new message and per-kind case coverage (`TestRuntimeV2RemotePublicationFailurePathStaticGuards`). | Closed (kind-complete release switch + queued-at-shutdown harness row) | — | Done: shutdown drain releases every production kind; the exhaustiveness guard is the release switch itself; the queued-message row covers the post-Epic-13 families. |
| RV2-DEBT-048 | SOUNDNESS: select send arms are invisible to ownership. Three linked gaps, verified 2026-07-17 on LLVM with Epic 19 drops active (probe corrected the same day: STRING LITERALS ARE STATIC and never heap-allocate, so the first literal-payload probe round was vacuous — all rows below use loop-built heap strings). (1) Marker inconsistency: direct `ch.send(s)` rejects with `SEM3015: expected own string` demanding `ch.send(own s)`, but the same spelling inside a select arm compiles bare. (2) The payload is never marked moved: using `s` after a select whose send arm WON compiles and runs (use-after-move read — printed the delivered value). (3) DOUBLE FREE, deterministic 3/3: when the send arm wins, the delivered value is freed by the receiver's scope exit AND by the sender's scope exit (`free(): double free detected in tcache 2`, native LLVM). A LOSING send arm reclaims correctly (payload freed once at sender scope exit; the residual definitely-lost blocks are the two `rt_channel_new` channel OBJECTS — runtime-backed Channel values have no drop glue, the known pre-arc model for runtime handles, out of the drop arc's current scope). No unsafe/@intrinsic involved. RESIDUAL UPDATE 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 7): the residual's FAR half is closed — see RV2-DEBT-060, which gives the owner-side channel object a free path via the far-registry's existing lease-count reclaim predicate. The LOCAL half stays open and is now precisely bounded, not just deferred: `Channel<T>` is `@copy` (`core/intrinsics.sg`), and `isDroppableType` (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go`) excludes Copy types before any other check, so a local `own Channel<T>` binding never acquires a scope-exit drop obligation at all — there is no leaf-gated-but-missing case to wire up, unlike the far handle. `struct rt_channel` carries no refcount field and `rt_channel_close` only sets a flag; making local channels droppable needs either refcounting added to a type kept Copy on purpose (the golden `let sender_ch = ch; let recv_ch = ch;` fan-out shape depends on it) or an affine redesign with its own `.share()` equivalent — a language-semantics decision, not a backend task. Epic 21 forks 4-5 (`21-owner-routed-frees.md`) already anticipated exactly this contingency and recorded the fallback of deferring it; no new debt row needed for the local half, this note is that deferral's record. Fix direction (Epic 20 design review, fork 6): send-arm payloads join move tracking with PER-BRANCH move semantics — moved in the winner branch, still owned in every other arm's branch, maybe-moved (unusable, kind diagnostic) after the join — which is exactly the Epic 19 per-arm drop synthesis pattern; require the `own` marker for spelling consistency. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 3 (`20-tasks/03-select-send-arm-ownership.md`): `typeSelectAwaitExpr` now applies direct-send ownership discipline to local send arms (payload assignability vs the channel payload type; `own` marker for non-copy payloads — SEM3140; whole-binding payloads — SEM3141), and `typeSelectExpr` runs the compare-expression per-arm machinery (per-arm moved snapshots, union merge, `ArmDropsExpr` one-sided drop synthesis) so losing arms reclaim and post-join use is use-of-moved (SEM3130). The declared-outside-the-loop retry shape hits the standing back-edge conservatism with its recreate-each-iteration hint; the accepted retry form declares the payload inside the loop. Proof: goldens `select_send_missing_own`, `select_send_temp_payload`, `select_send_use_after_join`, `select_send_payload_type_mismatch`, `select_send_move_across_loop_backedge`, valid `select_send_own_binding` (losing-arm use + retry form); `TestRuntimeV2DropSelectSendArm` (exact free-count windows: winner-select 2 / winner-recv 0 / winner-drop-got 2 = payload+box exactly once at the receiver; loser-select 3 incl. in-arm reclaim; `SURGE_THREADS=1,2`) wired into `runtime-v2-heap-check`; VM parity via the VM sanitizer (same program clean on both backends); the pre-fix 3/3 native double-free reproducer runs clean; manual valgrind winner/loser 0 definitely-lost. `make check` + byte-stable golden regen + Sentrux `internal` 6484→6486 green. | Closed (per-branch move semantics + own marker; SEM3140/3141; heap-check rows) | — | Winning send arm frees the payload exactly once (census rows, loop-built payloads) — done; losing arms keep the value usable inside their bodies and reclaim at branch end — done; use after the select join is a compile error — done (SEM3130 + move hint); spelling matches direct send — done; accept+reject goldens — done. |
| RV2-DEBT-051 | Crossing captures whose struct carries a HEAP field arrive corrupted and leak on the happy path (pre-existing — reproduced 2026-07-17 with the drop-registration changes stashed). Repro: `@shard_movable type Job = { id: int, note: string }` with `note: build_note(...)` (runtime-built string), `spawn on distributed { ret describe(own j); }`, `far Task.await()`. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 5 row 2 — two independent defects, neither in the transport. (a) CORRUPTION = caller-side UAF before publish, not a pack/unpack mismatch: sema's statement-end temp-drop pass did not treat `own <expr>` as consuming its operand (`ExprUnaryOwn` had no `consumeTempCandidate`, unlike `&`/`&mut`), so the struct literal under `own Job{...}` stayed flagged and MIR dropped the "temp" at statement end — while BOTH backends lower `own` as identity over the boxed value, so the temp and the own binding are the SAME box: the drop freed the Job box `j` still pointed to, and the body read the freed box's slot 0 (allocator-reused pointer bits = the garbage `j.id`). All-Copy captures were masked because a no-heap-field struct is not droppable, so no statement-end drop was ever emitted for them. The UAF was NOT crossing-specific — any `own <producer>` of a heap-bearing struct dangled, local code included. FIX: `own` consumes its temp candidate (`internal/sema/type_expr_ops.go`; unit row `TestOwnOperandTransfersIntoBox`). The earlier "arity mismatch" evidence was a misread: the state box is 16B because `own T` layout resolves to T's layout (one slack slot), but pack, unpack, and registered drop all agree on one pointer field at offset 0. (b) LEAK = the shipped state envelope was never freed on the happy path: `__task_state()` hands the envelope to the body and nulls `task->state`; local async polls release their consumed state+payload boxes via `__async_state_free` before AsyncReturn (the resume-frame-reclamation model), but crossing poll bodies returned without the release. FIX: `rewriteSpawnOnPollReturns` releases the envelope box shallowly before AsyncReturn (captures were already unpacked at entry; the pending-side `__surge_drop_call` covers only never-handed-off states, so no state sees both releases) — covers spawn-on, immediate-on, and anchored bodies. PROOF: repro reads `j.id`/`j.note` correctly at shards 1/2/8; the crossing repro's valgrind definite-leak signature is now IDENTICAL to a no-crossing local-async control (shared baseline only); e2e `TestRuntimeV2CrossingHeapCaptureArrivesIntact` (mixed capture verified by content, shards 1/2/8, transport gate) + `TestRuntimeV2CrossingHeapCaptureCensusBalanced` (per-iteration far-loop heap growth == local spawn/await loop growth, 1 shard deterministic, drop-reclamation gate). Residual split to RV2-DEBT-052: every `compare` over an owned boxed union leaks the scrutinee box — local and far alike; the census row cancels it via the far-vs-local comparison and tightens automatically when 052 lands. | Closed (own consumes its operand; crossing bodies release the envelope; arrival + census e2e gated) | — | Done: a crossing body reads exactly the captured field values for mixed Copy/heap structs; the crossing adds zero happy-path heap growth over the local-await baseline; the Task 5 abandon rows build on the fixed contract. |
| RV2-DEBT-052 | `compare` over an owned boxed union never frees the scrutinee box on the LLVM backend: the scrutinee moves into the lowering temp, arms extract the tag payload by copy, and nothing releases the union box afterwards — one 16-byte block per evaluated compare (e.g. every consumed `TaskResult` from `t.await()`), local and far alike. CLOSED 2026-07-19 (Epic 20 Task 8 row 1, codex-implemented, adversarially reviewed, lead-verified; fixed FIRST per the user's honest-strict-zero decision). Same class as RV2-DEBT-040: the `__cmpN` scrutinee is a synthetic normalize-pass let created after sema computed drop obligations. FIX: per-arm release synthesis in `normalizeCompareExpr` (`normalize_compare_release.go`) gated on ownership-transferring scrutinees (non-Copy incl. `@copy`-attr awareness, non-borrowed, union-kind): an arm whose payload positions are ALL plain bindings releases the box shallowly through the DEBT-040 channel (renamed to `StmtEnvelopeRelease`/`InstrEnvelopeRelease`, mnemonics `release_box`/`release_cursor` — it now serves both consumers honestly); no-payload/wildcard/fallback arms deep-drop through the union's drop glue; mixed payload shapes and the whole-value binding arm skip the release (leak over wrong-free, recorded residuals); guards release only in the passed branch. Sema soundness pinned both ways (`TestCompareOwnedScrutineeMarksBindingMoved` existing + new Copy-side counterpart). PROOF: valgrind on the spawn+await+compare control: 32B/2 blocks definitely lost -> 0/0 (also explains the two 16B blocks previously misattributed to entry states in the RV2-DEBT-051/053 investigations — they were TaskResult scrutinee boxes); 5-probe census `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` at strict d(2000)-d(1)==0 (drop gate); the heap-capture census TIGHTENED from far==local equality to independent d8==d1 AND b8==b1. Found en route: RV2-DEBT-057 (union-cast box leak), RV2-DEBT-058 (compare-arm payload bindings never get scope-exit drops). | Closed (per-arm scrutinee release; strict-zero census; renamed shared envelope-release channel) | — | Done: a consumed union scrutinee frees exactly once on every arm shape that transfers ownership. RESIDUALS CLOSED 2026-07-31 (commit `80c74378`). The MIXED-payload arm leaked its envelope once per evaluation, measured at 384 bytes over 16 evaluations of `Pair(s, _) => len(s)`: a shallow free would have abandoned the ignored field and a deep drop would have freed the bound one twice, so neither ran. A position the pattern ignored now gets a binding of its own — the same `let` a named position gets, on the same arm-return drop list — which makes the arm all-bound and the existing shallow release correct again; a position that owns no heap needs no binding and stops blocking the release on its own account (the `int` in `Pair(s, _)` is bits the shallow free carries away). Only for a scrutinee the arm really owns: a borrowed one keeps its payload and a `@copy` one was cloned. A payload that CLONES when read is left alone too, and that one was learned the hard way: the first cut of this fix bound every ignored heap-owning position, which for a `@copy` composite gives the binding a CLONE while the original stays in the envelope — the arm then freed that envelope shallowly and abandoned it. Measured as a REGRESSION (16 bytes per evaluation of `compare Hold(Pair{...}) { Hold(_) => 1; ... }` with a `@copy` `Pair`, clean at the parent commit, where the arm was all-unbound and deep-dropped correctly); found by an independent review, fixed in `444efcc8`. The census could not see it because every union in it carried a `string`, which MOVES — it now also carries a non-`@copy` union holding a `@copy` payload, in the plain and the never-passing-guard spellings. Nested and literal payload patterns are STILL refused — they take out parts of a field, which the normalize pass does not model, and that is the remaining bound. The WHOLE-VALUE binding arm was re-measured and is already clean (0 definitely-lost over 16 evaluations), so it needed nothing. Gates: two new rows in `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` at the same strict d(2000)-d(1)==0 — an arm binding the owning field while ignoring bits, and its mirror where the IGNORED field is the owner — red before the fix (exit 121). Also measured clean: two ignored fields at once, and a mixed arm behind a FAILING guard, on both backends. |
| RV2-DEBT-053 | A completed remote body's OWNED RESULT is never reclaimed when the awaiting caller abandons the reply (found 2026-07-19 by the Epic 20 Task 5 row-6 investigation, codex-verified then confirmed by direct read). The shipped-STATE contract (publication-accepted handoff) covers only the body's captures via `state_drop_fn_id`; the reply-edge RESULT has no drop obligation anywhere: (a) release-while-DONE — `dispatch_release` (`rt_remote_task_dispatch.c`) sees TASK_DONE, skips cancel, releases the owner task; `free_task` never inspects `task->result_bits`, so a heap-carried result (boxed bigint/string — any LLVM-boxed value) leaks permanently; (b) orphaned caller pending — a reply that already landed in the caller-side `rt_remote_task_pending.result_bits` is stranded forever if the caller task is cancelled before its next poll: the pending lives in an OPAQUE-POINTER async-frame slot with no drop tracking, and task teardown has no path that finds it — `rt_remote_task_pending.caller_task_id` is a DEAD FIELD for AWAIT/CANCEL/RELEASE ops (never assigned in `start_remote_task`/`rt_far_task_release`), while the EXECUTE/CHANNEL_SELECT ops set it and get exactly the needed sweep (`rt_immediate_on_release_owned`, op filter `rt_immediate_on.c` ~295). Plain-Copy results (fixnum) are inert bits — no manifestation. The leak is silent (no counter/log on the discard paths). Preferred fix per the investigation: (1) populate `caller_task_id` for AWAIT/CANCEL and widen the existing caller-teardown sweep's op filter (single-shot discipline mirroring `consume_handle` so it cannot race the compiled `finish_retry` consume), then (2) layer a `result_drop_fn_id` threaded like the state's `BodyFuncID` (payload type is known at the crossing lowering site) for the actual drop; a caller-side frame-drop sweep was considered and rejected as the same opaque-pointer-bypasses-ARC hazard class as RV2-DEBT-051. Stopgap candidate: a debug counter/assertion on the two discard paths to make the leak visible in census rows. **2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 5): the OWNER-SIDE half (053a) is CLOSED.** A `result_drop_fn_id` is now threaded from the spawn-on crossing (`emitSpawnOnCrossing`, keyed on the body's `PayloadType`) through `rt_remote_spawn_publish`/`_placement` and the spawn pending into the created body task (`task->result_drop_fn_id`), mirroring the state's `BodyFuncID`. `free_task` (`rt_task_lifetime.c`) drops an unconsumed heap result exactly once via a new `__surge_drop_result_call` dispatch + per-payload-type `drop_result.typeN` wrappers (its OWN id space keyed by TypeID, separate from the state switch's FuncID space, because a result may be a heap leaf — string/dyn-array — needing full value-drop, not just struct-box glue). Mutual exclusion with the consume path is by construction: every owner-side site that ships/hands out `result_bits` (the AWAIT reply in `dispatch_request` + `rt_remote_task_reply_owner_done`; local `rt_far_task_take_result`) clears `result_drop_fn_id` to 0 when ownership transfers, so a consumed result is never double-freed. The check lives in `free_task` (not a dispatch_release-only finalizer) because free_task is the sole free chokepoint for every abandon route and already hosts result-lease cleanup. Proof: deterministic `TestRuntimeV2RemoteTaskBehavior` rows `result-owner-release` (drop fires exactly once with the right id+pointer; negative control with the fix disabled fails "not dropped exactly once"), `result-copy-inert` (id 0 → no drop), `result-consumed-no-double-drop` (cleared → no double-free); LLVM emission row `TestEmitSpawnOnRegistersResultDropFn` pins the codegen. NOTE: a compiled-Surge heap-far-result leak is UNREACHABLE today — the non-copy reply gate (`crossingRecordExecutable`, Epic 21 Task 8, still closed) refuses non-copy far results at build time, so the fix is preventive machinery, dormant (resultDropID=0) for every currently-compilable program; the proofs therefore drive the owner-side free path directly (harness) and the codegen at MIR level (bypassing the gate). **2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 6): the CALLER-SIDE half (053b) is CLOSED.** `start_remote_task` now populates `caller_task_id` for AWAIT/CANCEL (previously a dead field on those two ops) and takes a `result_drop_fn_id`, threaded from the crossing lowering site's known payload type through `rt_far_task_await`/`rt_far_task_cancel`'s new parameter and stored on the pending. `rt_remote_task_pending`'s existing free path drops an unconsumed heap result exactly once via `__surge_drop_result_call`, mirroring the owner-side mechanism from 053a. A new caller-teardown sweep (`rt_remote_task_release_owned`, called from `mark_done` alongside the existing far-task and immediate-on sweeps) scans pendings by `caller_task_id` on task death and gives up the caller's own reference — deliberately not a routed cancel to the owner, since the owner-side `consume_handle` CAS was already spent by the original `.await()`/`.cancel()` and a second attempt would produce a bogus reply while leaking the owner's reference (confirmed by direct trace during design review, not assumed). The sweep must unlink-then-release (`rt_remote_task_pending_consume`, not a bare release) even though it may not be the last reference: a bare release without unlinking left the struct dangling in the registry list when it did turn out to be the last ref, caught by the negative-control harness rows before this closed (a real double-free, not hypothetical). Proof: five new `TestRuntimeV2RemoteTaskBehavior` rows — `caller-abandon-drops-landed-result-exactly-once`, `caller-abandon-copy-inert` and `caller-abandon-consumed-no-double-drop` (negative controls), `caller-abandon-filters-by-op-and-caller` (proves the sweep only touches the matching op+caller among siblings), `caller-abandon-in-flight-pending-survives` (proves a still-in-flight pending outlives the sweep on its remaining ref). Same UNREACHABLE note as 053a applies: the non-copy reply gate (Epic 21 Task 8, still open) keeps this dormant for every currently-compilable program today; the proofs drive the C-level pending lifecycle and MIR-level codegen directly. | Closed | Epic 20 Task 5 row 6 named the edge; 053a owner-side landed Epic 21 Task 5; 053b caller-side landed Epic 21 Task 6 | Both halves closed: a caller gone before consuming the reply reclaims a heap-carried result exactly once on both paths (release-while-DONE and orphaned landed reply); a census/harness row pins each; the AWAIT/CANCEL `caller_task_id` field is populated and load-bearing. |
| RV2-DEBT-055 | `for _ in arr` (discarded loop variable) fails to COMPILE on both backends: "MIR validation failed: function main: local L3 (__iter1): unknown type" — `normalizeIterFor`'s element-type resolution (`data.VarType`, falling back to `ctx.bindingType(data.VarSym)`) never falls back to the iterable's own element type when the pattern binds no symbol. Pre-existing (verified on unmodified HEAD 2026-07-19, found during the RV2-DEBT-040 fix). Consequence: the discarded-payload branch of the iterator release (full drop of the unconsumed Option box) is correct but unreachable dead code until this is fixed. CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 1): added `iterableElementType` (`internal/hir/normalize_for.go`), a third fallback tried after `VarType`/`bindingType(VarSym)` both come up empty — it resolves the iterable expression's own type (peeling `&`/`own`/`*` wrappers), then tries `ArrayInfo` (dynamic arrays), `ArrayFixedInfo` (fixed-size arrays), and finally a single-type-argument generic struct instance (`Range<T>`, the only such iterable this protocol builds). Verified on both backends (LLVM and VM share the one HIR normalization pass): a negative control (fix reverted) reproduces the exact ledgered error text; with the fix, `for _ in <array>`, `for _ in <stored Range<T>>`, and `for _ in <fixed-size array>` all compile and run correctly. New probe `array_for_discard_n_times` added to the existing `TestRuntimeV2IterProtocolReclamationCensusBalanced` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_iter_protocol_reclamation_e2e_test.go`, same n=1/n=2000 differential convention as its sibling probes) exercises the discarded-payload release branch this row's own text flagged as dead code — it is correct, no leak. | Closed (normalizeIterFor falls back to the iterable's own element type) | — | `for _ in <iterable>` compiles and runs on both backends — done; the discarded-payload release branch gets a census row — done (`array_for_discard_n_times`); the element type falls back to the iterable's element type when no binding exists — done. |
| RV2-DEBT-057 | Casting a union value between two structurally-identical but distinctly-interned union type IDs leaks the source box on the LLVM backend: `emitUnionCast` (internal/backend/llvm/emit_union_cast.go) allocates a fresh box and never frees the one it replaces — reproduces on a plain `let v: Outcome = Payload(...)` with no compare involved (16B definitely lost per cast, valgrind-confirmed on clean HEAD). Wide potential blast radius: any tag-constructor result assigned to a differently-interned union binding. Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-052 fix (its census probes construct scrutinees directly to avoid this confound; the residual constants in the tightened heap-capture census are candidates of this class). CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 1): `emitUnionCast` frees the old envelope exactly once after every payload field it needs is copied into SSA temporaries, guarded by the same `isRefType` ownership check the function already used for its deref branch (a borrowed `&T` source is never freed — only a genuinely owned source, which this cast always operates on: either a fresh tag-constructor temporary or a binding already consumed by the enclosing let/assignment's own move semantics). Verified: negative control (fix reverted) reproduces the exact pre-fix ledger shape on a 500-iteration loop (8,000B/500 blocks, valgrind); with the fix, that program and showcase 26 (a real, previously-shipped program using this exact `let out: State = Running(...)` pattern) both drop to 0 valgrind errors / a measured 108-byte, 9-block reduction respectively (showcase 26's small residual, 42B/2 blocks, is a separate, already-ledgered gap — RV2-DEBT-058's class — not this one). New gate row `TestRuntimeV2DropUnionCastReclamation` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_drop_union_cast_e2e_test.go`): an exact single-window free-count assertion (frees=1) plus an n=1/n=500 alloc/free differential proving the fix holds under a cast-heavy loop, not just once; wired into `runtime-v2-heap-check`. The heap-capture census residual constants (d=1, b=3) are NOT re-attributed by this fix — they persist unchanged, so they are not this class (falls to RV2-DEBT-058 or another cause; left for that investigation). | Closed (emitUnionCast frees its source envelope exactly once; guarded by the existing borrow check) | — | The union-cast source box frees exactly once — done; a census row windows a cast-heavy loop at strict zero — done (`TestRuntimeV2DropUnionCastReclamation`); the heap-capture census residual constants shrink or are re-attributed with evidence — NOT this fix, still open, re-attributed to investigate under RV2-DEBT-058 or a separate cause. |
| RV2-DEBT-058 | Compare-arm payload bindings never acquire scope-exit drop obligations: `Payload(s) => consume_locally(s)` type-checks via `inferComparePatternTypes` but the binding never reaches `registerDroppableBinding` (only the `ast.StmtLet` walk does that), so a heap payload bound-and-consumed-locally in an arm leaks — masked everywhere today because the ubiquitous shape `Success(x) => x` MOVES the binding out. Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-052 fix (its bound/guarded probes deliberately use the moving shape). Also the recorded reason RV2-DEBT-052's mixed-payload and whole-value-binding residuals had to err toward leaking: without binding-level obligations there is no safe owner to hand the box to. CLOSED 2026-07-30 together with RV2-DEBT-087, which is this row found again from the other side — the diagnosis here (only the `let` walk reaches registerDroppableBinding) was exactly right. See 087 for the fix and for the second half nobody predicted: the arm's drops were emitted before the arm's value, so an arm that READS its own payload freed it and then read it. | Closed (with RV2-DEBT-087) | Sema follow-up (teach the compare-arm binding path to register droppable bindings; then revisit the 052 residual arm shapes) | An arm-bound heap payload consumed locally reclaims at arm end; the 052 residual shapes (mixed payloads, whole-value binding) can then release through the binding instead of leaking; census rows pin both. |
| RV2-DEBT-059 | A task cancelled before its body ever ran leaks its compiled async frame: `spawn body(); child.cancel(); child.await()` — where `body` contains any internal `.await()` — leaks exactly one init state+payload pair per occurrence (32B direct + 24B indirect on the minimal local repro; the far-task caller-cancel shape carries the same defect plus its capture/handle state, 72B/occurrence), and leaks ZERO when the cancel line is removed. Root cause class: `rewriteAsyncReturns` deliberately keeps a cancelled frame's state alive (the runtime may re-park/re-enter it while scope children drain) and the pair is "abandoned once the task completes" — but no abandon-time drain ever reclaims it. Bounded and proportional to cancelled-task count, never growing per-task. Found 2026-07-20 by the Epic 20 Task 8 carried caller-cancel e2e (`TestRuntimeV2FarTaskCallerCancel`), whose valgrind census pins SAFE-AND-BOUNDED (crash-class valgrind errors fail; the loss must stay a clean multiple of the per-occurrence unit) instead of asserting zero, precisely so this row stays visible. CLOSED 2026-07-20 by `bdcc0695`: both cancelled exits (`rt_async_yield` and `rt_async_return_cancelled`) record the state and its TypeID-keyed drop function once on `rt_task`, and `mark_done` consumes and clears that obligation exactly once. The focused A/B behind `TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed` removes the 32-byte outer state box (32B direct + 24B indirect before, 24B direct + 0 indirect after). The remaining nested `Task<T>`/`Channel<T>` handle leak is RV2-DEBT-062, not an unclosed part of 059; RV2-DEBT-061 remains an independently scoped crossing race, so the broader caller-cancel census is intentionally not misrepresented as strict zero. | Closed (outer abandoned async state reclaimed exactly once) | Epic 21 Task 6 (`bdcc0695`) | Commit `bdcc0695fd0e9074ad4a52d14bacb6eb77c6dc03`; `TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed` plus the recorded pre/post Valgrind A/B prove the 059-owned outer state box is gone. Residual nested handles remain explicitly under RV2-DEBT-062. |
| RV2-DEBT-060 | Every `channel_on(...)` and every `.share()` leaks its caller-side `far Channel<T>` handle box: the LLVM backend allocates it via `rt_far_channel_handle_alloc` (emit_crossing_channel_create.go, emit_crossing_share.go) and no generated code path ever frees it — `rt_far_channel_release` is fully implemented in the runtime and NEVER CALLED from compiled code (zero references outside its defining file). Deterministic and shard-topology-independent: the strict-census valgrind program loses exactly 1,280B/52 blocks at 1, 2, and 8 shards. Root asymmetry: `far Task<T>` handles retire on `.await()` consumption, but `far Channel<T>` has no consuming operation — used repeatedly, never awaited away — so the handle box needs DROP GLUE, which channels do not have (the recorded pre-arc channel model, RV2-DEBT-048's residual class). A share() call additionally leaves the dispatch-side sibling lease still-reachable via the global registry (same pre-arc class, not newly found). Affects every Epic 16/17 e2e that creates channels; none ran under valgrind until the Task 8 strict census (2026-07-20), which asserts the exact documented bound at both tiers so any magnitude change trips. CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 7, FAR half only — see RV2-DEBT-048 for the local-channel residual this row's own text already flagged as a distinct class): the handle box now frees via a new `isFarChannelType` leaf case in `emitInstrDrop` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_instr.go`, NOT `emitDropValue` — an earlier attempt to also add the case there, for composite-field drops, turned out to race a pre-existing bug and was reverted; see RV2-DEBT-061) dispatching to the already-implemented `rt_far_channel_handle_drop`. The owner-side `rt_channel` OBJECT (the RV2-DEBT-048 residual's far half) now frees too: a new `rt_channel_free` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c`, single `rt_free` reconstructing the exact `rt_channel_new` allocation size from the stored capacity) is called from `release_entry` (`runtime/native/rt_far_channel.c`) — the single choke point every reclaim path (release, release_all's shutdown sweep, unpin-to-zero) already routes through, gated on the SAME `active_leases==0 && inflight==0` predicate `.share()`'s sibling leases already exercise correctly (Epic 16). Traced both mint call sites directly: the registry is the channel object's sole owner from creation on, so this is exactly-once by construction, not by convention. HONEST SCOPE: reclaims the channel object (struct+buffer) for any element type; does NOT drain buffered-but-unreceived payloads (raw bits, no destructor path — RV2-DEBT-060's non-copy half rides Epic 21's Task 8/fork 5). For `Channel<int>`/fixnum T — every program this fix's tests use, since ChannelCreate stays Copy-only until fork 5 — this is a complete fix, no payload can leak. New gate row `TestRuntimeV2DropFarChannelHandleAndObjectValgrindZero` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_drop_far_channel_e2e_test.go`): create + four independent `.share()` leases + scope exit, strict zero at 1/2/8 shards; deliberately does NOT exercise actual send/recv (`on ch {...}`) — see RV2-DEBT-061, a pre-existing, unrelated race that fuller send/recv programs hit intermittently and would make a census flaky. Negative control: 120B/5 blocks on unmodified HEAD (1 channel_on + 4 shares, matching `rt_far_task_handle`'s 24-byte size), 0/0 with the fix across 10/10 manual runs plus the automated gate. | Closed (handle box + owner-object free, FAR channels only; local channels are RV2-DEBT-048's residual) | — | A `far Channel<T>` binding's death releases its handle box exactly once — done; the strict-census tiers tighten from documented bounds to zero for the share/select verticals — done for create+share (this row's gate); sibling leases retire with their entries — done (registry predicate unchanged, now also gates the object free). |
| RV2-DEBT-064 | SOUNDNESS, pre-existing, newly found 2026-07-20 while building RV2-DEBT-059/062's non-copy remote-select e2e coverage (Epic 21 Task 8): `emitChannelSelectCrossing` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_crossing_select.go`) has exactly ONE call site to `rt_far_channel_select`, shared between the true first attempt and every retry — unlike `emitChannelCreateCrossing`, which deliberately splits into separate `initBB`/`retryBB` blocks specifically so capacity/drop-id are computed ONCE (the retry branch passes literal `i64 0, i64 0, i64 0` instead of re-evaluating anything). Because a remote select is a crossing like any other, it needs at least one suspend/resume round trip to the arms' owner shard even at `SURGE_SHARDS=1` (matches every other crossing's self-crossing-still-uses-transport behavior); the compiled retry re-enters the SAME MIR block containing the whole arm-building loop, so a SEND arm's owned payload expression (`ch1.send(own job1)`) gets RE-EVALUATED on every retry — confirmed directly in emitted LLVM IR: the `build()`-style payload constructor was called twice per retry round (4 total calls across 2 rounds) feeding a `rt_far_channel_select` call whose OWN retry branch (`runtime/native/rt_far_channel_select.c`, `*pending != NULL`) never reads `send_bits`/`send_drop_fn_ids` at all — so every retry's freshly-built payload is silently discarded (leaked), and the FINAL, successful call's arm array holds whichever build happened to run last. Reproduces as a deterministic crash, not just a leak: `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` on a minimal two-arm non-copy select (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_far_channel_noncopy_e2e_test.go`'s abandoned `TestRuntimeV2FarChannelSelectNonCopyWinnerConsumedOnce`, removed from that file pending this fix rather than left permanently red). Confirmed NOT caused by RV2-DEBT-059/062's own new mechanism: the round-trip send/recv row (create + one send + one recv, no select) is clean under valgrind (only the pre-existing baseline residual noted in that same test file); this row's `record_select_commit`/`select_committed_index` write-once-read-once tracking was independently verified correct via direct instrumentation (traced winner/loser indices and per-arm drop-fn-id/bits at the free site — the skip/drop decision was exactly right every time) before this root cause was found — the crash is upstream of that mechanism, in the crossing's own retry plumbing, not in it. Distinct from RV2-DEBT-048 (CLOSED): that row's per-arm move-semantics fix (SEM3140/3141) is for LOCAL selects, whose winner is decided synchronously in one function; this row is specifically about the REMOTE crossing's async retry reconstructing owned arm values it should only ever build once. CLOSED 2026-07-23, and the mechanism above is CORRECTED — the crash is real and reproduced, but the retry re-evaluation this row blamed for it does NOT reproduce. ROOT CAUSE (valgrind, both frees and their order captured): DOUBLE OWNERSHIP of a losing SEND arm's payload on the normal, non-cancelled completion path. The runtime drops every non-committed arm in `rt_remote_task_pending_release`'s `select_arms` loop, reached on that path via `rt_far_channel_select` -> `rt_immediate_on_finish_retry` -> `..._consume` -> `..._release`; compiled code then drops the SAME pointer again, because sema's per-arm drop synthesis (`internal/sema/type_expr_select.go` ~92-121 — the moved-set union plus `oneSidedDroppables`, shared verbatim with `compare` and with LOCAL select, no far-specific branch) places a drop of every other arm's moved payload into the winning arm's block. FIX: hand the arm table's payload ownership back to the caller exactly when a winner reply reaches compiled code — new `select_finish_retry`/`select_disown_arms` (`runtime/native/rt_far_channel_select.c`) zero every arm's `payload_drop_fn_id` when the terminal reply is a genuine winner, so the free path drops nothing there; every other outcome (Cancelled reply, failed status, teardown that never reaches the caller) keeps the obligation on the free path unchanged. This mirrors the established clear-on-consume idiom for `result_drop_fn_id` (`rt_remote_task_api.c`'s `finish_retry`) but is GATED where that one is unconditional: a non-success result leaves `result_bits` at 0 so there is nothing to take, whereas an arm payload stays fully alive on a Cancelled reply AND the compiled cancelled path re-enters the pend edge without running any arm block. Gate correctness verified by direct read: `winnerBB` is the only path in `emit_crossing_select.go` reaching the arm dispatch (cancelled goes to the pend edge, every error status panics), and the arm-drop union is arm-count-general, so the caller reclaims ALL losers for any N. THE RETRY RE-EVALUATION IS ALSO REAL, but far narrower than this row stated, and it took an adversarial review to find the shape that proves it. Three first probes — (1) a send arm whose Copy value comes from a marker-printing function; (2) the same through a select with NO ready arm, so it genuinely parks owner-side and resumes through a real retry round; (3) a lease-minting arm RECEIVER (`b.share().recv()`) — ALL printed one marker and measured byte-identical `definitely lost` with and without an `initBB`/`retryBB` split, so the split was briefly (and wrongly) reverted as unfalsifiable. The probe set had a hole: those are all PLACE operands, which `splitAsyncAwaits` (`internal/mir/async_normalize.go`) temps into a preceding block, so a resumed retry only re-loads them. A CONST operand is embedded in the crossing instruction itself and lowers INLINE inside the crossing block — and several const kinds allocate there: an `int`/`uint`/`float` literal outside the fixnum inline range (`rt_bigint_from_literal` and friends, `emit_term.go`) and a string const (`rt_string_from_bytes`). Measured A/B against HEAD on a bignum-literal send arm: `72 bytes in 2 blocks` without the split versus `36 bytes in 1 block` with it, leak stack through `rt_bigint_from_literal` — exactly one orphaned bigint per retry round. The split is therefore KEPT, with the accurate mechanism recorded in its code comment so the next reader does not re-derive the place-operand probe and revert it again. Boundary condition for anyone touching that row: the literal MUST exceed the fixnum inline range — re-measured with a small literal the leak is 0 both ways, so an obvious-looking `send(7)` arm proves nothing. `emitChannelCreateCrossing`'s split is the same category, conditional on operand form here rather than unconditional there. PROOF ROWS: `TestRuntimeV2FarSelectConstArmEvaluatedOnce` pins the split (one un-reclaimed bigint, not two; its single remaining block is the separate pre-existing `typeOwnsHeap`-false-for-bignum gap, stated in the row and not fixed here). `TestRuntimeV2FarSelectNonCopySendArm` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_far_select_noncopy_e2e_test.go`) — three far channels, one winning SEND arm buffered then reclaimed at channel teardown, one losing SEND arm reclaimed by the winning arm's compiled drop, exit-code rows at `SURGE_SHARDS=1,2,8` plus a valgrind row at STRICT ZERO definitely-lost; reverting the runtime fix reproduces the double free (negative control). Deliberately avoids `on ch {...}` so RV2-DEBT-061's intermittent race cannot be mistaken for this row's subject. HARNESS NOTE recorded during this work: the exit-code rows are NOT the real gate — glibc's tcache double-free detector is probabilistic, and the pre-fix binary passed them outright on some runs while valgrind reported the invalid free on that same binary. TWO ADJACENT FINDS, both fixed here: (a) the LLVM collect pass never collected `Crossing.RemoteOps[k].Value` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_collect.go`), though the local `InstrSelect` case collects `arm.Value` right beside it — so a far-select SEND arm carrying any non-trivial constant failed to COMPILE (`missing string const`); (b) `runtime-v2-transport-check` was RED at HEAD (`db8e10a4`): that commit added `rt_far_channel_select`'s `send_drop_fn_ids` parameter without updating the C harness fixture in `internal/vm/runtime_v2_remote_publication_test.go`, so five rows failed to build — a broken gate hiding regressions, repaired by passing `NULL` for the new parameter. | Closed (double-ownership gate + const-operand retry split) | Epic 21 Task 8 select fork | A non-Copy far select reclaims every SEND arm payload exactly once — winner delivered, losers dropped once — proven valgrind-clean at strict zero, with the fix's negative control reproducing the double free; the existing Copy-payload select e2e/census rows rerun unchanged as regression guards. |
| RV2-DEBT-065 | SOUNDNESS + diagnostic hole, pre-existing, found 2026-07-23 by the adversarial review of RV2-DEBT-064 and CONFIRMED independently by direct e2e probe. A select (or compare) arm that is CLOSED — one that exits via `return` rather than producing a value — never contributes its moves to the union moved-set: the merge loop skips `armClosed[i]` entries (`internal/sema/type_expr_select.go` ~88-96; the same skip exists on the compare path, `type_expr_flow.go` ~99-108). A binding moved into a closed arm therefore stays LIVE past the join. Probe (local `Channel<string>`, `ch.send(own u) => { return 5; }` as the closed arm, recv arm wins): the program COMPILES with no diagnostic and `print(u)` after the select prints the moved payload's contents — where the identical select with an OPEN send arm correctly errors SEM3130 use-of-moved-value. Two consequences: the use-after-move diagnostic is silently absent for this shape, and the review reports the payload is additionally freed by the losing-arm reclamation while compiled code still holds the binding (double free + UAF); the diagnostic hole is confirmed here, the memory-safety half is the review's finding and was not independently reproduced in the probe run (which printed correct contents and exited 0). CLOSED 2026-07-23, with the severity CORRECTED DOWN and independently corroborated. `armClosed` plays two roles and only one was wrong. Skipping a closed arm in the MERGE is correct for ordinary flow-sensitivity — control never reaches the join through it, so its BODY-local moves must not poison the post-join state. But an arm's AWAIT is evaluated before the select runs, so a move there is unconditional: a `send(own x)` payload is handed to `rt_select_poll` (local) or shipped in the far arm table before any winner exists, exactly as `checkSelectSendPayloadOwnership`'s own diagnostic text promises ("if this arm loses, the value is reclaimed in the other arms and cannot be used after the select"). FIX: `typeSelectExpr` snapshots each arm's moved-set once more between typing the arm's await and typing its body (`movedAwait[i]`), and the merge unions that await-half back in for CLOSED arms while still ignoring their body-local moves; nothing merges when every arm is closed, since the join is then unreachable. The position IS the invariant, which is why no per-call-site bookkeeping is needed. LOAD-BEARING NEIGHBOUR, now commented in the code: the drop-synthesis loop must keep skipping closed arms — a closed arm's own abrupt exit already runs `recordEarlyExitDrops` against its moved-set, so also giving it an `ArmDrops` entry would drop the other arms' payloads twice. The merge deliberately reads closed arms (await half only) while the drop loop deliberately does not. MECHANISM CORRECTION (found by adversarial review, verified here): `break` does NOT close an arm at all — `compareArmAbruptExit` -> `blockAbruptStatus` -> `flowStatus` in abrupt-exit mode treats only `return`, `panic(...)`, `exit(...)` and an all-abrupt `compare` as closed, and `StmtBreak`/`StmtContinue` fall through to `returnOpen`. So a `break` arm is an OPEN arm, is merged, and `rejectLoopBackEdgeMoves` already saw its move — which is why the loop shape appeared "already defended" during the first investigation. The `return`-closed loop variant was NOT defended: a payload bound outside a loop and moved by a `return`-closed send arm inside it COMPILED before this fix and is correctly refused after (A/B verified with a pre-fix compiler). That back-edge bypass is a second, real diagnostic hole this fix closes. NEGATIVE CONTROL: new golden fixture `testdata/golden/sema/invalid/select_send_closed_arm_use_after_join` — SEM3130 with the fix, an EMPTY regenerated `.diag` with the fix stashed. SEVERITY: the originating review called this double free + UAF. NOT CONFIRMED, and now corroborated as leak-or-nothing by an independent pass that BUILT AND RAN thirteen shapes under valgrind (local and far; closed arm winning and losing; closed recv arm winning while an open send arm loses — the RV2-DEBT-064 disown case; two closed arms plus one open; a `default` arm winning; cancel-while-pending; the loop variant) and found no invalid free, no invalid read/write, and no differential leak anywhere: the reclaim merely relocated between an `ArmDrops` entry and a scope-exit or early-exit drop, still exactly once. The mechanism that makes it safe is that `armClosed` implies control leaves the FUNCTION, so a winning closed arm's `return` runs `recordEarlyExitDrops` against a moved-set where the payload IS moved. This is therefore a contract-fidelity and diagnostic-consistency fix, NOT a memory-safety fix, and must not be described as closing a soundness hole. REGRESSION EVIDENCE: `internal/sema`, `internal/hir`, `internal/mir` green; the far-select, drop-select, remote-select and strict-census runtime rows green; `runtime-v2-crossing-check` and `runtime-v2-transport-check` green; a pre/post diagnostic sweep over all 694 `.sg` fixtures under `testdata/golden/sema` and `testdata/golden/crossing` differs on exactly one file — the new fixture. RESIDUAL, recorded not fixed: an `async { }` block hosting the select, where a `return` closes the arm but exits only the block while the payload binding lives in the enclosing function, is the one construct where "closed" would not mean "left the binding's scope". It cannot be built today — async blocks cannot capture channels (`mir: unknown local symbol` on the channel) — so the interaction is untestable; if that capture gap is ever closed, re-test this. | Closed (await-half moves survive a closed arm; body-local moves still don't) | Epic 21 follow-up | A binding moved into a closed select send arm is use-of-moved after the join, with a diagnostic negative control and a reclamation row proving exactly one owner. |
| RV2-DEBT-066 | Per-arm drop synthesis and early-exit drop synthesis can double up on the SAME binding: an arm containing a conditional `return` gets a `wrapArmDrops` drop at the arm head (`internal/hir/lower_expr.go` ~46-62) and a `recordEarlyExitDrops` drop on the return path (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go` ~224-252). Reported by the RV2-DEBT-064 adversarial review with a MIR reading (`bb2: drop L4; if ... bb8` / `bb8: drop L4`), described as not remote-select-specific and likely reaching compare arms too. NOT INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED: an e2e probe built for exactly this shape (local `Channel<string>` select, losing non-Copy send arm, recv arm containing `if c { return 5; }`) ran clean under valgrind — no invalid free, the payload reclaimed exactly once; the only definitely-lost blocks were the known local channel-object class. So either the MIR observation names two drops of different bindings, or the discriminating shape is narrower than the probe used. Needs a shape that actually fails before any fix. Matters because RV2-DEBT-064's fix makes compiled code the SOLE owner of losing arm payloads on the winner path, so a compiled-side double drop would no longer be masked by anything. RESOLVED 2026-07-23: the MIR reading was RIGHT and the conclusion drawn from it was WRONG — there is a genuine duplicate, and it is harmless by construction. Reproduced exactly: for `select { ch.send(own s) => 1; rx.recv() => { if c { return 5; } ret 2; }; }` the emitted `run$poll` has `bb2: drop L9; if copy L0 then bb8 else bb9` and `bb8: drop L9; goto bb26`, where `L9` is `s`, the losing send arm's payload — two drops of the same local on ONE taken path (the arm-head drop from `wrapArmDrops`, then the early-exit drop from `recordEarlyExitDrops`). It does not double free because `emitInstrDrop` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_instr.go`) ends every drop with `store ptr null` into the dropped slot, and each drop entry point tolerates NULL (`rt_string_free` returns immediately on NULL, `rt_far_channel_handle_drop` likewise). The second drop therefore loads NULL and no-ops. Measured: the program runs valgrind-clean on the doubled path — no invalid free, no invalid read/write, the only definitely-lost blocks being the known local channel-object class. (An earlier e2e probe of this shape also ran clean; that was correct, not a missed reproduction.) CLOSED as not-a-defect, with one thing worth stating loudly because it is now load-bearing: drop idempotence in this compiler rests ENTIRELY on that null-after-drop store plus null tolerance in every drop entry point. Any future drop path that frees without nulling its slot, or any drop callee that stops checking NULL, turns every redundant drop of this kind into a real double free. The redundancy itself is a MIR quality wart (a dead instruction on that path), not a correctness bug, and is not worth fixing on its own. | Closed (real duplicate, harmless: drop nulls its slot and every drop callee tolerates NULL) | Resolved during Epic 21 follow-up triage | Either a failing shape is produced and the overlap fixed with a negative control, or the overlap is shown impossible and the review's MIR reading explained. |
| RV2-DEBT-067 | `oneSidedDroppables` (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go` ~327-342) checks `isDroppableBinding` and `bindingDeclaredAtOrAbove` but NOT `isAliasedBinding`, so a binding that merely ALIASES container-owned state (its value came from a field/element/deref read — the case `markAliasedBinding` exists for) can receive a synthesized arm drop and free memory its container still owns. Reported by the RV2-DEBT-064 adversarial review from code reading; NOT reproduced with a failing program here. CLOSED 2026-07-23, and it was NOT speculative — REPRODUCED as real memory corruption. Shape: `type Holder = { name: string }; let h = Holder { name: build(...) }; let x: string = h.name; select { ch.send(own x) => 1; rx.recv() => 2; }` with the send arm losing, then `print(h.name)`. Under valgrind: multiple `Invalid read` plus an `Invalid free` — the arm-drop synthesized into the winning recv arm frees the string the struct still owns, the later field read touches freed memory, and the container's own drop frees it again. So this is use-after-free plus double free, not a latent code smell. FIX: reject the payload at the ownership check rather than at the drop synthesis. `checkSelectSendPayloadOwnership` (`internal/sema/type_expr_select.go`) now refuses an aliased binding with SEM3141, immediately after the existing whole-binding/projection check — an alias is that same hazard one binding removed. Rejecting at the source is what the fix needs to be, because skipping the drop (the review's suggested `isAliasedBinding` guard inside `oneSidedDroppables`) would only fix the LOSING arm: on the winning arm the payload is genuinely delivered to the channel while the container still owns it, so the double free happens anyway. NEGATIVE CONTROL: new golden fixture `testdata/golden/sema/invalid/select_send_aliased_payload`. REGRESSION: `internal/sema`/`hir`/`mir` green, the far-select, drop-select, remote-select and strict-census runtime rows green, `runtime-v2-crossing-check` and `runtime-v2-transport-check` green. RESIDUAL, deliberately not fixed: `oneSidedDroppables` (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go`) still does not consult `isAliasedBinding`, unlike its sibling `liveDroppables` which skips aliases explicitly. With the source rejected, no select shape can reach it with an alias today, but the asymmetry remains and compare-arm drop synthesis shares that function — no reproducing shape was found there, so the guard was not added blind. | Closed (rejected at the ownership check, SEM3141; reproduced first) | Resolved during Epic 21 follow-up triage | An aliased binding cannot reach arm-drop synthesis, proven by a diagnostic row on `let x = s.field; ... send(own x)`, or the alias case is shown unreachable there. |
| RV2-DEBT-070 | `make golden-check` is NONDETERMINISTIC and therefore an unreliable gate: it runs `golden-update` and then diffs against git, and the SAME tree regenerates different content run to run. Measured 2026-07-23: three consecutive runs on one unchanged tree gave exit 0, 2, 0; the differing file was `testdata/golden/mir/compare_guard_await_release.mir` and the diff was pure identifier renumbering — `type#1514` vs `type#1510` and a rotation of drop-function ids (`const 4`/`5`/`6` permuted), never a change in which instructions or drops exist. The fixture contains no `select` and was unrelated to the change being tested, so the numbering is shared across fixtures rather than per-file, and the assignment order varies between runs. Consequence: a genuine golden regression is indistinguishable from a flake on a single run, and a real change to MIR output can be masked by a run that happens to match. Discovered while validating RV2-DEBT-065, where it first presented as a false regression. Distinct from the closed RV2-DEBT-045 (module-record dedupe for stdlib diagnostics); this is MIR type/drop-fn id assignment order. ROOT CAUSE LOCALISED 2026-07-23, and it is WORSE THAN A GATE BUG: the COMPILER ITSELF is nondeterministic. `scripts/golden_update.sh` is sequential and compiles each fixture in its OWN `surge` process, so nothing is shared across fixtures — the flake is inside a single invocation. Measured directly: `surge diag --format short --emit-mir testdata/golden/mir/compare_guard_await_release.sg` on an unchanged tree produced DIFFERING output in 7 of 20 runs, settling into exactly two stable variants; the differing block is the async state types (`__state_init`/`__payload_init`), shifted by exactly 4 TypeIDs, plus a permutation of drop-function ids. Stage bisection over the same file: `--emit-instantiations` 1 distinct output of 8, `--emit-hir` 1 of 8, `--emit-mono` 6 of 8. The mono report's own diff is pure LINE ORDER — the TypeID multiset is identical — so mono's printing iterates a map, while the id divergence itself arrives later. MECHANISM: `Module.Funcs` is a `map[FuncID]*Func` (`internal/mir/module.go`), Go randomises map iteration, and many passes range over it WHILE ALLOCATING identifiers — async lowering builds state types (`internal/mir/async_lowering_single.go`), and the LLVM backend assigns drop-function ids (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_collect.go`, `emit_globals.go`). Whichever function is lowered first gets the lower ids. PARTIAL FIX PROBED AND REVERTED: sorting the async-lowering iteration by `FuncID` reduced but did NOT eliminate the divergence (still 2 distinct outputs of 12), confirming multiple allocating iteration sites; a half-fix was not kept because it would mask the remainder without closing it. CONSEQUENCE beyond the gate: builds are not reproducible, so any artifact comparison — golden, MIR/IR diffing, bisecting a codegen regression — is unreliable, and a real regression is indistinguishable from a flake on a single run. MIR HALF FIXED 2026-07-23 (commit `7c75aa99`): `Module.SortedFuncIDs`, `MonoModule.SortedFuncKeys` and `MonoModule.SortedTypeKeys` were added and all 28 walks routed through them, with a source-shape gate (`TestCompilerWalksFuncsInDeterministicOrder`, `internal/mir`) keeping the direct form from returning — it exempts the `Sorted*` helpers themselves and `hir.Module`, whose `Funcs` is a slice, and its negative control is reintroducing one direct walk. Measured: the known-flaky fixture went from 3 distinct outputs in 20 runs to 20 identical; a sweep of all 884 golden fixtures under `--emit-mir` reports 0 nondeterministic at N=3 where it previously flagged one; `make golden-check` passes on repeated runs where it previously alternated exit 0 and 2 on an unchanged tree. Committed goldens needed NO regeneration — the sorted order matches what was already checked in. ROW STAYS OPEN for a narrower remainder: `--emit-mono` is still nondeterministic (7 distinct of 12 on the same fixture). That report's own collections are ordered now and its comparators sort by `OrigSym`, so the divergence has moved into those sort KEYS — symbol ids assigned during monomorphization are themselves order-dependent, a deeper root than the walks fixed here. Its type listing additionally carries DUPLICATE entries (8 rows for 4 distinct types, the duplicate groups swapping position), which is worth understanding on its own and may be the same root. No golden fixture compares `--emit-mono` today, so the remainder is a debug-dump defect rather than a gate one; the reproducible-build claim now holds for MIR and the LLVM backend. Note the reviewer's second sighting (a `make golden` that exited 0 having printed only ">> Building surge") is most likely their own killed run, not this defect — recorded so it is not double-counted as evidence. REMAINDER ROOT-CAUSED AND CLOSED 2026-07-30. The remainder was NOT what this row predicted: measurement refutes the "symbol ids assigned during monomorphization are themselves order-dependent" hypothesis. All 20 runs of `--emit-mono` on the flaky fixture are content-identical MODULO LINE ORDER — the printed `sym=` (InstanceSym) and `type#` values are stable, only the order of whole GROUPS moved, with order INSIDE each group fixed. That shape says the SORT KEY moved, not the ids being sorted: the mono dump orders by `OrigSym`, and `OrigSym` is a symbol id from the resolver, not one minted by monomorphization. Probing the symbol arena directly (id -> name for all 2849 symbols, repeated runs) showed the name multiset identical and a BLOCK of ~700 ids permuted, starting at id 721 — core's exports. MECHANISM: `injectCoreExports` (`internal/symbols/resolve_module_members.go`) walked `fr.moduleExports` and `exports.Symbols` — BOTH maps — calling `syntheticSymbolForExport`, which allocates from a bump arena, so every core symbol's id depended on Go's randomised map order; the same defect sat in `declareImportAll` (`resolve_imports.go`) for `import *`. Note `exportsPrelude`, ten lines below the offender in the same file, ALREADY sorted both collections — the fix is that walk's shape, now shared as `ModuleExports.SortedNames` and `sortedCoreModulePaths`. This is the same CLASS as the 2026-07-23 half fix but a different LAYER: that one ordered walks over `Module.Funcs` in mir/mono/backend, this one orders symbol declaration in the resolver, which sits upstream of everything. GATE: `TestSameSourceGetsSameSymbolIDs` (`internal/driver`) compiles one source TWICE IN ONE PROCESS and compares symbol ids — Go re-randomises each execution of a `range`, so one process suffices. It is a BEHAVIOURAL gate, unlike the source-shape `TestCompilerWalksFuncsInDeterministicOrder`, which is why it catches this class wherever it appears rather than at one known syntax; its negative control is reverting either sort, which fails it at exactly id 721. Measured after the fix: `--emit-mono` on the flaky fixture 30/30 identical (was 11 distinct of 20); a sweep of all 888 golden fixtures at N=3 reports 0 nondeterministic under BOTH `--emit-mono` and `--emit-mir`; the symbol arena is byte-identical across runs. Committed goldens needed NO regeneration — the sorted order again matches what was already checked in. The duplicate type rows in the mono dump (8 rows for 4 types) are now STABLE but still duplicated, and are a distinct defect tracked as RV2-DEBT-092. | Closed | Golden gate determinism | The same tree regenerates byte-identical goldens across repeated runs, proven by a repeat-run check, or the id numbering is made per-fixture so cross-fixture order cannot leak. |
| RV2-DEBT-077 | Reading a MOVE-ONLY composite out of a container yields an ALIAS, not a value, and the container stays live beside it. Repro: `type Inner = { x: int }; type Outer = { inner: Inner, label: int };` then `let o = Outer {...}; let mut e = o.inner; e.x = 99;` prints `o=99 e=99` — writing through the extracted binding is visible through the container. Identical on BOTH backends, so it is not a differential; measured 2026-07-27 while landing Epic 23 step 4, and PRE-EXISTING (the step touched only the Copy path, and the repro behaves the same with that change reverted). Distinct from the Copy case, which step 4 fixed: a Copy composite extraction now lowers to `OperandCopyValue` and duplicates, while this path lowers to `OperandMove` of a temp that already aliased the container, so no operand kind is wrong — the question is a LANGUAGE one the epic does not own. Moving a field out of a live struct is a partial move, and sema does not track partially-moved bindings, so it neither rejects the program nor invalidates `o`. Two defensible answers, and picking one is the work: reject the partial move until it is tracked (Rust's model marks the source partially moved and forbids whole-value use afterwards), or accept it and make the extraction a real transfer that invalidates the source field. This is why contract row 9 (extraction independence) is currently pinned for the Copy case only. Epic 23's inline representation does NOT close it by itself: an inline extraction copies the bits, which changes the symptom from a shared box to a silent bitwise duplicate of a move-only value — arguably worse, since it manufactures a second owner of any heap the fields hold. Decide the semantics before Phase 2 makes the copy free. CLOSED 2026-07-29 by Epic 24 step 8. The answer is the second of the two the row names: `own o.field` TRANSFERS, and the source place is invalidated. The extraction stops counting a second holder (`FieldAccessData.MoveOut`, sema -> HIR -> MIR, printed `field_move`), the receiving binding is no longer marked an alias, and the container drops only the places it still holds. Proven by `TestRuntimeV2PartialMoveReclaimsOnlyWhatItStillHolds` (valgrind, native, strict zero over six shapes). | Closed (a field read that takes ownership is a real transfer) |  A move-only composite field read out of a container either is rejected as an untracked partial move, or transfers and invalidates the source; contract row 9 covers the move-only shape as well as the Copy one. |
| RV2-DEBT-078 | A `compare` over a value-composite scrutinee LEAKS its duplicate: measured 2026-07-28 while freezing Epic 23's contract, 3 boxes per evaluation for `compare *h { Hold(p) => p; ... }` where `h: &Held` and `Held` is a `@copy` union of a `@copy` struct. Attributed by differential: a WILDCARD arm still loses 16 direct + 16 indirect blocks over 16 evaluations — that is the compare's own copy of the union, envelope plus the payload it owns — and a BINDING arm adds 16 more direct blocks, the binding's separate copy of the payload. A union with a scalar payload loses the envelope alone. So the two leaks are independent: the scrutinee copy leaks always, the payload-binding copy leaks only when the payload is itself a composite. Found because the freeze audited what each contract row is actually PINNED by, and rows 3, 4, 5 and 7 claimed reclamation properties that no census measured — the contract test runs without valgrind. Rows 3, 5 and 7 were leaking too and are fixed (see below); this is the shape that is not. MECHANISM, read off the MIR: the compare's synthetic `let __cmpN = v` now consumes a value composite, so `__cmpN` holds an independent CLONE rather than the alias it held when a composite read was a pointer copy. Two premises of `compareScrutineeReleaseSafe` are therefore stale for this shape — "the scrutinee reads through a borrow so we own nothing" and "the scrutinee is Copy so the original stays usable" — and both refuse the release that the clone now needs. Worse, the arm's payload binding is ALSO a clone (`L5 = copy_value L6`), while `tagPayloadReleaseShape` still classifies an all-bound arm as safe for a SHALLOW envelope release on the premise that the payload moved out to the binding; it did not, so the envelope's own payload box is abandoned as well. FIX ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED IN THE SAME SESSION: making the release predicate answer true for any value composite fixed part of the leak and broke two gates at once — `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` crashed and the union-payload independence row failed — because it releases in cases the compare still does not own. The correct fix has to move shallow-vs-deep with the binding's behavior (a cloning binding leaves the payload in the envelope, so the release must be DEEP), which is the same delicate area as RV2-DEBT-052 and RV2-DEBT-075 and wants its own change rather than a tail-end patch. Not a correctness hazard beyond the leak: no invalid read or free at any point, on either backend. CLOSED 2026-07-30. Re-measured first, and this row's own numbers had gone stale: the BINDING-arm half was already fixed by RV2-DEBT-087, which landed after the row was filed. What remained was ONE leak, not two — the compare's own clone of the scrutinee, leaking on EVERY arm shape (over 16 evaluations: 16 direct blocks for the envelope, plus 16 indirect when the payload is itself a composite; a scalar payload loses the envelope alone). The `@copy` attribute is what selects the shape: a plain move-only union read through the same deref does not leak. FIX: both predicates were reading the TYPE, and what decides ownership is what the consuming READ did. MIR settles that at the use site (`placeOperand`): non-Copy MOVES, a Copy value composite DUPLICATES into an independent clone, and both leave the compare owning something. So the decision is now a named three-way classification — `scrutineeBorrowed` / `scrutineeMoved` / `scrutineeDuplicated` (`compareScrutineeOwnership`) — and both halves hang off it: a duplicated scrutinee may be released even though it is Copy and even through a deref (freeing a clone cannot disturb the original), and its arms must release DEEP, because the arm's binding clones AGAIN rather than taking the payload out, so a shallow envelope free would abandon the payload box. `scrutineeReadsThroughBorrow` stops being an unconditional veto and disqualifies only the MOVING read, which is the genuine move-out-of-a-borrow. INDEPENDENT REVIEW confirmed both halves and supplied a stronger argument for the second than the one used: a valid `@copy` union CANNOT contain a move-only member (recursively enforced, `internal/sema/attr_validation_types.go`), so no arm can have taken anything out of the clone and a deep drop cannot double-free. It also noted that `scrutineeDuplicated` is not literally true of every rvalue — MIR may MOVE an already-owned temporary — so the name describes the required reclamation strategy rather than the actual transfer; safe either way, since both mean the compare owns it. Measured: all three shapes go to strict zero with 0 memcheck errors, and the all-bound and whole-value-binding arms stay at zero. PINNED, not just measured: two rows joined the reclamation census (`composite-bound`, `composite-wildcard`), whose negative control is reverting this fix — it then exits 91 on the `composite-bound` differential. | Closed | Compare scrutinee release (HIR normalize) | `compare` over a borrowed or Copy value-composite scrutinee reports strict-zero definitely-lost; contract row 4 joins the reclamation census; `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` and the union-payload independence row stay green. |
| RV2-DEBT-079 | Ownership of an OWNED crossing capture across the transport edge is not settled, and the two sides disagree in a way neither side's comments admit. Discovered 2026-07-28 while specifying Epic 24 step 0 (the generated transfer protocol). `rewriteSpawnOnPollReturns` (`internal/mir/lower_expr_crossing_spawn_poll.go`) synthesizes a drop for COPY captures only, stating that an owned one may be CONSUMED by the body and a synthesized drop cannot see that; the body's unpacked local carries no scope-exit obligation (sema knows the symbol as a binding of the ENCLOSING function); and the envelope release is shallow by construction. By that reading an owned capture the body reads but never hands on is abandoned. MEASURED: it is NOT. A `@shard_movable` struct with a 200-character string field, moved into `spawn on distributed { ret j.id * 100 + 6; }` (no allocation inside the body, so nothing else can be attributed), reports `definitely lost: 0` over 8 crossings on the native backend, both before and after the Epic 24 step-0 fix. So SOMETHING reclaims it, and it is not any of the three sites above. The converse is proven too: registering the capture as an obligation of the crossing body — the obvious fix, and the one an adversarial review recommended — turns the program into an INVALID READ plus an INVALID FREE inside the body's drop glue, so the body is demonstrably not the only holder.
