FIXED 2026-08-12 by a mark the alias set could not carry: `markNonOwningBinding` (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go`), set at the loop's binding site and NEVER cleared, because `recordReassignOldDrop` clears the alias mark on the way past to say an ordinary binding owns again after assignment - which is right for an ordinary binding and wrong for a loop binding, so a second assignment in the same body would have freed the container's value even with the alias mark. Both `recordReassignOldDrop` and `recordPlaceOldDrop` consult it. EVIDENCE: both spellings exit 0 on both lanes; valgrind on the whole-binding spelling goes from `Invalid free(), 7 errors from 3 contexts` to one leak record and no invalid free; `TestRuntimeV2LoopBindingOverwriteIsNotAnInvalidFree` and two sema rows were each shown to FAIL against the pristine `ab55197c` tree. | Closed 2026-08-12 | Epic 23b Wave D step D1 | Freeing through a loop binding is impossible, both spellings are memory-safe on both lanes, and the gate fails against the unfixed tree. The residual leak is RV2-DEBT-212 and is deliberately separate. |

| RV2-DEBT-223 | `TestGoldenScriptSignalsPreserveLiveCorpus/HUP` IS LOAD-SENSITIVE, AND IT SITS INSIDE THE PRE-COMMIT HOOK. MEASURED 2026-08-19 at `149706b5` while taking a full gate baseline: in `make test` the test failed with `script_signal_test.go:75: script did not stop after HUP` after 5.14s; run alone immediately afterwards with `go test ./internal/goldencheck -run TestGoldenScriptSignalsPreserveLiveCorpus -count=1 -v` it PASSED in 0.14s with all three subtests (INT, TERM, HUP) shown executing at 0.05s each - so it is not a vacuous pass, the assertions really ran. The 5.05s figure is the subtest's own wait bound, so under load the signalled script simply had not been scheduled to its handler before the bound expired. THIS IS THE SECOND KNOWN LOAD-SENSITIVE MEMBER OF A MANDATORY LANE AND THE FIRST ONE INSIDE `make test`: RV2-DEBT-213 records `runtime-v2-http-owner-check` failing in a serial gate suite and passing alone, but that target is not in `make check`, whereas `make test` IS the pre-commit hook - so this one can refuse a correct commit outright, exactly as RV2-DEBT-220 did before its timeout was raised. It is a DIFFERENT mechanism from 220: 220 was a per-package clock that measured nothing about the code, this is a signal-delivery wait inside one test. The two facts that make it a row rather than a flake note: the failure names a test unrelated to whatever is being committed, and a reader who sees it red has no way to tell it from a real regression without re-running it alone. **FIXED 2026-08-20, AND THE ROW UNDERSTATED THE DEFECT.** The assertion was VACUOUS: it checked only that the script stopped, which is true whether the script TRAPS the signal or merely dies by it. The fixer proved that by deleting the HUP trap and watching the old test stay green. What only the trap achieves is a CONTROLLED exit - a shell killed by a signal reports no exit status, so the cleanup trap observes `$? == 0` and skips the rollback that puts the backup back over a half-installed corpus. The test now asserts the exit code the script's own trap installs (130 INT / 143 TERM / 129 HUP), which is what makes it able to fail at all. On the load-sensitivity itself: there is NO earlier event to wait on, because the script answers a trapped signal by running `exit 128+n` - the handler running IS the process exiting. So the wait can only be a wall clock, and it is now a deadlock GUARD rather than an assertion: 60s, chosen against measurements taken under load average 147 where start-to-marker peaked at 326ms over 496 runs and kill-to-reap at 158ms over 1339 deliveries. That is 184x and 380x margin against the 15x and 32x the old 5s bound carried, and all three subtests can each burn the full guard and still finish inside the package timeout, so a genuine hang is still reported as this test failing rather than as the package dying. VERIFIED BY THE LEAD, not taken from the fixer: three subtests green, and the HUP subtest shown FAILING with the script's `trap 'exit 129' HUP` removed - `script answered HUP with exit status -1, want 129`. **CLOSED 2026-08-21 at `03cba4d8`, AND THE CLOSING EVIDENCE ITSELF HAD A FALSE STATEMENT IN IT UNTIL NOW.** Three things were done, in the order that matters. (1) THE MARGIN IS RE-MEASURED INDEPENDENTLY, which is what this condition literally asked for and what nobody had redone: 32 spinners plus a full tagged `internal/vm` run alongside, load average 20.6, **40 consecutive runs, 120 subtest executions, zero failures, slowest subtest 0.19s end to end**. The fixer's original figures (326ms / 158ms at load 147) are now corroborated by a second measurement on a different load rather than merely re-endorsed in prose -- three endorsements are not three measurements. (2) THE BUDGET ARITHMETIC IN THE COMMENT WAS WRONG AND IS FIXED. It claimed all three subtests could each burn the whole guard and still finish inside the 300s package timeout; each subtest carries TWO bounds (`waitForMarker`, then the post-kill guard), so at 60s the worst case was 3 x 2 x 60 = 360s against `Makefile:93`'s 300s -- a hang would have killed the package BEFORE this test could name itself, the exact opposite of what the bound exists for. The guard is now 45s, worst case 270s, property true; margin over the slowest measured subtest is 236x. (3) THE HANDLER-REACHED ALTERNATIVE IS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, and the reason is on the record: the HUP handler's whole body is `exit 129` (`scripts/golden_update.sh:110`), so the handler running IS the process exiting and any sync point inside the trap fires microseconds before the `wait4` that `cmd.Wait()` already blocks on. Building one would mean instrumenting the three lines under test with test-only apparatus -- and the mutation proof deletes exactly those lines. Rule 13 re-verified today on a `git archive` copy: HUP fails with `script answered HUP with exit status -1, want 129`, INT and TERM stay green, so the failure is isolated to the handling removed. The 'until then' operating instruction is retired with this closure. | Closed 2026-08-21 | Epic 23b gate integrity | Closed; see the note. |
| RV2-DEBT-224 | `make runtime-v2-file-size-check` IS RED WITH ELEVEN VIOLATIONS, AND NOBODY COULD SEE IT BECAUSE ITS REQUIRED INPUT WAS NEVER WRITTEN DOWN. The target dies immediately with `EPIC_BASE is required` (`scripts/runtime_v2_file_size_check.sh:131`), and the two places that tell a reader how to run it both contain a PLACEHOLDER rather than a value: `23b-wave-d-execution-plan.md:237` and `23b-inline-storage-and-typed-carriers.md:721,754` all say `EPIC_BASE=<recorded-base>`. So a mandatory closeout gate has been un-runnable for the life of the epic, and every invocation of it failed for the wrong reason - which is RV2-DEBT-200's shape one layer up: not a gate that reports success while absent, but a gate that reports failure while never having measured anything. THE BASE WAS RECOVERED 2026-08-19 from the carrier census header, `internal/carriergate/testdata/legacy_carriers.json` -> `base_commit = 7df10725e001ddf915d536aa58f880bd7e04aafd`, which is independently the commit that ADDED the epic document (`7df10725 Epic 23b typed-carrier design ready snapshot`); two independent sources agreeing is why this is recorded as recovered rather than guessed. RUN WITH IT, the gate reports `FAIL files=692 violations=11`: `NEW_OVER_500` on `internal/abimanifest/manifest_test.go` (527); `LEGACY_GROWTH` on `cmd/surge/diagnose.go` (561->565), `internal/backend/llvm/emit_instr_binary.go` (528->548), `internal/backend/llvm/emit_intrinsics_runtime.go` (597->599), `internal/mir/lower.go` (581->583), `internal/mir/validate.go` (543->555), `internal/sema/magic_names.go` (670->672), `internal/vm/mt_correctness_test.go` (1249->1258), `runtime/native/rt_async_internal.h` (676->681), `runtime/native/rt_fs.c` (929->954), `runtime/native/rt_string.c` (745->748). None is diagnosed; the row exists so they stop being invisible. NOTE FOR ANY ARRAY WORK: the fourth rule `REWRITE_OVER_500` (script line 232) fires when `2 * effective_churn >= effective_base && effective_head > 500`, and `internal/vm/intrinsic_array.go` stands at effective 544 against a base of 581 with churn 65 - so a rewrite of its element storage must finish BELOW 500, which makes splitting that file an obligation rather than a preference. | Open | Epic 23b gate integrity | `EPIC_BASE=7df10725e001ddf915d536aa58f880bd7e04aafd` is recorded in the epic document and the Wave D plan in place of the placeholder, so the gate is runnable from its own instructions; and the eleven violations are each either fixed or given a dated owner ruling. The gate is shown able to PASS at least once, because a gate that has only ever been observed failing has not been shown to measure the right thing either. **PROGRESS 2026-08-21: THE COUNT WENT 11 TO 14 TO 12, AND THE MOVEMENT IS THE POINT.** Re-measured with the recovered base, the gate found FOURTEEN, not the eleven recorded -- three had appeared since, and two of those three were MINE, from the alignment fix landing in `emit_access.go` and pushing it 450 -> 501. A row that had only ever been read once was hiding new arrivals behind its own stale list, which is the same shape as the gate never having run. Five files are now split along a seam they already had, each move verbatim and none of them a rewrite: `manifest_test.go` 575 -> 416 (the instruments apart from the questions), `magic_names.go` 712 -> 660 (operator spelling is not a typing rule), `validate.go` 662 -> 547 (three checks that ask about the END of a life), `emit_instr_binary.go` 566 -> 387 (bignum arithmetic is calls and owned results), `emit_intrinsics_runtime.go` 625 -> 482 (the five intrinsics that move raw bytes share an obligation the rest do not), plus `emit_access.go` 501 -> 432 undoing my own. **TWELVE REMAIN**, all LEGACY_GROWTH except one NEW_OVER_500, and for a legacy row the bar is to give back the growth rather than to reach 500: `rt_fs.c` +25, `runtime_v2_remote_publication_test.go` +18, `emit_instr_binary.go` +20, `mt_correctness_test.go` +9, `rt_async_internal.h` +5, `diagnose.go` +4, `rt_string.c` +3, `magic_names.go` +2, `lower.go` +2, `emit_intrinsics_runtime.go` +2, `validate.go` +12, `manifest_test.go` at 527. Note for whoever takes them: `cmd/surge/diagnose.go` is +4 but `runDiagnose` is ONE 580-line function, so it is a real refactor rather than a trim, and the two big test files each end in a multi-hundred-line C harness held in a string constant -- that harness is the seam. **CLOSED 2026-08-21: `runtime-v2-file-size-check: PASS files=769 violations=0`**, and the row's own bar -- that the gate be shown able to PASS at least once, not merely observed failing -- is met by that line. The count went 11 (as recorded) to 14 (as actually measured) to 0. Sixteen files were split and every cut follows a seam the file already had, never a line count: test instruments apart from test questions, operator spelling apart from typing rules, end-of-life checks apart from structural ones, bignum arithmetic apart from machine-word arithmetic, byte-moving intrinsics apart from the rest, FsResult construction apart from syscalls, number/text conversion apart from text manipulation, the C stand apart from the tests that drive it, trace declarations apart from the scheduling contract. **TWO THINGS LEARNED THE HARD WAY.** First, extraction can make the count WORSE: lifting the 1659-line C stand into one new file traded a LEGACY_GROWTH for a NEW_OVER_500 plus a REWRITE_OVER_500, so the stand was re-split by MODE FAMILY (prologue, spawn, immediate/anchored, far select) rather than by size. Second, a C header cannot always be split: `rt_net_poll_wake`, `rt_task` and `waker_key` are typedefs of ANONYMOUS structs and cannot be forward declared, so two declarations stayed put rather than have the runtime's types renamed to satisfy a size rule. Splitting rt_fs.c and rt_string.c did cost two new internal headers, which is the honest price of the same operation in C. Every touched C file passes `make c-check-changed`, and the fixtures that fail around them fail identically at the baseline commit. |
| RV2-DEBT-225 | THE VM REFUSED A `@packed` CONTAINER HOLDING A WIDE FIXED ARRAY - a program its own sema accepts. `@packed type P = { flag: bool, cells: int64[3], tail: bool }` panics `VM1999: storage: reference to type#9 at offset 1 is not 8-byte aligned` at CONSTRUCTION, while the native lane runs it and prints the right answer: measured 2026-08-19 at `149706b5` as `exit code mismatch: vm=1 llvm=0`, a LANE DISAGREEMENT. This is the Wave C `@packed` shape returning on the array path, and the codebase had already written down the rule the array path breaks - `internal/vm/storage_shape.go:214-221` says a member's alignment "is the CONTAINER's business, not the member type's" and that requiring the member type's alignment "rejects a program the language accepts". A struct field reaches that rule; a fixed array element does not, because `compositeMembers` returns into `arrayMembers` at `storage_shape.go:178`, BEFORE the block that applies it, and an array's layout carries no per-element alignments to correct it with - `arrayFixedLayout` returns `makePhysical(..., nil, nil, nil)` (`internal/layout/compute.go:88`). So each element arrived claiming `memberAt`'s `align = facts.Align` of the ELEMENT (`storage_shape.go:157-161`) while sitting at an absolute offset of `1 + i*8`, and `resolve` refused it (`internal/vm/storage.go:228-232`). WHY THE EXISTING FIXTURE MISSED IT: `storage_packed_field.sg` puts PACKED values in a fixed array inside an UNPACKED struct, so the element alignment is 1 and every offset divides it - the wrong answer and the right answer coincide. The new row varies exactly one condition: a WIDE element inside a PACKED container. **FIXED 2026-08-19.** The alignment an access may REQUIRE is now capped by what the base address guarantees, computed once in `memberAccessAlign` and applied at `memberRef` - the single site where any member reference is minted - rather than in one shape's member list. EVIDENCE: `TestRuntimeV2OrdinaryStorageParity/storage_packed_wide_array` fails on the unfixed tree with the exit-code mismatch above and passes after; the whole 18-row ordinary-storage corpus is green on both lanes. | Closed 2026-08-19 | Epic 23b Wave D step D1 | Both lanes run the fixture, agree, and print `packed wide array ok`, and the fixture is registered so `TestRuntimeV2OrdinaryStorageCorpusIsComplete` keeps it running. **VERIFIED CLOSED 2026-08-21.** Both lanes now run `@packed type P = { flag: bool, cells: int64[3], tail: bool }`, write `p.cells[1] = 55:int64`, read it back and print `packed wide array ok` with exit 0 -- the lane DISAGREEMENT this row is about is gone, checked by running the program on each lane rather than by reading a test name. The fixture is registered: `testdata/runtime-v2-ordinary-storage/layout/storage_packed_wide_array.sg` is listed in `runtime_v2_ordinary_storage_corpus_test.go:64` with its expected output, so `TestRuntimeV2OrdinaryStorageCorpusIsComplete` keeps it running and a silent removal would fail the corpus-completeness check. Note the ordering the row itself warned about: this fixture going green does NOT prove RV2-DEBT-226, which is why 226 carries its own emitted-IR assertion and was closed separately. |
| RV2-DEBT-226 | THE NATIVE LANE CLAIMS AN ALIGNMENT THE ADDRESS CANNOT HONOUR, on every fixed-array element access that is not reached through the place walk. Found 2026-08-19 while fixing RV2-DEBT-225, and it is why that row's parity fixture cannot close this one: after the VM stops refusing, BOTH lanes exit 0 and the fixture goes green while the defect stays. It is visible only in the emitted IR, and there it is unambiguous - for `p.cells[1] = 55:int64` inside a `@packed` container the emitter produces `%t63 = getelementptr inbounds i8, ptr %l0, i64 1`, then `%t73 = mul i64 %t69, 8`, `%t74 = getelementptr inbounds i8, ptr %t63, i64 %t73`, and finally `store i64 %t75, ptr %t74, align 8`. The address is congruent to 1 mod 8 for every index, so `align 8` is a promise LLVM is entitled to act on and the address never keeps; it is undefined behaviour that currently works because x86 tolerates unaligned access. THE MECHANISM IS A SPLIT BRAIN, NOT A TYPO: `emitPlaceStorage` genuinely threads the alignment and folds it per projection - `curAlign = memberAccessAlign(curAlign, off)` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_helpers_place.go:171`) - and its sibling `emitPlacePtr` is documented as "discarding what its address is aligned to" (`:10-11`). Every OTHER access site re-derives the element alignment from the element type instead, through `arrayElemStrideAlign`: `emit_access.go:103` and `:192`, `emit_intrinsics_array_element.go:82`, `emit_calls_intrinsics.go:322` and `:526`, `emit_magic_array.go:96`, `emit_iter.go:334`, `emit_literals.go:176` and `:254`, `emit_intrinsics_array.go:72`, plus the drop/clone walks at `emit_drop_glue.go:486`, `emit_drop_array.go:13`, `emit_clone_array.go:52`. Not all of those are reachable with a packed base - a DYNAMIC array's elements live in runtime-owned storage behind a handle, where nothing the container guarantees reaches them and re-deriving is CORRECT - so this row needs a census of which sites can see an inline fixed array, not a sweep. NOT FIXED; a spot fix was written and reverted because it could not be shown to reach the site that emitted the store above. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D step D1 | Each listed site is classified as inline-fixed-array-reachable or handle-backed, the reachable ones take the alignment from the address rather than the element type, and the proof is an EMITTED-IR assertion that the packed element access carries `align 1` - shown failing at this commit, because a behavioural fixture provably cannot see this. **CLOSED 2026-08-20 (`bfec1711`), AND THE ROW'S OWN BAR IS THE ONE THAT WAS MET.** The fix is structural rather than per-site: `emitAlloca` now RETURNS the alignment it reserved, and `emitArrayFixedElemPtr` returns the element's alignment alongside its address, so the alignment travels WITH the address instead of being derived a second time from the element type downstream. That second derivation was the defect — an address is what has an alignment, a type is not. Proof is exactly what this row demanded, an EMITTED-IR assertion rather than a behavioural fixture: `TestPackedWideElementWriteStoresAtAlignOne` reads the IR and requires the packed wide element store to carry `align 1`, and `TestNoAccessClaimsMoreAlignmentThanItsAddressHas` checks the same property across a wide element read back, a run-time index, and a field of a struct element. Shown failing without the change and passing with it, in the same commit: FOUR failures to zero, measured by restoring the pre-fix sources under the new tests via `git archive` rather than by stashing. |

Found 2026-08-19 while designing step D3's native half, which needs an `rt_value_ops` descriptor per element type and therefore needs a drop obligation it can name. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-19: D3 emits descriptors from the BACKEND** using the layout registry and `typeOwnsHeap`, and this row records the accepted cost — until the registry route lands, "is this type droppable" has TWO sources of truth, the backend's predicate and the staged flag that cannot be bound. That is a divergence with a single witness of exactly the kind this project has been bitten by before, which is why it is a row rather than a footnote. **CLOSED 2026-08-21 (`c3bf1743`, `930054cd`).** `drop_in_place` moved from `filledNowhere` to `filledByBackendDerivedBody`, `Capabilities.Droppable` was deleted so the classifier is the single source of the verdict, and the backend names the body from the type id the way it already names glue. Measured on a probe: 10 descriptors emitted, 3 carrying a real drop body, and every name they point at defined in the same module. THE BIT MEANS WHAT THE MANIFEST SAYS -- present AND with work to do (owner ruling 2026-08-21) -- so a carrier whose leaf this backend cannot reclaim gets NO descriptor rather than a set bit over an empty body: that shape passes the runtime's preflight, runs, and leaks in silence. Two tests, both shown failing: `TestTheDroppableBitAgreesWithTheBackendThatHasToBackIt` (caught a disabled skip with 'Channel<int> diverges and yet is emittable', and fails on a stale divergence row as well as a new one) and `TestAnEmittedDescriptorCarriesARealDropBody` ('not one carries a drop body: the bit is staged again in all but name'). The one divergence of the opposite direction was FIXED rather than recorded: see the nominal-array edge in `internal/sema/capability_type_graph.go`. **AND THE ROW'S MOTIVATION IS SETTLED: D3 IS UNBLOCKED BY THIS.** The number the channel ring drains through is the ELEMENT's type id, not the channel's -- proven by reading the emitted call, `rt_channel_new(i64 %cap, i64 1511)` where 1511 is `Item`, whose descriptor already carries `ptr @drop.type1511`. Reclaiming the channel VALUE is RV2-DEBT-155 and was never this row's precondition. | Closed (2026-08-21) | Epic 23b, operations registry | `FlagDroppable` is un-staged, `Entry` grows the move/drop/plan slots, and the backend's descriptor emission reads the registry instead of computing the obligation a second time — with a test that FAILS if the two answers disagree for any type in the corpus. |
| RV2-DEBT-228 | THE CI SUITE HAS NOT RUN ON THIS BRANCH SINCE 2026-06-26, AND ELEVEN JOBS WENT WITH IT. MEASURED 2026-08-20 at `aee4d9e8` while taking the D3 gate baseline. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` triggers on `push` to `dev`/`main` and on `pull_request` INTO `dev`/`main` (`:3-9`). PR #164, which carried this branch, was CLOSED 2026-06-25, so every push since has matched no trigger. `gh run list` shows the last run for this ref at 2026-06-26 (#164, run 28244884991) and it was HONESTLY green - all eleven jobs executed, C runtime checks (clang) in 1m14s including `make ctidy` and `make cppcheck`. Between that run and this measurement the branch took **723 commits**, none of them seen by CI. **THE HOOK IS NOT THE HOLE AND MUST NOT BE BLAMED FOR IT.** `scripts/pre-commit` runs `make check` = `make test` + `make lint` (golangci-lint, whose enabled set INCLUDES staticcheck's SA analyses, govet with `enable-all`, and gosec) + `make c-check` (`cfmt-check` plus `c-warnings`, which compiles every C source under `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`) + `check_file_sizes.sh`. It does exactly what it claims. What CI adds on top of it, and what therefore has been unobserved for two months, is: `make ctidy`, `make cppcheck`, `scan-build`, the ASan+UBSan build and test run, `make runtime-v2-check`, `make golden-corpus-determinism`, `make behaviour-check`, `make behaviour-check-all`, `./check_file_sizes.sh -a`, and the release build. **THREE OF THEM ARE RED AS OF THIS MEASUREMENT AND NONE OF THE THREE IS A CORRECTNESS FAILURE.** `make staticcheck` exits 2 on ~32 findings, ALL of them ST1003 (`__id`/`__k` range variables, `SemaOnTcpRemoteIO`) or ST1000 (missing package comments) - the stylecheck family, which golangci-lint does not enable, so no SA analysis is among them. `make cppcheck` exits 1 on four style findings: three `knownConditionTrueFalse` in `rt_backtrace.c:90,160` (linker-provided symbols compared against NULL, from the CFI backtrace work) and one `constVariablePointer` at `rt_async_channel.c:90`. `make ctidy` exits 1 on ~97 warnings of which 87 are `bugprone-reserved-identifier` against `__surge_runtime_abi_typed_carrier_v2_<hash>` in the GENERATED ABI header - the identifier the ABI proof is required to carry - plus six `bugprone-sizeof-expression`, two `bugprone-casting-through-void`, and one `clang-analyzer-core.CallAndMessage` at `rt_net_listener_socket.c:102` that is a FALSE POSITIVE: `actual` is filled by `getsockname`, whose success the preceding line checks, and the analyzer does not model that. **TWO OF THE ELEVEN CANNOT BE RUN LOCALLY AT ALL AS OF THIS MEASUREMENT, WHICH IS WHY THE SCHEDULE MATTERED MORE THAN IT LOOKED.** `scan-build` is not installed on the development machine (exit 127; it ships in `clang-tools`), so CI was the ONLY place the clang static analyser has ever run over this repository - and it has not run since 2026-06-26. `make runtime-v2-carrier-bench` refuses fail-closed on a dirty worktree (`benchmark worktree is dirty`), and this tree is permanently dirty in normal use because agent tooling tracks `.claude/*` and two database files, so it cannot be measured in place at all: it needs `git archive` or a dedicated worktree. Neither of these is a red gate; both are UNMEASURED, and a baseline that files them as red would be claiming knowledge it does not have. Measured honestly in this baseline: the ASan+UBSan BUILD over all 94 C files passes in 18s, and the sanitizer TEST step - re-run WITHOUT the `\|\| true` ci.yml wraps it in - passes in 16.5s of real execution. **AND THEN THE SECOND COMMAND, MEASURED SEPARATELY, TURNED OUT TO BE UNABLE TO PASS EITHER - `\|\| true` was hiding a step that was broken from birth, not merely a step that could not fail.** `go test ./... -run TestRuntime` under the job's own environment dies with `undefined reference to __asan_report_load_n` in every package that links the C runtime (`cmd/surge`, `internal/carriergate`, `internal/vm`): cgo was compiled WITH the sanitizers and linked WITHOUT them, because the job sets `CGO_CFLAGS` and no `CGO_LDFLAGS`. Adding `CGO_LDFLAGS` moves the failure one layer on - `relocation target __ubsan_handle_type_mismatch_v1 not defined` - because Go's INTERNAL linker does not pull the UBSan runtime in, so the link must be external. With `CGO_LDFLAGS` plus `-ldflags='-linkmode=external -extldflags=-fsanitize=address,undefined'` the step builds and genuinely runs. FIXED 2026-08-20 in `ci.yml` together with removing both `\|\| true`s, per owner ruling. What it reports once it can report anything: **ZERO sanitizer findings**, and two failures that are already this ledger's - `TestRuntimeV2CompositeCopyIsIndependent/llvm`, which RV2-DEBT-213 records as a build failure, and an accept shard-compatibility test from the same family - plus a timeout, since sanitized runs need a budget several times the plain one (60s raised to 300s/600s). So the job is now capable of refusing, and what it currently refuses is known debt rather than anything new. **AND ONE OF THE ELEVEN JOBS COULD NOT HAVE FAILED EVEN WHILE IT RAN.** The `c-sanitizers` job's second step ends both of its commands in `\|\| true` (`ci.yml:151,153`), so the ASan+UBSan TEST run reports success unconditionally; only its build step (`\|\| exit 1`) can fail. `c-scan-build` is half the same shape - its per-file compiles are `\|\| true`, though `--status-bugs` still surfaces analyser findings. So the sanitizer evidence this branch has been missing for two months was already weaker than its green tick implied, and restoring the schedule alone does not restore the check. **`make sec` IS A SECOND SUCH TARGET, AND ITS REDNESS IS A SUPPRESSION-SYNTAX MISMATCH RATHER THAN 89 HOLES.** Measured in the same baseline: it ran for 6687s (1h51m) over 877 files and exits 2 on 89 findings, of which **86 are G115** (integer-overflow conversion, e.g. `uint64(i) * stride`) and one is G602; **68 of the 89 carry `//nolint:gosec` on the flagged line itself**. Bare gosec does not read golangci-lint's `//nolint` directive - it honours `#nosec` - so the suppressions this repo actually wrote are invisible to it, while the SAME analyser runs inside `make lint` (gosec is enabled in `.golangci.yaml`, G104 excluded) where those directives are honoured and the hook is green. Two hours of wall clock for a duplicate analysis whose only new output is its own inability to read the repo's suppressions is worth naming before anyone 'fixes' 89 findings. **`make staticcheck` IS THE ONE TARGET THAT NO GATE HAS EVER RUN** - it is absent from the hook and absent from ci.yml alike, where the Lint job invokes golangci-lint directly. This is RV2-DEBT-200's shape once more: a target that reads as a check and measures nothing, except that here it is the SCHEDULE rather than the target that went missing. NOT FIXED; recorded before any repair, so the repair can be shown to change something. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20: THE BRANCH STAYS OFF CI UNTIL THE REFACTOR IS DONE, AND THE REASON IS NOT INATTENTION.** Asked to choose between a push trigger, a draft PR into `dev`, and a dated ruling, the owner declined the first two on a specific ground: running the FULL suite now would fail in the places the refactor has not reached yet, and every red run would then pose the same question - skip the failing check or stop and fix it. A gate that asks that question on every push trains the answer 'skip', which is how a suite becomes decorative. So the suite returns when the refactor can satisfy it, and until then the local hook plus the serial gate baselines are what stands in for it. THE COST IS STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: eleven jobs stay unobserved, including `scan-build`, which is installed nowhere locally, so the clang static analyser is not running anywhere at all in the meantime. | Open | Epic 23b gate integrity | The branch is put back under CI when the refactor no longer fails it - the ruling above sets that as the exit condition rather than a date. Evidence is a run of the suite on a commit of this branch with each of the eleven jobs shown executing, not merely reported green, per [[gates-can-be-green-without-running]]. Separately, each of the three red targets is either fixed, suppressed at its source with the suppression naming the reason (the generated ABI identifier and the getsockname model are suppressions, not repairs), or given a dated ruling that keeps it red on purpose - and `make staticcheck` is either added to a gate or deleted from the Makefile, because a target no schedule runs is not a check. |
| RV2-DEBT-229 | FIVE NATIVE FILES CANNOT BE COMMITTED UNDER THE C-CHECK RULE THE HOOK NOW ENFORCES, AND TWO OF THEM ARE D3'S. Owner ruling 2026-08-20 made Global Rule 6 enforceable rather than declarative: a C edit must PASS `cppcheck` and `clang-tidy`, not merely be accompanied by a note that they were run. `scripts/pre-commit` now runs `make c-check-changed` over the staged `*.c`/`*.h`, narrowed to those files because the whole-tree targets carry the RV2-DEBT-228 backlog. MEASURED 2026-08-20 at `aee4d9e8` from the baseline's own `ctidy` and `cppcheck` logs, so this is a count of the tree rather than of a fresh run: **`rt_backtrace.c` (6 clang-tidy + 3 cppcheck), `rt_far_channel.c` (1), `rt_remote_task_lease.c` (1), `rt_net_listener_socket.c` (1), `rt_async_channel.c` (1 cppcheck)**. `rt_async_channel.c` is the core file D3 rewrites and `rt_far_channel.c` is its remote half, so the rule bites the very next step - which is the point of taking it before the step rather than during it. NOT ALL FIVE ARE DEFECTS AND THE DIFFERENCE DECIDES THE REPAIR: `rt_net_listener_socket.c:102` is a FALSE POSITIVE (`actual` is filled by `getsockname`, whose success the preceding line checks; the analyzer does not model it) and wants a suppression that NAMES that reason, not a code change. `rt_backtrace.c`'s three `knownConditionTrueFalse` are linker-provided section symbols compared against NULL - a deliberate defensive idiom from the CFI backtrace work, so the same applies. `rt_async_channel.c:90` (`constVariablePointer`), `rt_far_channel.c:380` (`bugprone-casting-through-void`) and `rt_remote_task_lease.c:184` (`bugprone-assignment-in-if-condition`) are ordinary edits. THE ENFORCEMENT ITSELF WAS PROVED BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED, per Global Rule 13: a clean file passes; an unused variable added to it is refused by the strict compile; `rt_async_channel.c` is refused by cppcheck alone; and `rt_far_channel.c`, `rt_remote_task_lease.c`, `rt_net_listener_socket.c` are each refused by clang-tidy alone. One negative control PASSED VACUOUSLY on the first attempt - a cast written through `unsigned long` rather than `uintptr_t`, which `bugprone-casting-through-void` does not match - and was replaced by these real findings rather than being reported as evidence. **CLOSED 2026-08-20, AND THE TWO WHOLE-TREE TARGETS WENT GREEN WITH IT.** All five files pass `make c-check-changed`, and only ONE of the nine findings needed suppressing on its own terms; the rest had causes that could be removed. `rt_async_channel.c`: the pointer only reads, so it is const. `rt_far_channel.c`: `handle_free` took a const handle and laundered the const away through pointer -> const void* -> uintptr_t - freeing is not a const operation, so free and drop (the only two functions that OWN the token) now take it mutable, matching what `handle_alloc` always returned. `rt_remote_task_lease.c`: the reset of `expected` between two CAS attempts moved out of a comma expression inside an `if` into a named `lease_state_transition` helper, which is also what the code meant. `rt_net_listener_socket.c`: `struct sockaddr_in actual` is now zero-initialised rather than suppressed. `rt_backtrace.c`: the cast through `const void*` was simply unnecessary and was deleted; what REMAINED is genuinely false and is suppressed with its reason in the code - the address of a WEAK symbol can be null, which is the entire point of weak, and `__start_SECTION`/`__stop_SECTION` are names the LINKER synthesises, so no rename exists. **`--inline-suppr` had to be added to both cppcheck invocations first**: without it there was no legal way to say 'this one is wrong', which would have made the new mandatory rule unsatisfiable wherever the tool is mistaken. **The generated ABI header's 87 findings are closed at the GENERATOR** (`internal/abimanifest/render_c.go`), not by widening a filter: the sentinel is deliberately in the reserved identifier space because a name a Surge program could also define would prove nothing about which runtime linked, and the `sizeof(((T*)0)->ops)` asserts compare two pointer sizes on purpose, to catch a field's type drifting. The manifest hash `f30fcfb0…` is unchanged, because `ContentHash` hashes the canonical MANIFEST rather than the rendered text. Both suppressions were shown to be narrow: a real `knownConditionTrueFalse` and a real reserved identifier introduced elsewhere in the same file are still caught and still refuse the file. RESULT: `make cppcheck` and `make ctidy` are GREEN whole-tree for the first time in this ledger's history. | Open | Epic 23b gate integrity, and D3 by consequence | Each of the five is closed as a code edit or as a SOURCE-LEVEL suppression whose comment states why the finding is wrong there; a blanket `-Wno`, a widened filter, or a removed check does not close a row. `rt_async_channel.c` and `rt_far_channel.c` must be clean before D3's commit can land at all, so they are D3's entry cost rather than a parallel task. Evidence is `make c-check-changed C_CHANGED="<file>"` exiting 0 for each of the five, and the suppressions shown still allowing a REAL finding of the same check through. |
| RV2-DEBT-230 | **WAVE B NEVER GENERATED ITS OPERATIONS, AND EVERY TYPED-OWNER MIGRATION HAS BEEN BLOCKED BEHIND THAT SINCE.** Found 2026-08-20 while opening Wave D step D3 and ruled by the owner the same day: the step was stopped before a line was written. Wave B items 3 and 5 read GENERATE - 'Generate concrete move/copy/clone/drop/trace/cross operations and `KeyOps`' and 'Generate read-only cross plans plus move/clone operations' (`23b-inline-storage-and-typed-carriers.md:188,190`) - and the wave closes with 'This wave is an integration dependency for all erased runtime owners. It must land before parallel owner migrations branch from it' (`:203-204`). P2's entry condition names it: 'P1 and the shared Wave B owner/slot API are integrated' (`:318`). **MEASURED: nothing generates.** `internal/backend/llvm/` mentions `rt_value_ops` exactly once, as the SHAPE of the record in `typed_carrier_v2.generated.go`; there is no descriptor constructor anywhere in the compiler. The only constructions in the tree are the runtime's own and the harnesses under `internal/vm/testdata/`. This is not cosmetic: `rt_slot_operations_preflight` refuses any descriptor whose `move_init` or `plan_cross` is null, unconditionally and before any flag is consulted (`rt_slot_control.c:42`), so no owner can be retyped until both exist for real types. WHAT MADE IT INVISIBLE: Wave B's other six items DID land - the ABI manifest, the slot lifecycle, the detached dispatch helpers, the lock-detachment proof - and the wave was treated as closed on that evidence. An item that says GENERATE is satisfied by emitted code, not by a header that declares what would be emitted. | Open | Epic 23b Wave B, blocking P2/P3 | The compiler emits `rt_value_ops` for real types, and the proof is `rt_slot_operations_preflight` returning OK for an emitted descriptor rather than a hand-written harness one - shown failing first for a descriptor with a null `plan_cross`, since that is the field nothing produces today. Until then no owner migration may start, and any plan that schedules one is scheduling against an unmet entry condition. **COST CORRECTION 2026-08-20 (from RV2-DEBT-232's answer): this row's fear that a mandatory `plan_cross` would multiply the generation work is WRONG - the multiplier is 1.** Every descriptor Wave B can emit today is non-crossing by construction, because `Entry.checkSlots` refuses any entry that sets a staged bit and ShardMovable/CrossClonable/Droppable/Traceable are all staged (`internal/valueops/flags.go:106-113`, `entry.go:165-180`). So all of them need the same shared refusal, the compiler already ships the one-symbol-fills-a-slot mechanism (`slotRule.runtimeSymbol`/`RuntimeFilledSlot`), and the body exists as `harness_noop_plan`. The gap that actually needs work is `move_init`: `valueops.Entry` has no field for it either and `slotRules` is flag-indexed, so neither unconditional slot has a row - and move_init is the half that needs a real per-type body. **THE STRUCTURAL MODEL OWED, owner ruling 2026-08-20:** `slotRules` currently encodes 'a slot exists if and only if a capability bit names it', which is why BOTH mandatory slots fell out of the registry. The table must model APPLICABILITY instead - `unconditional \| capability(flag)` - after which one structural edit produces both missing slots naturally: `move_init` per-type generated, `plan_cross` bound to a shared module-local invariant trap until a descriptor has a real crossing implementation. This is the first concrete piece of work in closing this row, and it is a registry change rather than a descriptor-emission change. **DONE 2026-08-20 (`12ddc918`).** `slotRules` now models presence and fill as separate axes; both mandatory rows exist; `RuntimeFilledSlot` is replaced by a slot-name-keyed `SlotFiller` that fails closed (the old one took a bit, so a query for bit 0 would have matched the first zero-bit row and answered wrongly); and the backend emits `__surge_value_plan_cross_unavailable` as a `define internal` trap in the same commit as the row naming it. `Entry` gained no field, per the ruling that the backend derives a move body's name from the type id. **The test that would have caught the original defect now exists**: `TestEveryManifestValueOpsSlotHasARule` compares the table against the rt_value_ops RECORD in both directions and on both axes, shown failing with the `move_init` row deleted and again with `plan_cross` demoted to a capability slot. (2) A descriptor binding a type's clone is a REFERENCE, and reachability did not know it: the first clonable descriptor named `fn.2` for `string.__clone`, which no source line calls, and the module never defined it. | RV2-DEBT-232 | A PURELY LOCAL OWNER IS REQUIRED TO PROVE A CROSSING CAPABILITY IT WILL NEVER USE. `rt_slot_operations_preflight` refuses a descriptor unless BOTH `move_init` and `plan_cross` are non-null (`rt_slot_control.c:42`), while every other callback is checked biconditionally against its flag - `copy_init` exactly when RT_VALUE_FLAG_COPY, `drop_in_place` exactly when RT_VALUE_FLAG_DROPPABLE, `cross_move_init` exactly when RT_VALUE_FLAG_SHARD_MOVABLE, and so on (`:45-57`). So a local channel of a type that can never cross a shard must still carry a cross-planning callback in order to be admitted. Raised 2026-08-20 by the owner while ruling D3, with the explicit instruction not to repair it silently inside a migration step. The question this row must answer is whether the unconditional pair is deliberate - `plan_cross` being read-only and total, it may be intended as a universal preflight rather than a capability - or whether admission should split into a local-owner check (layout, `move_init`, plus the flag-conditional copy/clone/drop) and a separate cross check (`plan_cross`, `cross_move_init`, `cross_clone_init`). **ANSWERED 2026-08-20, AND THE ROW'S OWN HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED.** The requirement is an authored, hash-frozen DECLARATION with no recorded reason - not a considered universal doctrine and not an accidental artefact. (1) THE DECLARATION IS PRIOR TO THE CHECK: `runtime/abi/typed_carrier_v2.json` marks `plan_cross` `"nullable": false` / 'Always-present read-only crossing preflight', wording it shares only with `move_init`, and it is byte-identical at the freeze commit `0db416cc` (2026-08-04) - BEFORE `rt_slot_control.c` existed (`dbf536f3`, 2026-08-05) and before the check landed (`8512f4c1`). Nullability is not a default: `internal/abimanifest/validate.go:242-243` refuses a callback type that does not state it. Someone typed `false`. (2) THE MANIFEST'S RULE IS MECHANICAL, NOT DOCTRINAL: six capability bits each NAME one slot (`_CLONABLE`->clone_init, `_DROPPABLE`->drop_in_place, `_TRACEABLE`->trace, `_SHARD_MOVABLE`->cross_move_init, `_CROSS_CLONABLE`->cross_clone_init, `_COPY`->copy_init), exactly 1:1 with the six nullable callbacks. `move_init` and `plan_cross` are non-null for one reason: **no bit names them.** The one recorded stretch of reasoning behaves exactly as that rule predicts - `NOTES.md:6410-6412` argues mandatory move, argues flag-gated drop, and is SILENT on plan_cross. (3) THE ROW'S HYPOTHESIS IS CONTRADICTED BY THE FROZEN CONTRACT: `rt_value_plan_cross_fn` takes `mode: rt_cross_mode`, whose only two values are `RT_CROSS_MOVE` and `RT_CROSS_CLONE` - both naming apply callbacks that exist only under `SHARD_MOVABLE`/`CROSS_CLONABLE`. For a type with neither flag there is NO LEGAL VALUE OF `mode`: the function is not total on such a type, it is vacuous. And none of the seven `rt_carrier_status` values means 'this type cannot cross'. So 'a universal preflight every type can answer' cannot be written into the docs - the frozen `mode` parameter contradicts it. (4) 'THE CHECK MIRRORS THE MANIFEST' OVERSTATES IT: `nullable` on a callback field is machine-inert everywhere else - `cType` never reads it (`internal/abimanifest/render_c.go:175-196`), the generated header contains ZERO `nonnull`, and the checks header has 154 `_Static_assert`s and zero NULL tests. The manifest carries NINE `nullable: false` fields and exactly TWO are null-checked anywhere in the tree, both inside this preflight - `rt_key_ops.hash`/`equal` are not, in a record that EMBEDS `rt_value_ops`. Enforcement was its own act. **(5) THE COST PREMISE IN THIS ROW AND IN RV2-DEBT-230 IS WRONG: THE MULTIPLIER IS 1, NOT N.** Every descriptor Wave B can emit today is non-crossing BY CONSTRUCTION - `FlagShardMovable`/`FlagCrossClonable`/`FlagDroppable`/`FlagTraceable` are `staged: true` and `Entry.checkSlots` refuses any entry that sets a staged bit (`internal/valueops/flags.go:106-113`, `entry.go:165-180`), so `Flags` can only ever carry Copy and Clonable. 100% of emittable descriptors need a plan_cross and 100% need the SAME one. The compiler already ships the mechanism for one shared symbol filling a slot across all descriptors - `slotRule.runtimeSymbol` + `RuntimeFilledSlot`, instantiated as `rt_value_copy_init_unbound_trap` (`flags.go:66-102`) - and a non-crossing plan_cross is STRICTLY EASIER than that precedent, because the copy trap must be branched away from for want of a width while a permanent refusal is simply the right answer. The body already exists: `harness_noop_plan` (`internal/vm/testdata/slot_control_harness.c:106-111`), six lines, three `(void)`s, bound as the plan_cross of ten descriptors across four files including two whose flags are 0. **The real gap is mandatory-slot-shaped, not plan_cross-shaped**: `valueops.Entry` has no field for `move_init` either and `slotRules` is flag-indexed, so NEITHER unconditional slot has a row - one edit gives both, and plan_cross is the cheaper half. NOTE the citation drift this answer corrects: the unconditional pair is `rt_slot_control.c:42`; `:44` is the middle of the COPY biconditional and was cited wrongly in four documents. **SPLITTING PREFLIGHT IS REFUSED BY COST, NOT ONLY BY SILENCE**: flipping `nullable` moves the canonical hash (`parse.go:57-70`), hence the mandatory link sentinel and all four generated views; splitting only the C check while the manifest still says `nullable: false` would put the runtime in contradiction with its own frozen contract. THREE NARROWER DECISIONS REMAIN OPEN AND NEED A RULING: (a) which status a permanent refusal returns - `INVALID_STATE` is the harness precedent but its frozen semantics is about slot lifecycle, not type capability; (b) who supplies the shared symbol - a runtime ABI symbol (additive, moves the hash) or a backend-emitted module-local refusal bound everywhere (zero ABI cost, nothing in the record forbids it); (c) what the documentation says, given that no reason exists in the record - `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md` contains ZERO occurrences of `plan_cross`, so the crossing-planning contract was never written into the durable doc at all. ALSO ESTABLISHED: `plan_cross` has ZERO call sites in the entire repository, and `rt_value_ops.c` ships detached dispatch helpers for copy, move and drop but none for it. **OWNER RULINGS 2026-08-20, ALL THREE OPEN DECISIONS TAKEN, AND THE ROW'S ORIGINAL FORK WITHDRAWN.** (a) **The stub is an invariant trap that does not return normally.** No new status is added and `INVALID_STATE` is not reinterpreted. A `plan_cross` call on a descriptor whose own flags admit neither cross mode is a compiler/runtime protocol violation, not a recoverable refusal: the caller chose an operation the descriptor excludes. The recoverable statuses are outcomes of a LEGAL call; a status here would assert the call was legal and merely refused. If C requires a `return` after the abort it is unreachable filler, not callback semantics - and a test must pin the stub as TERMINAL: a child process that reaches it dies on the invariant rather than receiving a status and continuing. (b) **The shared stub is emitted by the BACKEND as a module-local function, not added to the runtime ABI.** Adding a runtime symbol for a function the runtime has no right to call would extend the ABI, move the hash and the sentinel, and churn four generated views for no new runtime capability; the accepted design already makes `ValueOps<T>` compiler-generated and descriptors compiler-emitted process-lifetime data, and nothing requires a descriptor's function pointer to name an exported runtime symbol. Working name: `__surge_value_plan_cross_unavailable`. (c) **The documentation separates ABI PRESENCE from OPERATION CAPABILITY and does not invent a historical motive.** The normative rule now sits beside `ValueOps<T>` in `23-storage-model-and-typed-carrier-abi.md`; the historical finding is in `NOTES.md`; `RUNTIME_V2.md` is deliberately left alone, because it already delegates the physical carrier contract to that document and duplicating it would drift again. The load-bearing sentence pair: `move_init` is SEMANTICALLY unconditional - every initialized `T` can transfer ownership into an empty destination - while `plan_cross` is only STRUCTURALLY unconditional, its slot always present while its callable domain is decided by the cross capability flags. Without that pair a later reader sees two non-null slots and concludes they mean the same thing. **THE ROW'S ORIGINAL FORK IS WITHDRAWN BY THE OWNER: preflight stays single.** Its job is now stated positively - check that a descriptor is structurally coherent, mandatory pointers present and capability-gated pointers matching their bits. Whether a `T` may cross a shard is a different layer and preflight does not answer it. REMAINING: the manifest's own `semantics` text for `plan_cross` still reads 'Always-present read-only crossing preflight' with no mention of the capability split. Correcting it costs an ABI identity move - `CanonicalBytes` marshals the WHOLE manifest including every `semantics` string (`parse.go:57-66`) and `Semantics` carries no `json:"-"` - so the hash, the link sentinel and every generated view change for a comment. **DONE 2026-08-20 by owner ruling - the price was judged worth paying, because the sentence that produced the wrong reading lived in the machine contract itself.** The field now contrasts the two mandatory slots explicitly, and the identity moved: `f30fcfb0…` -> `9653d773…`, six generated views rewritten, objects built before that commit no longer link against a runtime built after it. Gates green afterwards: the ABI manifest gate, `c-check`, `ctidy`, `cppcheck` and the slot-control gate. | Open, answered | Epic 23b Wave B | The intent behind the unconditional `plan_cross` requirement is established from the design rather than guessed - and then either the requirement is documented as universal with its reason, or preflight splits into local and cross admission. Evidence either way is a descriptor for a shard-pinned type being admitted by a local owner, which is the case that decides it. **CLOSED 2026-08-21. THE DESIGN HALF WAS ALREADY DONE; THE MISSING PIECE WAS A TEST THAT EXECUTES THE STUB RATHER THAN READS IT.** Verified in place: preflight is still UNIFIED -- `rt_slot_control.c:14` is the only `rt_slot_operations_preflight`, with the unconditional pair at `:42` and the six flag biconditionals at `:43-57`, and the two wrappers (`:62`, `:93`) both route through it; `plan_cross` is required unconditionally and filled by a module-local invariant trap (`internal/valueops/flags.go:118` names the symbol, the emission is `typed_carrier_abi.go:63`); and the docs separate ABI PRESENCE from operation CAPABILITY. **WHAT WAS MISSING AND IS NOW THERE:** the only proof of terminality was TEXTUAL -- a test reading the emitted body for `llvm.trap`, no `ret`, and `llvm.used`. Nothing ever CALLED the stub; a grep for a call-shaped `plan_cross(` across the repo returned zero hits. `TestPlanCrossUnavailableStubIsTerminalWhenCalled` now links a compiler-emitted descriptor and dispatches THROUGH ITS OWN SLOT, so what is proven is that the slot a real descriptor carries is terminal, not that some function somewhere traps. **TWO TRAPS AVOIDED IN WRITING IT, both caught by adversarial review of the first draft.** (1) It does NOT pin a signal number: `llvm.trap` lowers to `ud2`/SIGILL on x86-64 and `brk`/SIGTRAP on AArch64, so pinning one would make this gate host-specific -- the exact defect the copy-trap gate's own comment warns against. It asserts `Signaled()` plus 'did not return'. (2) It does NOT accept 'the child died' as proof: a mis-sliced module, a wrong symbol in the slot or a fault in the probe would all pass that. The probe prints and FLUSHES a marker immediately before the dispatch, and the test requires that marker PRESENT and the 'returned status' line ABSENT -- reaching the stub and not coming back is the claim. **NEGATIVE CONTROL:** `defectReturningPlanCrossStub` makes the same writer emit `ret i32 0`, and `TestAReturningPlanCrossStubIsCaught` shows the probe then completes with a status -- so the gate can tell a trap from a return. Both tests are wired into `runtime-v2-abi-manifest-check` alongside the descriptor admission pair, because a test no gate selects is a test nobody runs. | Closed 2026-08-21 |
| RV2-DEBT-233 | **A BARE TYPE MEMBER OF A MIXED UNION IS NEVER DROPPED, SO EVERYTHING IT OWNS LEAKS.** Surge unions may mix tag members with bare type members - `type Result<T, E> = ResOk(T) \| E` (`testdata/golden/sema/valid/mixed_union_exhaustive.sg:4`) - and both the drop PREDICATE and the drop BODY read only the tag members. `typeOwnsHeapRec`'s union arm walks `e.tagCases(id)` and its `PayloadTypes` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_drop_glue.go:106-118`), and `emitUnionPayloadDrops` walks the same list (`:597-600`), so a member that is a plain type rather than a tag is invisible to both. The predicate therefore answers 'owns no heap' for a union that owns a string, no drop glue is generated, and if glue were generated it would still skip that member. MEASURED 2026-08-20 at `d1a81b26` with valgrind, and the two arms differ by one word of source: `let r: Result<int, string> = s` where `s` is a heap string reports **58 bytes definitely lost in 1 block**, while the same string in the same union reached through a TAG - `type Result2<T, E> = ResOk(T) \| ResErr(E)` with `ResErr(s)` - reports **0 bytes in use at exit**. The bare-member program exits 0 and prints nothing wrong, so nothing but a leak checker sees it. FOUND BY A DISAGREEMENT, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: sema and the backend hold two independent verdicts on 'droppable' - `capability.CarrierDroppable` and `typeOwnsHeap` - and the owner accepted that duplication as the cost of emitting descriptors from the backend (ruling 2026-08-19). Comparing them across the corpus found seven types where they disagree, ALL in the same direction: sema says droppable, the backend says it owns no heap. Six of the seven are the same shape as this one. A second source of truth is a liability until it is compared, and comparing it is how this surfaced. **THE ROOT IS IN THE BUILDER, NOT ONLY THE WALK.** `buildTagLayouts` handles a bare type member by recursing into it when it is itself a union and adding NO case otherwise (`internal/mir/lower_meta.go:348-355`); the async builder is blunter still and simply `continue`s (`internal/mir/async_lowering_helpers.go:105`). So `TagLayouts` is a tag-only view by construction, and every consumer that treats it as the union's full membership inherits the blind spot. **A SECOND HAZARD IS SUSPECTED HERE AND IS NOT PROVEN - do not repeat it as fact.** The physical layout DOES count bare members (`internal/layout/compute.go:270-280` gives `UnionMemberType` its own payload), so a case index into `TagLayouts` and a case index into `PhysicalFacts.UnionCase` need not mean the same variant, and `emitUnionPayloadDrops` feeds the former into the latter (`emit_drop_glue.go:626`). A probe with the bare member FIRST - `type Mixed<E, T> = E \| Wrapped(T)` with a heap string in the tag - was built and run under valgrind and came back CLEAN, 0 bytes and 0 errors, so either the indices agree or the two payload offsets coincided for that shape. The disproof attempt is recorded because it is evidence. **CONFIRMED 2026-08-20, AND IT DOES NOT CORRUPT - IT REFUSES.** The case the earlier probe lacked is a NESTED union: `type Inner = T1(A) \| T2(B)` used as a bare member of `type Outer = Outer1(C) \| Inner`. `collectTagCases` flattens Inner's tags into `TagLayouts[Outer]` as [Outer1, T1, T2] while the physical layout has TWO direct members, so a flattened index of 2 is handed to a case list of length 2 and the compiler stops with `LLVM emit failed: missing finalized union case 2 for type#1532`. So the index hazard is real and is caught by an emit-time refusal rather than by silently freeing the wrong arm - the honest reading is 'unsupported form', not 'memory corruption'. **AND THE NESTED FORM IS UNREACHABLE ANYWAY, WHICH SHRINKS THE MIGRATION.** Assigning a hoisted tag straight into the outer union is refused by sema (`cannot assign T1<int> to Outer<int, int, bool>`); going through the inner union first reaches MIR and dies there (`MIR validation failed: local L9 (v): unknown type`); and the VM lane panics `VM1999: unimplemented: __to conversion` on the same program. A pure union of value types is rejected outright (`pure union of value types is not allowed; use tagged variants instead`). So no program can write a hoisted tag today, which means **the migration's writer never has to materialise two discriminants** - it always writes a direct-member index. The flattened view is needed for READING only. **SCOPED 2026-08-20 AFTER THE OWNER CHOSE THE FULL-REPRESENTATION PATH, AND THE SCOPING FOUND TWO FACTS THAT CHANGE IT.** **(1) A BARE MEMBER HAS NO DISCRIMINANT IN THE NATIVE LANE - not a wrong one, none.** Every native writer of the tag word is keyed by a tag name or symbol (`emit_helpers_tags.go:181,193`, `emit_intrinsics_tag.go:30`, `emit_union_cast.go:211`); none has a bare-member entry point. The value simply PASSES THROUGH as the union (`emit_return.go:52,85` return the operand unchanged when its type equals a `UnionMemberType` of the expected union). On assignment it is worse: `storeIntoPlace` forces the destination's spelling (`emit_instr.go:203-213`) and a union is a byte run, so `emitValueStore` memcpys the UNION's size out of whatever the scalar handle pointed at - which is the shape of the measured leak. The VM, by contrast, writes a real discriminant for a bare arm and takes it from the SAME enumeration the layout uses (`internal/vm/storage_union.go:158-201` -> `storage_ops.go:483-496`, arms built one-for-one from `info.Members` at `storage_shape.go:284-297`, with an explicit cardinality refusal at `:279-283`). **CONSEQUENCE: the walk cannot be widened before the writer exists.** A repaired `emitUnionPayloadDrops` with a bare arm would switch on a value nothing writes, turning a 58-byte leak into a wrong-arm free. Writer and walk must ship together. **(2) `TagLayouts` IS NOT A LIST OF DIRECT TAG MEMBERS - it is a FLATTENED, DEDUPLICATED tag set.** `collectTagCases` recurses into a bare member that is itself a union (`lower_meta.go:352-355`) and drops duplicates through a `seen` key (`:305-311`). So its index differs from the physical index in TWO ways, not one: bare members are skipped AND nested tags are hoisted. A tag-only projection of the direct membership therefore CANNOT reproduce today's `TagLayouts` content, which is what the owner's step 1 assumed. Any full-representation work must keep the flattened view as its own derived thing rather than as a filter. **MEASURED AND NOT A DEFECT: the two lanes' discriminants disagree by construction but the disagreement is not observable.** The native lane stores a flattened-tag index and the VM stores a Members index into the same 4-byte slot, yet each lane reads back its own, and a mixed union round-trips identically on both: `type M<E, T> = E \| Tagged(T)` with `Tagged(7)` prints `result=7` under both backends. Recorded so the next reader does not file a parity defect that does not exist - the representations differ, the answers do not. **FIXED 2026-08-20 under the owner's nested-union ruling, and the leak is gone: 58 bytes definitely lost -> 0 bytes in use at exit, 0 errors, with the tag-reached control still at 0.** Four pieces, landed together because a partial one would have turned the leak into a wrong-arm free. (1) `mir.UnionCaseMeta` + `ModuleMeta.UnionCases` record the FULL direct membership of every union, in `UnionInfo.Members` order, each case carrying the `PhysicalCaseIndex` the layout is enumerated by. `TagLayouts` is untouched and stays the flattened tag view for tag switches - the type's own comment now records why it can never be membership: it can be LONGER than the members and it is NOT INJECTIVE, since two distinct `nothing` alternatives collapse into one entry. (2) A new accessor returns membership and frozen facts together and REFUSES when the two enumerations disagree on cardinality, mirroring the VM's own refusal. It fails CLOSED, which is the opposite of what it replaced: `typeOwnsHeapRec` answered 'owns no heap' on any error, and a union of only bare members has no tag-layout entry at all, so `int \| string` silently answered no. (3) A WRITER. A value whose type is a bare member of a union destination is now MATERIALISED - direct-member index at offset 0, then the member's value at that case's payload offset - instead of passing through untouched while the destination's spelling won and the union's whole size was memcpy'd out of the member's handle. (4) The drop body walks the membership and switches on the direct index. **THE FAIL-CLOSED ACCESSOR IMMEDIATELY EARNED ITSELF**: eight crossing tests went red with `missing union membership metadata`, which uncovered a SECOND builder - `ensureTagLayout` (`internal/mir/async_lowering_helpers.go`) mints unions the main lowering never sees and recorded only the tag view. Both builders now record both. The old code would have answered that gap with silence. EVIDENCE: three tests, each shown FAILING against the reverted piece - 'no discriminant N is stored for the bare member' with the writer disabled, 'no arm for bare member N' with the drop body returned to the flattened list, and an enumeration-agreement test that refuses to pass if the probe stops carrying a mixed union. Both behaviour lanes green. **REVERTED 2026-08-20, SAME DAY, AND THE REVERT IS THE FINDING.** The fix was real — the leak went 58 bytes to 0 — but it was landed HALF-MIGRATED and that is worse than not landing it. Numbers, measured with `SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 go test -tags runtime_v2_pending ./internal/vm -count=1`: **49 failures before, 62 after.** The 24 that appeared cluster on one thing — seven `FDRegistry` rows, three `HTTPOwnerLocalBehavior`, `json_validate`, `http_cookie_helpers`, `http_query_helpers`, `fs_metadata_smoke` — every one of them a path built on `Erring`. **WHY HALF A MIGRATION IS WORSE THAN NONE.** While every site read the flattened tag view they were wrong TOGETHER, consistently, and the only symptom was a leak. Migrating the drop walk to the direct index without the writers made them DISAGREE: `type Maybe = string \| nothing` has direct membership [0] string, [1] nothing, while the flattened view holds only `nothing` at position 0 — so `let m: Maybe = nothing` stored 0, the drop walk read 0 as the STRING member, and freed uninitialised alloca bytes as a string. Five lines of source, segmentation fault, measured: exit 0 before, exit 139 after, exit 0 again once the writer was migrated too. **FIVE SITES FOUND, AND A SIXTH STILL AT LARGE, WHICH IS WHY THIS WAS REVERTED RATHER THAN PATCHED AGAIN.** Migrated during the attempt: the drop predicate, the drop body, the bare-member writer, `tagCaseMeta` (the one chokepoint both readers and writers take their number from), the union clone walk, and `canDuplicateValueRec` (which was fail-OPEN on error). After all six, `json_validate` still died in `drop.type1518` calling `rt_string_free` on an invalid pointer — so at least one more site feeds a flattened index somewhere, and chasing them one at a time was multiplying risk instead of reducing it. **WHAT THE NEXT ATTEMPT MUST DO DIFFERENTLY**, and this is the durable part: enumerate EVERY site that either stores a discriminant or indexes `PhysicalFacts.UnionCase`, from the code rather than from a plan, and migrate them in ONE commit. `emit_union_cast.go` is known-flattened on both sides and was never reached. **A SEVENTH SITE SURFACED AFTER LANDING, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT MISSED IT WAS BROKEN, NOT THE REASONING.** `async_return` returns a bare member AS ITSELF. In MIR the tag arm arrives as `L7 = cast move L6 to <union>` while the bare arm arrives as `async_return move L9, move L2` with L2 typed as the MEMBER -- so the widening that `emit_return.go` does for an ordinary return had no counterpart on the async path. Once the reader was narrowing at the payload offset and this writer was still leaving the member at zero, every `compare` over an async `Erring` result read the discriminant as the member's first field. Reproduced with no networking at all: an `async fn` returning `MyResult<uint>`, exit 0 before the migration and SIGSEGV after; the fix widens at `async_return` using the function's declared `Result` type and the same materialisation the ordinary return uses. **WHY IT ESCAPED THE GATE:** the run I closed the row on had died at the default 10-minute `go test` timeout -- the log ends `FAIL surge/internal/vm 600.023s` -- so tests that never executed read as tests that passed, and 'no new failures' was a statement about a truncated list. `runtime-v2-fd-registry-check` and `runtime-v2-net-handle-check`, both green earlier the same day, were red again and only the per-gate sweep caught it. Tagged runs of this package need `--timeout 3600s`, and the tail of the log has to be read before any count is quoted. **CLOSED 2026-08-21, AND THE FIX WAS ONE CHOKEPOINT RATHER THAN THIRTY EDITS.** `tagCaseMeta` is the single place a discriminant number is decided -- every writer, every `icmp`, every `switch` and all seventeen callers of `emitTagValueSinglePayload` take their number from it. Making IT answer with the direct index reconciled the whole set at once; only the sites that bypass it needed migrating by hand: the union cast (its own switch, its own destination index, its own layout indexing), the clone walk, the drop walk, and the three predicates that skipped bare members. Representation C needed two new operations, symmetric by construction: `emitUnionMaterialiseBareMember` widens a bare member into its union on the way in, and `emitUnionNarrowToBareMember` reads it back out at its case's payload offset on the way out. Both are needed because MIR spells each as a plain `move` and is right not to know where a payload sits. **NUMBERS.** Tagged set, same command throughout (`SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 go test -tags runtime_v2_pending ./internal/vm -count=1`): **49 failures before, 19 after, and NONE of the 19 is new** -- 30 tests fixed, zero regressed. The four live defects this row recorded are all gone, verified as programs rather than as test names: `type Maybe = string \| nothing` went from VM `hello` against native exit 139 to both lanes printing `hello`; `type M = int \| Foo(string, int)` went from not compiling at all to running; the union leak went 58 bytes to 0 under valgrind; and `json_validate` went exit 139 to exit 0. **FIVE REGRESSIONS WERE FOUND AND FIXED BEFORE LANDING, and the method matters more than the count.** A first pass showed 25 failures with 5 that were not in the baseline, four of them network. Rather than assume they were the network fix landed earlier the same day, the five were run on a worktree at `1d65610e` -- fdreg present, migration absent -- where all five PASSED, which made them this change's and nobody else's. Their cause was the last representation-C site: `net_make_error`, `fs_make_error` and `entropy_make_error` returned an untagged struct pointer. That was consistent as long as the READER also worked by convention C; once the reader expected a discriminant it was not. All three now allocate their case's tag and copy the error's bytes to the payload offset. **WHAT THIS ROW GOT RIGHT THE SECOND TIME:** the inventory came from the code and the migration landed as one commit. The first attempt fixed four sites of thirty and shipped, which is why it had a sixth. | Closed 2026-08-21 | **INVENTORIED 2026-08-20 BY TWO INDEPENDENT READERS, AND THERE ARE THREE REPRESENTATIONS, NOT TWO.** That is why the last attempt kept finding one more site. | RV2-DEBT-240 | **A `timeout` BUDGET IS NOT WALL-CLOCK TIME, SO A TASK WAITING ON THE NETWORK IS CANCELLED AT ONCE.** `timeout(task, ms)` measures its budget against the executor's virtual clock `ex->now_ms`, and an executor that considers itself idle jumps that clock straight to the next deadline. A task parked on a socket is idle by that definition, so the deadline arrives immediately in real time. Found 2026-08-21 while diagnosing RV2-DEBT-239, and proven by a DIFFERENTIAL ON ONE CONDITION: the identical program built with a 1000 ms budget and with a 60000 ms budget dies in the same 0.006-0.107 s distribution — a sixty-second budget expiring in six milliseconds is not a slow clock, it is a clock that is not time. **THE SITE FIRST RECORDED HERE WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION IS WORTH KEEPING.** `next_ready` (`runtime/native/rt_async_state.c:512-543`) is the HONEST path: it polls the network for the whole remaining budget and jumps only after the wait really happened -- which is why a single-worker run measured 1.006 s for a 1000 ms budget while a four-worker run measured 0.0057 s. `next_ready` is also reachable only when one worker exists (`rt_async_task.c:349-371`). The clock is jumped by the IO THREAD, in `rt_io_main` (`runtime/native/rt_worker_turn.c:261`, `:284`, `:299`), and gdb caught it there with `have_net=1 have_timer=1 idle=1 deadline=60000` and `ex->now_ms == 0` -- sixty seconds of budget created by one CAS out of zero. The branch at `:280-288` says why it was written: the poller's owner runs 1 ms slices and never advances the clock itself, so someone had to, or every sleeper starved behind a worker monopolising the poll. Reproduced on 2, 3, 4 and 8 threads alike (6 of 6 each), so it is not a race for ownership of the poller. WHAT IT COSTS BEYOND THE TEST: every Surge program that waits on the network under a timeout gets a false expiry, which is the whole shape of a server. It also silently defeats the accept loop of `stdlib/http`, and therefore any behavioural evidence about the HTTP owner path. **CLOSED 2026-08-21 (`0183443c`).** The rule is expressed as an invariant rather than a special case: `now_ms <= real_elapsed_ms + clock_free_ms`, where the second term counts virtual milliseconds nobody waited for -- yield ticks, and idle jumps taken while no socket waiter was registered. The ceiling therefore rises with the wall on its own, so a refusal to move the clock expires by itself and cannot deadlock; simply declining to jump DOES deadlock, measured three times while choosing the form. Differential after the fix: budget 1000 ms on 4 threads went 0.0057 s -> 1.010 s, budget 60000 ms went 0.0061 s -> still alive at 8 s, and `sleep(5000)` with no network stayed at 0.007 s, which is the invariant that had to survive. `TestRuntimeV2TimeoutOverNetWaitSurvivesItsWallBudget` and its negative control are selected by `runtime-v2-waiter-check`. Eight time- and network-sensitive gates re-run green: lock, waiter, lifecycle, accept, heap, net-handle, fd-registry, http-owner. | Closed (2026-08-21) | Runtime / scheduler clock | A task parked on network readiness holds the virtual clock: `advance_time_to_next_timer` refuses to jump while a shard has registered net waiters, or the budget is measured against wall time for such tasks. The closing test is the differential above — a 60000 ms budget must outlive a 1000 ms one — shown failing on the unfixed tree. |
