| RV2-DEBT-243 | **A REFERENCE-COUNTED SCALAR STORED PAST A CALL WAS NEVER RETAINED, SO A `Channel<float>` EITHER SEGFAULTED OR PRINTED A WRONG ANSWER.** Found 2026-08-21 while censusing channel element kinds ahead of D3. `float` is the one element whose descriptor is both COPY and DROPPABLE (`flags 5`): Copy at the surface, a counted `SurgeBigFloat` block underneath. Sending one gave the channel a second owner without bumping the count, and both owners released. TWO FACES, one cause, both measured on the native lane at `e906a733`: with the sender's binding still live the program printed the right answer and then **SEGFAULTED** at teardown (`./target/debug/float_live` -> `Segmentation fault (core dumped)`, exit 139); with the sender a temporary the channel kept a freed block and the program printed a **WRONG ANSWER** -- `small` for `1.5 > 1.0` -- and exited 0. The differential names the condition exactly: the same program without a channel prints `big`, and the answer flips on whether the sender's copy is still alive at the receive. THE FIX IS ONE EDGE THAT WAS NEVER CONNECTED. `ArgContractStore` already documents this case verbatim -- 'a reference-counted scalar stored past the call needs its own retained reference exactly like any other stored value' (`internal/mir/instr.go:192-203`) -- and `applyChannelSendContracts` already marked the position STORE. But the operand kind is chosen separately, by `placeOperand`'s consuming rule, which produces a bare Copy at a call site because most callees BORROW: a float handed to an arithmetic entry point must not be retained. So the contract said sink and the operand said copy, and nothing reconciled them. `retainStoredRefCountedArgs` now upgrades Copy to Retain for exactly the STORE positions, which also covers `rt_array_push`, `rt_map_insert`, `__index_set` and `__task_create` -- every sink in the audited table, not just the channel. WHY NO GATE SAW IT: the only executable channel fixtures in the tree are `Channel<int>`; every golden async row, the single `testdata/mt` fixture and both channel-touching parity rows are int, and the sole heap-owning composite channel element in the repository lives in a compile-time IR test that is never run. | Closed (2026-08-21) | MIR lowering / call argument contracts | A stored reference-counted scalar is passed as `OperandRetain` and a borrowed one is not, both pinned by tests that fail on revert; and an executable fixture sends a float into a channel from a live binding and from a temporary, asserting the value survives both. | 
| RV2-DEBT-244 | **THE VM LANE REFUSES A CORRECT `Channel<float>` PROGRAM WITH `VM3301: use-after-free`.** Measured 2026-08-21 at `e906a733`, before and after the RV2-DEBT-243 fix, so it is independent of it: sending a `float` into a channel and unpacking the received `Option<float>` prints the right answer and then panics `VM3301: use-after-free: local "x" used after drop`, where `x` is the binding the `compare` arm introduces for the RECEIVED value. That value is freshly produced by the receive; nothing has dropped it. The native lane runs the same program cleanly once RV2-DEBT-243 is fixed, so the two lanes now disagree on a program the language allows. NOTE FOR WHOEVER TAKES IT: this is why the fixture added for RV2-DEBT-243 carries a `.backends` sidecar naming only `llvm` -- the exclusion is a defect in this row, not a property of the program, and the sidecar is the place a reader will find that stated. Two further VM/native divergences on channel payloads were reported by the same census and are NOT yet reproduced by hand: a parked send of a MOVED payload panicking `VM1002` where native runs, and a composite sent from a retiring frame on the sync-channel path panicking `VM1999: stale reference`, bisected to `75b56918`. Both need re-deriving before they are trusted. | Open | VM lane, channel payload ownership | The float fixture's `.backends` sidecar is DELETED and the row runs on both lanes, plus the two divergences above are each either reproduced and fixed or shown not to exist. A fix that makes the panic go away by weakening VM3301 does not close this: the check must still catch a genuine use-after-free. | 
| RV2-DEBT-245 | **TWO OF THE THREE CHANNEL RECLAIM PATHS ARE UNREACHABLE OR UNMEASURABLE, SO THE TYPED-STORAGE FLIP HAS NOTHING TO BE MEASURED AGAINST ON THEM.** Established 2026-08-21/22 while building coverage ahead of D3. **P1, the drain inside `rt_channel_free`:** compiled code never emits the call, and the only chain into it is `release_entry` -> `rt_channel_free_when_unlocked` -> `rt_channel_free`, which is a FAR channel path. A local channel is therefore never freed and its buffered payloads leak by construction -- the same fact RV2-DEBT-155 owns from the other end. Consequence for D3: the flip's ring drain cannot be observed in-process at all, so a typed drain that reclaims nothing would look identical to one that works. **P3, the cancelled receiver whose mailbox already holds a value** (`rt_async_channel.c:293-297`): reachable from source -- `spawn` a task that blocks on `recv`, send into it, then `cancel()` -- and it runs, but the payload's fate could not be established by measurement. Differential on payload SIZE (10 bytes vs 2010, everything else identical): allocation counts are byte-for-byte equal at 86 allocs / 6 frees, the extra 2000 bytes appear in neither `definitely lost` nor `still reachable`, and the background `still reachable` figure moves by ~700 bytes between the two runs, which is larger than the signal. Valgrind cannot answer this one; a counter inside the runtime can. NOT A BLOCKER FOR D3, and deliberately so: what IS covered is P2 (`rt_channel_release_payload`, the only path a linked binary actually reaches), now pinned for a string element and a heap-owning composite by `TestRuntimeV2SelectReleasesA{String,Composite}PayloadExactlyOnce` in the heap gate. | Open | Runtime / channel payload reclamation coverage | P1 becomes observable -- either a local channel is reclaimed (which is RV2-DEBT-155) or a test pins today's leak so the flip's own behaviour is readable against it. P3 is measured with an instrument that can see it: a runtime-side drop counter, or an element whose destructor is observable from the program, rather than a leak summary whose noise exceeds the payload. Each of the seven element classes named in the D3 census gets a row on each path, or is recorded as deliberately out of scope with the reason. **2026-08-24, the instrument exists.** `TestRuntimeV2ChannelOwnedElementArrivesExactlyOnce` drives a channel whose element OWNS a heap block: its move empties the source, its drop frees the block and is COUNTED from inside the program, and the receiver frees what it was handed. That answers both halves this row said could not be measured. P1: the stand calls `rt_channel_free` itself, so the ring/park drain runs in-process and its drops land in the census -- the buffered rows read `drops=0` because every value was delivered, which is a reading the old leak summary could not produce. P3: the `owned-cancelled-sender` row parks a sender with a staged value, cancels it, and reads `drops=1 received=0` -- the payload's fate, by count, not by a figure smaller than the background noise. The rows also run under ASan+UBSan and TSan through `runtime-v2-carrier-sanitizer-check`. WHAT REMAINS: one element class, not the seven the census names, and no coverage of the cancelled RECEIVER whose mailbox already holds a value. | 
| RV2-DEBT-246 | **THE VM SERVES A DUPLICATED TASK RESULT THROUGH `cloneValueComposite`, A `composite-box-marker` CARRIER.** D4b's VM half (`internal/vm/task_result.go`, `taskResultValue`) builds an asker that is not the last one an independent value by calling `cloneValueComposite`, the VM's clone of a value. The live carrier ratchet flags that call because the token belongs to the boxed-composite era (`composite-box-marker` retires to zero), so it is recorded as a `migration` carrier (`retired_by: vm-typed-duplication`) rather than an allowance: an allowance is for a row present at the base census, and this call is new. It is a genuine duplication and not a box -- the canonical value stays in the task's own slot and the last asker moves it out -- but the VM has no typed duplication entry of its own yet (storage model §9 gives VM aggregates the same generated structure walk as native lowering), so the call stays on the marker until one exists. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-28 -- RENAME.** Re-counted at `20755cf6`: all EIGHT live `composite-box-marker` findings are the same symbol, `cloneValueComposite` (three of them in comments) -- `internal/vm/clone_value.go` x3, `task_result.go` x2, `task_entitlement.go`, `eval.go`, `resource_internal_test.go`. `AllocStruct` and `AllocTag`, which the category description still names, no longer exist in the tree. So this is not a representation defect at all: the row itself already established that the duplication is genuine and not a box, and what remains is a NAME from the boxed-composite era being counted by a census that counts tokens. The ruling is to rename rather than to build a second duplication path for behaviour that is already correct. The repository's own precedent is the `Payload` -> `TaskState` rename, a census cleared without a representation change. | Closed 2026-09-01 (task result now duplicates from exact typed owner storage) | Epic 23b Wave F -- completed by D8 VM cutover | The VM's task owner duplicates a result through a typed per-value duplication entry that the VM's own `ValueOps` walk provides, the `cloneValueComposite` call in `task_result.go` is gone, and the migration row is removed from `legacy_carriers.json` in the same commit. |

RV2-DEBT-167 (closed 2026-08-26) releases the canonical RESULT the moment its entitlement cohort empties, and deliberately stops there: join waiters, scope children, `runUntilDone` and the timeout state all look tasks up by id, and deleting the entry would turn a leak into an `invalid task id` panic. | RV2-DEBT-248 | **`park_current`'S TOKEN-ABORT BRANCH SWEPT A JOIN REGISTRATION UNQUALIFIED, AND THE FIX HAS ONLY A RATE FOR EVIDENCE.** Found 2026-08-26 by D4b's contention census (four askers on four workers): one hang in roughly fifteen runs. The first abort branch of `park_current` refuses `remove_waiter_generation` for a key with no generation (join, scope, remote-reply entries sit at seq 0, and seq 0 is 'match any' -- RV2-DEBT-046); the second branch, taken when a wake token is found at the moment the park commits, swept unconditionally, and the requeued task can already be polling on another worker and have re-registered the same join key. The trace showed the stranded asker's two JOINADD entries removed by that branch and the completion drain collecting the other three askers only. Fixed in the same wave by gating the second branch on a nonzero generation exactly as the first; 60 of 60 runs afterwards. WHAT IS OWED: the proof is a rate, not an interleaving. §7 of the epic says a timeout-only or rate-only row does not prove the intended window; a deterministic sync point between `park_requeue_locked` and the removal, with a negative control that restores the sweep and strands the joiner at that point, is the closing evidence. **CLOSED 2026-09-01.** One after-requeue point alone cannot select the second branch: a token present on entry selects the first branch. The closing stand therefore uses `SP_PARK_AFTER_INITIAL_TOKEN_CHECK` to prove the first exchange returned zero and land a real `wake_task` while the joiner is still RUNNING, then `SP_PARK_ABORT_AFTER_REQUEUE` to hold the old poller after the second exchange consumed that token. A sibling carrier re-polls the READY task and publishes the fresh registration while the old poller is held. The positive reports `initial=2 abort=1 entries_before=2 entries_parked=2 attempts=2`, then receives kind 1 / value 42. The Rule 13 build restores only the unqualified seq-0 sweep and reports `target=done joiner=waiting entries=0 attempts=2` before failing with `debt248 negative control swept the fresh join registration`; timeout bounds are failure guards, never the success oracle. Both arms passed five consecutive deterministic runs, and the release sync-point gate proves no rendezvous symbol survives tag-off compilation. | Closed (2026-09-01) | Epic 23b Wave D, D4b (C3/C4 sync-point rows) | `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleDebt248SecondTokenAbortKeepsFreshJoinWaiter` and `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleDebt248SecondTokenAbortNegativeControl` are wired into `runtime-v2-lifecycle-check`; the exact focused pair passed at count 1 and count 5. `check_sync_points.sh` passed its enumerator/name/window census, release-symbol absence, and default-target guards. The worktree file-size gate against exact base `6bd9ae091aa3d8c54c81cbc0259c3ab6c253d6bb` also passed with the stand at 435 effective LOC. |

| RV2-DEBT-256 | **`ownsHeap` ANSWERS TRUE FOR EVERY VALUE COMPOSITE, SO SEM3197 REFUSES HANDING A `@copy` PAYLOAD OUT OF A BORROWED `compare`.** `@copy type Pair = { a: int, b: int }; type Held = Hold(Pair) | Nothing_; fn payload_of(h: &Held) -> Pair { return compare *h { Hold(p) => p; _ => ... }; }` is refused with SEM3197 `cannot hand `p` out of this arm` for `int`, `float` and `bool` fields alike (probes 2026-08-26): `rejectArmHandingOutBorrowedPayload` gates on `tc.ownsHeap(resultType)`, and `ownsHeap` (`ownership_axes.go`) says a Copy composite "owns its box" -- the v1 boxed representation, before the storage flips made a value composite inline. The float probe is the axis's own answer, not a residue: a `@copy` pair of floats owns two counted blocks, so SEM3197 keeps refusing it; whether the rule's escape (`payloadTakesItsOwnReference`, bare scalars only) should widen to a Copy composite whose extraction retains its fields is a rule question, open. | RV2-DEBT-258 | **A `for` THAT CONSUMES ITS ELEMENTS DOES NOT EMPTY THE CONTAINER'S SLOTS.** `for t in tasks { t.await() }` over `Task<int>[]` is the form the task tracker requires (`checkForInTaskConsumed`: the loop binding must be MOVED, or "task in container is not consumed in for-in loop"), and `for s in names { take(s) }` over `string[]` is accepted the same way; in both the loop reads the element's word and leaves it in the slot, so the container's drop glue meets an element that was already given away. Measured 2026-08-26 at `ed8144ee`: the string form is `Invalid read` + `Invalid free` under valgrind (a double free that predates every wave), and the task form -- invisible while `Task<T>` had no drop glue -- is `panic: async: invalid task owner shard` at teardown, which is why nine `TestMT*` rows and `TestRuntimeV2HeapAccountingConcurrentWorkersContract` went red the moment D4b made the handle droppable (`rt_task_handle_drop(NULL)` for a slot that WAS emptied is guarded separately: `TestRuntimeV2TaskHandleDropTreatsAnEmptySlotAsNothing`). The owner's 2026-08-13 ruling (RV2-DEBT-212) closed write-through; it did not say what a consuming iteration is. Two consistent answers: (a) a `for` whose binding is moved is a consuming iteration -- the element leaves its slot (HIR/MIR move-out, slot emptied, the container itself moved by the loop so later reads are refused by move tracking); (b) sema refuses moving out of a loop binding, and containers of affine elements are drained by the `while len(c) > 0 { let t = c.pop(); ... }` form the tracker already accepts (`taskContainerDrainLoop`). The nine MT programs and the heap contract used the `for` form. **Owner ruled 2026-08-26: variant (b) -- a `for` READS.** Sema refuses a move out of the loop binding, whole or by field, with SEM3205 (`cannot move out of `t`: a `for` loop binding only reads the element`, help names the drain and, for an ordinary clonable element, the copy); the task tracker no longer demands that move (`checkForInTaskConsumed` is gone: a `for` over a pending container leaves it pending, and the scope-exit SEM3107 now notes the loop that read it and names the drain); `for x in own xs` -- the spelling a consuming loop would have, parsed and silently ignored until now -- is refused with SEM3206 (`a consuming `for` is not a feature yet`). Goldens: `sema/invalid/ownership/for_in_moves_out_of_binding.sg`, `sema/invalid/ownership/for_in_own_iterable.sg`, `sema/invalid/concurrency/task_container_for_in_is_not_a_drain.sg`, valid twin `sema/valid/ownership/for_in_reads_then_pop_drains.sg`; unit rows `TestForInDoesNotConsumeItsElements` / `TestForInRefusalsNameTheDrain` (red on revert: `expected [SEM3205], got clean`). The drain form the tracker accepts is `while xs.__len() > 0:uint { let t = xs.pop().safe(); ... }` (`taskContainerDrainLoop` recognises the `__len()` MEMBER call, not the free `len(xs)`); the nine MT programs, `testdata/mt/mt_correctness_channels.sg`, the heap contract, `showcases/async/02_fanout_fanin`, `testdata/llvm_parity/fs_dir_smoke.sg` and `stdlib/fs/fs.sg` (`walkdir_collect` pushed the loop's copy of an entry `sorted` still owned) are rewritten to it; the reproducer runs unskipped as `TestRuntimeV2TaskHandleArrayDrainedByPopTearsDownClean`. **Owner's note for later:** think about a more concise form or sugar such as `await_all` over a container of tasks ("not the main thing, as long as the language can await out of arrays"); `for x in own xs` is a separate feature, not part of this row. **Addendum 2026-08-26 (owner, commit 3c6f4746):** `print` takes its string by BORROW (`print(s: &string, end: string = "\n")` in core/base.sg, `write_str(s: &string)` in stdlib/term), so `for name in names { print(name) }` is a read, `print(s); print(s);` reads once-owned storage twice, a literal or a temporary is autoreffed and dropped after the call, and the lane's `print(clone(name))` in `testdata/llvm_parity/fs_dir_smoke.sg` is gone (5dc89de3); `print(42)` stays SEM3015 (`expected &string, got int`) because 28678e9c answers a borrowed parameter's TYPE before the argument's PLACE. | RV2-DEBT-259 | **`rt_channel_free` DOES NOT FOLLOW THE TEARDOWN ORDER §7 PRESCRIBES.** `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md` §7 ("Channel lifetime"): "under the owner lock mark the object dying, detach its initialized slots, invalidate generations; release the lock; run `drop_in_place` on the detached values; free the storage." Read against `rt_async_channel.c:162–` while landing D3b C0 (2026-08-26): the function takes no lock at all and refuses to run under one (`:169`), carries no dying mark (`closed` is `close`, and close is not destroy), invalidates generations only slot by slot inside `rt_typed_fifo_drain`/`rt_park_pool_drain`, and detaches and drops one slot at a time (`rt_typed_fifo.c:339–342`) rather than detaching all, releasing, then dropping. It is safe today by construction -- the last handle and the last pin retire before anyone can reach the object, and `reclaiming` (an `atomic_exchange`) settles who reclaims -- which is a different argument from the one the document makes. Either the document's order is implemented (a dying mark under the owner lock, all slots detached before any drop) or §7 is rewritten to the refcount argument; a lane must not pick. | Open -- owner or model decision | Epic 23b Wave D, D3b (channel lifetime) | `rt_channel_free` and §7 say the same thing, with a sync-point row that observes the detach-then-drop boundary (or its absence) and a negative control. |

(5) `TestOwnsHeapLegsAgreeOverHandlesAndInstantiations` asks the two legs over a `core` snippet with `Task<int>`, `Task<string>`, `Channel<int>`, a generic `@copy` union and a generic `@copy` tuple alias — and found the two COPY authorities do part on the alias (`Result.IsCopyType(Pair<int>)` = false, the interner's bit = true; the axis still agrees because a composite never consults its own bit). A `break` leaving an INNER loop nested in the drain was charged to the drain, because the tracker's stack held drain loops only and could not count the loops in between (`loopNestingDepth` reads the loop drop marks now; `continue` is recorded nowhere, `return` still marks every enclosing drain). | RV2-DEBT-261 | **A `@failfast` BLOCK COULD RESOLVE `Success` AFTER A CHILD WAS CANCELLED.** Measured by the lead 2026-08-26 (pinned to CPUs 0,2): `TestMTStructuredConcurrency` exit 12 twice and exit 13 once in six runs at `60287ed8`, one in six at `91c2778e`, three of three green unpinned. `docs/LANGUAGE.md` (`@failfast`): "The scope then completes as `Cancelled()`." `rt_scope_join_all` (`rt_async_scope.c:208–255` at `91c2778e`) took its two answers apart: the first snapshot read `failfast_triggered` and `active_children` under the pinned lock (`:219–224`), the register-then-verify re-check read `active_children` alone (`:246`, `scope_active_locked`) and returned `true` with the flag from the first read -- while `scope_on_child_done` (`:352–366`) sets the flag and retires the cancelled child from the count in ONE critical section. A cancelled completion landing between the two reads therefore answered "drained, not fail-fast", and the generated tail (`insertScopeJoins`, `internal/mir/async_lowering_state_machine.go:220–276`) took the `Success` branch. Exit 12 is the first block's `return 10` (the fast child ran to completion before its `cancel()`, so the early return's `rt_scope_cancel_all` cancelled `slow`, whose completion raced the join's verify); exit 13 is the second block's tail `return 0` against two cancelled completions whose accounting waited behind the control lane (`scope_on_child_done` takes control for a fail-fast completion, after `TASK_DONE` is already visible at `rt_task_complete.c:252`). The VM lane (`internal/asyncrt/scope.go:126`) reads both in one step on one thread and has no window. Fix: `scope_join_snapshot_locked` reads both under one lock at the verify too; `SP_SCOPE_FAILFAST_JOIN_BEFORE_VERIFY` holds the gap open, `RV2_DEBT_261_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` drops the flag from the verify, the stand `debt261-failfast-join-verify` (`runtime-v2-lifecycle-check`, proof + negative control) and the program row `TestRuntimeV2FailfastJoinAnswersCancelled` (both lanes, `SURGE_THREADS=1/4`, eight rounds of both blocks) pin it. **MEASURED OUT 2026-08-28: 0 red in 800 runs.** The gate's own cancellation line, run 200 times and then 600 times on the dedicated machine at `cf20c74d`/`4db2f120`, produced no failure. The residue this row was chasing was roughly half a percent; at that rate zero in 800 has probability 0.995^800 ~ 1.8%, so the symptom is either gone or below about 0.37% at 95% confidence. The entry condition for the fourth-window lane -- a red under the gate -- is NOT met, and the lane is off the schedule until an instrument produces one. Recorded as a measurement, not a fix: nothing was changed to make this happen, and the three windows found behind this symptom earlier were each real. | Open -- the symptom is closed ON THE WORKING MACHINE and not on the dedicated one. At `b07851d1` (263 in): 0 of 20 and 0 of 20, 0 vacuous. On `surge-bm` (32 logical CPUs, cores 8-15 and 24-31 isolated, governor performance) the same two rows RUN ALONE at `d4d1b3eb` are also 0 of 20 -- but `make runtime-v2-lifecycle-check` under `taskset -c 8-15`, which runs them beside the rest of the gate, reported `TestRuntimeV2FailfastJoinAnswersCancelled/llvm/threads-4: exit=12 -- the first @failfast block (fast cancelled, slow cancelled by fail-fast) resolved Success`. | RV2-DEBT-263 | **A CANCELLED TASK COULD COMMIT `Success`, SO ITS `@failfast` SCOPE NEVER FIRED.** The second window behind RV2-DEBT-261's symptom, independent of it: with 261's join fix integrated the lead still measured `TestMTStructuredConcurrency` exit 12/13 in 7 of 20 pinned runs. Cancellation was observed ONLY at suspension points whose target was not already `TASK_DONE` -- `rt_task_poll` (`rt_async_task.c:247`), reached only after the `TASK_DONE` fast path above it (`:211–235`) has declined to answer, and `rt_async_yield` (`rt_async_poll.c:317`). That fast path answers from the TARGET and never consults the awaiter's own flag, so a body whose every remaining await resolves from an already-DONE child has NO suspension left to see its own cancel at: `let _ = spin(200).await(); return 1;` reads its cancelled child from the fast path, discards it, and returns. `rt_async_return` (`rt_async_poll.c:342–370`) then published `POLL_DONE_SUCCESS` unconditionally and `mark_done` (`rt_task_complete.c:234–280`) committed the kind it was handed, while fail-fast keys solely on the child's committed `result_kind` (`scope_on_child_done`, `rt_async_scope.c:322–353`). Result: `fast.cancel()` landing after the last check made `fast` answer `Success`, the early `return 10` cancelled `slow` through `rt_scope_cancel_all`, and `slow` escaped the same way -- so no child ever committed `Cancelled`, fail-fast never fired, and the block resolved `Success` (exit 12; exit 13 is the same escape reached by both children of the second block). The storage model already ruled the answer: `cancel` through a live handle is task-global and "before committed success it requests cancellation observed by every awaited entitlement; after success is committed it does not revoke already available independent results" (`23-storage-model-and-typed-carrier-abi.md:477–480`). Fix: the kind is decided at the COMMIT in `mark_done`, and the decision is ONE compare-and-swap per side on ONE word. `task->cancelled` is a three-state gate -- `OPEN`/`REQUESTED`/`SEALED` -- with `cancel_task` moving it `OPEN -> REQUESTED` and `mark_done` moving it `OPEN -> SEALED`; read-modify-writes on one atomic object are totally ordered by that object's modification order and each CAS reads the value written by the modification immediately before it, so whichever appears first wins and the other's CAS fails. "Both believed they won" has no execution. **The lane's FIRST shape of this fix claimed a linearization it did not have**, recorded here so it is not repeated: it paired a store-then-load on the cancel side with a LOAD-then-store on the completion side and called the bad pair a cycle in the seq-cst total order, which it is not -- `L(cancelled)=OPEN, S(cancelled), L(status)=RUNNING, S(status=DONE)` is simply the real-time order. It left the whole of `mark_done` inside the residual window, including a mutex acquisition in `release_matching_leases` (`rt_remote_task_lease.c`), and its gate ran green only because `mark_done_needs_control` takes control whenever `done_waiters > 0` and the external await in `main` holds that above zero for the whole program -- the control lock, i.e. the rejected alternative, arriving implicitly and unguaranteed. That alternative (the task's owner-shard lock around both sides) is still rejected on cost: a lock acquire and release on EVERY completion, on the steady request path Epic 7 took off the control lane. `REQUESTED` is the only state meaning "a cancel is outstanding", so `task_cancelled_load`'s readers keep their meaning. Second half: committing `Cancelled` with a value still in the slot was a state that did not exist before this lane, and `rt_remote_task_pin_result` named that slot in a capability whose holder moves the value out unconditionally (`finish_retry`, `rt_remote_task_api.c`) while the generated `Cancelled` arm never reads the storage it lands in (`emit_crossing_far_task.go`) -- a leak, missed by the lane's first audit. `rt_task_result_refuse` now empties the slot at the commit, before anything downstream can name it: `rt_value_cell_hand_off` marks the cell "already taken" (a state every reader already answers) and the value goes to the lane's deferred release, because its drop is generated code and may not run under a scheduler lock (rule 8 P2); an inline value pins its task so the drain cannot outlive the bytes. `pin_result` asks the kind first as a fail-closed second line. VM parity: `Executor.commitKind` decides and `MarkDone` RETURNS the payload it refuses (`internal/asyncrt/task_complete.go`), which `runReadyOne` destroys (`internal/vm/async_runtime.go`) -- the executor is generic over its payload, so handing it back is the only spelling that cannot lose it, and both lanes now destroy at the same MOMENT. `SP_ASYNC_RETURN_BEFORE_SUCCESS_COMMIT` holds the pre-commit window, `SP_MARKDONE_AFTER_SEAL_BEFORE_DONE` the residual one. **SAME MEASUREMENT, 2026-08-28: 0 red in 800 runs of the gate's own line.** See RV2-DEBT-261's note for the arithmetic. Neither row has an instrument producing a failure today. | Open -- the owed runs are delivered and one is red. On the working machine: 0 red in 20 pinned rounds for both rows, 0 vacuous (RV2-DEBT-261 carries the literal command and the before reading); `./check_sync_points.sh`, `make runtime-v2-syncpoint-check`, `make runtime-v2-lifecycle-check` (with its TSan rows), `make runtime-v2-panic-surface-check`, `make runtime-v2-file-size-check EPIC_BASE=691ae487` (29 files, 0 violations), `make check` on every commit through the hook, `make golden-check`, `make runtime-v2-heap-check` and `make runtime-v2-carrier-sanitizer-check` all green. `make runtime-v2-carrier-check` is red and was red on `691ae487` before this lane, so it is not this lane's. On `surge-bm` the lifecycle gate is RED at `TestRuntimeV2FailfastJoinAnswersCancelled/llvm/threads-4` with exit 12, so the commit boundary is not the last window: a third one is open, and RV2-DEBT-265 (an out-parameter left unwritten on two returns, read through an uninitialised `alloca i1`, whose wrong-default reading is exactly `Success`) is the first candidate. The FAR behavioural witness stays owed and is RV2-DEBT-267 Measured on `surge-bm` 2026-08-27 with the command the CI gate runs, repeated 200 times a side, a run counted VACUOUS when either row is missing or skipped: `SURGE_STDLIB=$(pwd) SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 taskset -c 8-15 go test ./internal/vm -run '^TestRuntimeV2(FailfastJoinAnswersCancelled|TimeoutTargetAnswersCancelledToEveryHandle)$' -count=1 -parallel=1 -p=1 -v --timeout 300s'. At `d4d1b3eb` (before RV2-DEBT-265): 3 red of 200, 0 vacuous -- two `SURGE_THREADS=4: exit=12` on the fail-fast row and one `exit=1` that is a separate defect (RV2-DEBT-291). At `082ffae9` (after 265): 1 red of 200, 0 vacuous, `exit=12` again. Two of 200 against one of 200 is not a difference this instrument can resolve, so RV2-DEBT-265 is NOT the window: the residue is alive at roughly half a percent, `llvm` only, `SURGE_THREADS=4` only, and the VM lane is green in every one of the 400 runs | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle) | Stand `debt263-cancel-commit-boundary` green at `SURGE_SHARDS=1`, `SURGE_THREADS=2/4/8` with "debt263 cancelled in window: cancelled=1 done=0 done_waiters=0" and "child kind=2 bits=0"; its negative control exits 1 with "child kind=1 bits=7". That row is also the proof the window is control-free: it cancels while a worker is held inside `mark_done`, and `rt_task_cancel` takes the control lock, so it would strand rather than pass if `mark_done` held it there. `TestRuntimeV2TimeoutTargetAnswersCancelledToEveryHandle`: exit 20 in 9 of 10 runs with the boundary made inert (5/5 at `SURGE_THREADS=1`, 4/5 at 4), 10 of 10 green with it. `TestRuntimeV2CancelledOwnedResultValgrindZero` (a composite of two strings, wider than the cell's inline run): strict zero on the fixed tree; "definitely lost: 200 bytes in 8 blocks" with the refusal handing off and never releasing; "Invalid free()", 33 errors from 7 contexts, with `rt_value_cell_hand_off` leaving the cell INITIALIZED. `TestMarkDoneCommitsCancelledForACancelledTask` red without the VM half ("a cancelled task committed kind 0, want 1"). | RV2-DEBT-264 | **THE TWO COPY AUTHORITIES PART OVER A GENERIC `@copy` TUPLE ALIAS.** `@copy type Pair<T> = (T, T);` instantiated as `Pair<int>` in a `core` snippet is Copy to one authority and not Copy to the other. Measured 2026-08-27 (throwaway probe over `TestOwnsHeapLegsAgreeOverHandlesAndInstantiations`'s snippet, printing every interner row): `Pair<int>` (kind alias) answers `in.IsCopy = true`, `Result.IsCopyType = false`, `OwnsHeap = false`; the tuple it names, `(int, int)`, answers `in.IsCopy = false`. The mechanism is in `Result.IsCopyType` (`internal/sema/copy_query.go:10`): it resolves the alias FIRST (`resolveAlias`) and then asks the resolved tuple, and the `@copy` attribute was recorded on the alias the author wrote it on, not on the structural type the alias names -- so resolving throws the bit away. A generic `@copy` UNION does not diverge (`Opt<int>`: both authorities `true`), because an instantiated union carries the bit on the union type itself; only an alias to a structural type loses it. **The ownership axis is not wrong today.** `ownsHeapIn` walks the members and never consults a composite's own Copy bit, so both legs answer `false` for `Pair<int>` and RV2-DEBT-256's "one axis, one answer" holds. What is unproven is everything else that asks `Result.IsCopyType`: implicit-copy decisions in `internal/hir/borrow_build.go:358`, `internal/mir/lower_type_queries.go:21` and `async_lowering_helpers.go:192`, the crossing-capture rules (`on_crossing_capture.go:218`, `:250`), the capability classifier (`capability_classifier.go:150`) and the clone advice (`clone_advice_capability.go:39`) -- each of which will treat `Pair<int>` as non-Copy while the interner, and therefore any check that asks it directly, treats it as Copy. No miscompile has been produced from this yet; the divergence was found by the agreement test, not by a failure, and finding out which side is right is a model question (is `@copy` on an alias a property of the alias or of what it names?) rather than a lane's. | Open | Epic 23b Wave F (ownership axes / Copy authorities); model question for the owner | The two authorities answer the same for every type an interner holds, or one of them is deleted in favour of the other. Pinned by a test that asks BOTH over a generic `@copy` tuple alias, a generic `@copy` union and a generic `@copy` struct, and that is red on today's tree with `Pair<int>: in.IsCopy=true, Result.IsCopyType=false`. |
| RV2-DEBT-265 | **`rt_scope_join_all` CAN ANSWER "DRAINED" WITHOUT SAYING WHETHER FAIL-FAST FIRED, AND THE CALLER READS UNDEFINED MEMORY.** Two early exits return `true` without writing `*failfast`: no executor (`rt_async_scope.c:220–222`) and a scope the slot no longer names (`:225–227`). The LLVM caller reserves the flag with `alloca i1` and never initializes it (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_async_join_all.go:27`), then loads it in the ready block to choose the block's answer (`:36`). So on either path the generated tail of a `@failfast` block branches on undefined memory -- it is UB, not merely a wrong default, and the wrong-default reading of it (`Success`) is exactly the answer RV2-DEBT-261 and RV2-DEBT-263 exist to prevent. Found by the second lens while deriving RV2-DEBT-263; not the cause of that symptom (a live scope takes neither exit). Fix shape: the runtime writes both out-parameters on every return, and the emitter stores a defined value into the `alloca` before the call so the two answers cannot disagree about who initializes it. | Open -- the undefined behaviour is removed on both ends, the symptom it was named for is not yet measured out. `rt_scope_join_all` writes `*pending` and `*failfast` before any path can return, so the two early exits answer "drained, nothing pending, no fail-fast" instead of leaving the caller's frame; `emitInstrJoinAll` stores `i64 0` and `i1 false` into the two slots before the call, so the ready block can never load an `alloca` that was never written and the two ends cannot disagree about who initialises them. `TestEmitJoinAllWritesItsFlagSlotBeforeTheCall` pins the emitter half and is red on the revert ("%t24 is read by the ready block but never written before the call to rt_scope_join_all"); the runtime half is by construction, both stores standing before the first return rather than on each path. What is NOT established is whether this was the third window behind RV2-DEBT-261/263 -- that is the gate-under-load reading on `surge-bm`, taken before and after Measured on `surge-bm` 2026-08-27 with the command the CI gate runs, repeated 200 times a side, a run counted VACUOUS when either row is missing or skipped: `SURGE_STDLIB=$(pwd) SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 taskset -c 8-15 go test ./internal/vm -run '^TestRuntimeV2(FailfastJoinAnswersCancelled|TimeoutTargetAnswersCancelledToEveryHandle)$' -count=1 -parallel=1 -p=1 -v --timeout 300s'. At `d4d1b3eb` (before RV2-DEBT-265): 3 red of 200, 0 vacuous -- two `SURGE_THREADS=4: exit=12` on the fail-fast row and one `exit=1` that is a separate defect (RV2-DEBT-291). At `082ffae9` (after 265): 1 red of 200, 0 vacuous, `exit=12` again. Two of 200 against one of 200 is not a difference this instrument can resolve, so RV2-DEBT-265 is NOT the window: the residue is alive at roughly half a percent, `llvm` only, `SURGE_THREADS=4` only, and the VM lane is green in every one of the 400 runs | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle) | A `@failfast` block whose scope handle no longer resolves answers deterministically, and the emitted IR has no load of an uninitialized `alloca` (an IR-shape test over `emitInstrJoinAll`). |
| RV2-DEBT-266 | **THE CANCEL-PENDING SCOPE TEARDOWN READS AND FREES A SCOPE UNDER THE CONTROL LANE ALONE, WHILE CHILD-DONE MUTATES IT UNDER THE PINNED SHARD LOCK.** `poll_task`'s `cancel_pending` branch (`rt_async_poll.c:116–140`) takes `rt_control_lock` and then reads `scope->active_children` and, at zero, calls `scope_exit_locked` -- which clears the slot and frees the scope and its `children` array -- holding NO shard lock. The steady same-owner `scope_on_child_done` is CONTROL-FREE and mutates `scope->children`, `active_children` and `failfast_triggered` under the scope's pinned owner shard lock (`rt_async_scope.c:324–338`), which is why `rt_scope_exit` (`:280–289`) and `apply_poll_outcome`'s cancelled teardown (`rt_task_complete.c`) both take that lock around the same call. Control does not exclude the pinned lock, so the read can be torn against a concurrent retire and the free can race a live child-done -- a use-after-free on owner-cancel teardown. The comment on `scope_exit_locked` (`rt_async_scope.c:291–296`) names only the two callers that DO hold the pinned lock and describes control as "the control lane over that shard", which has not been true since child-done went control-free. Found by the second lens while deriving RV2-DEBT-263; it yields `Cancelled` rather than `Success`, so it is not that symptom. Fix shape: take the pinned shard lock around the read and the exit here as the other two callers do, and correct the comment. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle) | The `cancel_pending` branch holds the scope's pinned owner shard lock across the `active_children` read and `scope_exit_locked`; TSan over an owner-cancel teardown with a concurrent child completion reports no race. Ledger reconciliation 2026-08-27: this row and RV2-DEBT-280 (site 1) and RV2-DEBT-283 (the free under a lock the writers do not hold) name ONE defect, found twice -- here while deriving RV2-DEBT-263, and again by the multi-carrier planes review, which had not seen this row because it lived only on the lane. The three are kept because each states a different half of the evidence, and whichever lane serializes the cancel-pending teardown closes all three together; no lane may claim one of them alone. |
| RV2-DEBT-267 | **NO FAR/REMOTE BEHAVIOURAL ROW COVERS A CANCELLED PRODUCER'S REPLY.** RV2-DEBT-263 found the far reply path naming a result slot for a task that answers `Cancelled` (`rt_remote_task_pin_result`, `rt_remote_task_result.c`), whose holder then moves the value into storage the generated `Cancelled` arm never reads (`finish_retry`, `rt_remote_task_api.c`; `emit_crossing_far_task.go`). Two things close it now -- the slot is emptied at the commit (`rt_task_result_refuse`) and `pin_result` asks the kind first -- and a static shape row pins both (`TestRuntimeV2LifecycleStaticFarReplyNamesResultOnlyForSuccess`). What is missing is a run: a `spawn on distributed` producer that reaches its `return` with an owning value and is cancelled after its last suspension, awaited across the crossing, under valgrind at strict zero. The lane that found the defect could not build one -- a far body is a single call expression (`ret f(own j)`), so "produced, then cancelled" needs a shape the crossing rows do not have today -- and shipped the guards without the witness rather than claiming one. Until it exists the far half of RV2-DEBT-263 is pinned by construction and by shape, not by a run. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle) | A far task that produces an owning value and is cancelled after its last suspension answers `Cancelled` to its awaiter, and valgrind reports definitely and indirectly lost 0 at one shard and at four. |
| RV2-DEBT-268 | **AN ARMED DEADLINE PUBLISHES NOTHING, AND THE CARRIER'S GROUP WAIT CARRIES NO DEADLINE.** `poll_sleep_task` (`runtime/native/rt_async_poll.c:38-47`, read at `810090a1`) takes the owner shard lock, calls `rt_sleep_store_add`, and releases it: no notification, no wake, no credit, whether or not the new deadline becomes the shard's minimum. `wake_pending` is bumped only on the task-publication path (`rt_ready_queue.c:226-231`). The carrier's own wait is `while (scheduler->wake_pending == 0 && !ex->shutdown) pthread_cond_wait(&shard->worker_cv, &shard->lock)` (`rt_worker_turn.c:182-184`) -- a plain `pthread_cond_wait`, no timeout, no deadline in the predicate -- so a carrier that parks with an armed deadline cannot serve it. Serving is delegated to two third parties: the io lane's `advance_time_to_next_timer` (`rt_worker_turn.c:255-271`), gated on `rt_sched_idle_sample_locked`, which requires EVERY shard idle, and `tick_virtual` (`rt_async_state.c:453-470`), which runs only when some task yields. With `SURGE_SHARDS>1` a deadline armed on a quiet shard is therefore not served while any other shard is busy, and its lateness has no bound anyone can state. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:197-202`: "Arming is a publication: a thread that arms a deadline becoming the shard's new minimum publishes a group notification before leaving the arming critical section, and a thread entering a wait reads that minimum under the same indivisible protocol that governs its wait transition." | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Timers + §10 (Epic 23b Wave D, scheduler) | The arm publishes a group notification when it lowers the shard minimum and counts a suppression when it does not (`timer_arm_notifications` / `_suppressed`); the group wait's predicate observes the shard minimum indivisibly with the wait transition; `timer_service_lateness` is reported as a p50/p99/max distribution against a declared ceiling, and a stand shows a deadline armed just before a peer parks being served without a third party. |
| RV2-DEBT-269 | **SHUTDOWN NEITHER FIRES NOR CANCELS ANY ARMED DEADLINE.** `rt_executor_request_shutdown` (`runtime/native/rt_shutdown.c:42-70`) sets `ex->shutdown`, fails pending remote spawns, releases far tasks, drains every shard's shutdown net waiters, wakes pollers, wakes transport, nudges io and broadcasts every condvar -- and touches no `sleep_store` on any shard (`grep sleep_store runtime/native/rt_shutdown.c` is empty; the only mutators are `rt_async_poll.c:45`, `rt_task_complete.c:225` and `rt_sleep_fire_due_on_shard`). Both loops that could fire a due deadline exit on `ex->shutdown` first (`rt_worker_turn.c:133`, `:250`), so an armed deadline at shutdown is simply abandoned. The clock code already assumes the opposite: `clock_wall_bound_active` (`rt_async_sleep.c:149-153`) exempts shutdown with the comment "teardown wants every pending timer to fire at once, and nothing is waiting for the wall then" -- a promise nothing keeps. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:211-212`: "A closing group fires or cancels every armed deadline before the last thread serving its shards leaves." | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Timers (Epic 23b Wave D, lifecycle) | Shutdown walks every shard's `sleep_store` and fires or cancels each armed deadline before the last serving thread leaves, an undrained store is recorded with its shard id and entry count, and a lifecycle stand arms a deadline that outlives its program and shows it resolved rather than abandoned (red on revert). |
| RV2-DEBT-270 | **CARRIER AFFINITY DOES NOT EXIST IN THE SCHEDULER: NOTHING RECORDS IT, NOTHING PUBLISHES BY IT, AND THE STEAL PREDICATE CANNOT TEST IT.** `rt_task_can_steal_from_shard` (`runtime/native/rt_scheduler_placement.c:117-125`) answers 1 for every task unless `placement_class == TASK_PLACEMENT_CONNECTION`, and even then compares only `owner_shard_id == shard_id`; the steal filter in `pop_task_from_deque` (`rt_ready_queue.c:152-165`) calls nothing else. With one shard -- the default topology -- the predicate is vacuously true for every task, so a peer carrier may steal anything. The publication side agrees: `wake_task` (`rt_task_park.c:165-167`) -> `ready_push_task_locked` (`rt_ready_queue.c:183-236`) pushes onto `current_local_queue(ex, scheduler)`, i.e. THE FIRING THREAD'S private deque, with no test of who may run the task, under the file's own comment at `:196` -- "No last-worker affinity is tracked; wake/spawn follows the current thread." The same function then suppresses the credit for exactly that push: `signal_ready_now = signal_ready && local->len > 1` (`:206-207`), so a single continuation lands in a stealable private deque and no peer is credited. `grep -i 'affinity\|affine' runtime/native/*.c *.h` returns that one comment and nothing else. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:700-710`: "A child that captures `&T` or `&mut T` is carrier-affine from its creation to its completion ... keeping the affinity at run time is the scheduler's part, whose steal and handoff predicates refuse that task on any carrier other than its parent's, exactly as placement refuses a connection task on a non-owner shard, and trace every refusal", and `:208-209`: "a carrier-affine sleeper may be elided only by its own carrier". A borrow-capturing child running on a peer carrier is a soundness hole, not a latency one, and the default runtime is `1 shard × N carriers`. | Open -- the fix's shape is owner question Р4 (publish-to-inject / per-class queues / filter-at-selection) | Runtime V2 §9 + §10 (Epic 23b Wave D, scheduler) | A task carries its carrier affinity, the steal and handoff predicates refuse it on any other carrier and trace every refusal, a fired or woken affine task is published where its own carrier reaches it with an eligible credit (no credit-free push into a stealable queue), and a stand shows a `&mut`-capturing child never observed on a peer carrier with a negative control that reproduces the crossing. |
| RV2-DEBT-271 | **A BATCH OF DUE SLEEPERS IS PUBLISHED IN DEADLINE ORDER AND SERVED IN REVERSE.** `rt_sleep_fire_due_on_shard` (`runtime/native/rt_async_sleep.c`, `rt_sleep_store_pop_due` at `:82-93`) pops due entries in nondecreasing `(deadline, task_id)` order and calls `wake_task` on each in that order. `ready_push_task_locked` puts each into the local deque with `deque_push_tail` (`rt_ready_queue.c:204-205`) under the comment "Local queues are popped from the tail, so tail insertion is the local priority path", and `worker_next_ready` pops the local queue LIFO (`pop_task_from_deque(..., lifo=1, ...)` at `rt_ready_queue.c:376`, `:391`, `:456`). So a batch of `k` due sleepers is served newest-deadline-first, exactly inverting the order it was popped in, and a peer that steals from the same deque pops the OTHER end (`lifo=0`), so two carriers of one shard observe two different orders for one batch. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:209-211`: "The batch is served in nondecreasing deadline order by the thread that pops it, and published so a peer observes that order at selection." | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Timers (Epic 23b Wave D, scheduler) | A due batch is served in nondecreasing deadline order by the thread that popped it and a peer observes that order at selection; `timer_batch_out_of_order_services` is reported and a stand with three deadlines 1 ms apart records the service order, red on revert. |
| RV2-DEBT-272 | **THE FD REGISTRY IS READ FROM THREE LANES THAT DO NOT HOLD ITS LANE, AGAINST A WRITER THAT REALLOCATES IT.** The registry's own lane rule is written in the tree (`runtime/native/rt_async_internal.h:466-472`: "Shard lane (rt_shard.lock): ... net poll state / fd registry"), and the growth path proves it matters: `rt_fd_registry_ensure_cap` (`rt_fd_registry.c:78-106`) `rt_realloc`s the row array and rewrites `registry->entries` at `:103`, `fd_registry_remove_at` (`:164-175`) swap-removes and decrements `registry->len` -- and the only lock held over the live registration path is the OWNER SHARD lock (`rt_async_waiter.c:567-583`: `rt_shard_lock(owner_shard)` ... `rt_fd_registry_attach_net_interest(&owner_shard->fd_registry, key)`), never the control lock. Three readers walk `registry->len`/`registry->entries` without that lock: `rt_io_main` calls `rt_net_has_waiters_on_shard(ex, 0)` holding only the control lock (`rt_worker_turn.c:257`, reader body at `rt_net_poller.c:152-165`); `next_ready` calls it with no shard lock at all (`rt_async_state.c:522`, `:553`); and `rt_net_owner_shard_for_key` (`rt_async_waiter.c:199-212`) walks EVERY shard's registry lock-free. `rt_net_has_waiters_any_shard` (`rt_net_poller.c:167-187`) takes the shard lock for the same question and says why in its comment, so the tree already contains both answers. This is a live data race on a pointer that is reallocated and an index that is decremented. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:219-222`: "Every reader reads the registry under that lane -- snapshot, has-waiters predicate, and idle sample alike". | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Readiness and the fd registry + §2 (Epic 23b Wave D, net) | Every registry read -- has-waiters predicate, owner probe, snapshot and idle sample -- runs under the owning shard's lane, a TSan stand registers and unregisters interests on one shard while another lane samples, and the stand is red on a negative control that restores an unlocked read. |
| RV2-DEBT-273 | **THE POLL LEASE IS CLAIMED, RELEASED AND OBSERVED OFF THE LANE, AND ITS RELEASE SIGNALS A CONDVAR NO REFUSED CLAIMANT WAITS ON.** `rt_net_begin_poll_on_shard` (`runtime/native/rt_net_poller.c:190-204`) takes and releases the shard lock INSIDE itself, so the worker turn observes the lease with the lock released (`rt_worker_turn.c:147` unlocks, `:148` claims) and takes the lock again only at `:150-161`; the transition to waiting happens later still, at `:170-184`. Claim observation and wait transition are therefore separated by two lock releases. Two other readers skip the lane entirely: `next_ready` reads `shard->net_polling` as a plain non-atomic field with no lock at `rt_async_state.c:535` and `:552`. And the release path (`rt_net_poll_waiters_owned_on_shard`, `rt_net_poller.c:207-217`) clears `net_polling` and does `pthread_cond_broadcast(&shard->poller_cv)` -- but a carrier whose claim was REFUSED falls through to `pthread_cond_wait(&shard->worker_cv, ...)` (`rt_worker_turn.c:182-184`), a different condvar; `poller_cv` has only the io lane and the compat runner on it (`rt_io_wait_slice`, `rt_worker_turn.c:79-103`). So the release of the lease wakes nobody who was refused it. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:218-228`: "it enters by claiming the shard's poll lease and leaves by releasing it, and the lease is claimed, released, and observed under the lane guarding the registry rows ... a carrier seeing an open interest and a free lease claims it instead, that observation and its transition to waiting linearized under that lane." | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Readiness and the fd registry + §2 (Epic 23b Wave D, net) | The lease is claimed, released and observed under the shard lane in one critical section with the wait transition; `net_polling` is never read outside it; the release wakes the wake source a refused claimant actually waits on; `net_poll_lease_grants_by_shard` / `_refusals_by_shard` / `_hold_ns_max_by_shard` are reported, with refusals a hard zero in the target topology. |
| RV2-DEBT-274 | **THE READINESS WAIT HAS NO BUDGET: ONE PATH WAITS FOREVER, ANOTHER SPINS A THOUSAND SLICES A SECOND.** Two ends of the same missing rule. (1) `next_ready` (`runtime/native/rt_async_state.c:545-549`), the compatibility single-runner path, calls `rt_net_poll_waiters_owned_on_shard(ex, 0, -1)` -- an UNBOUNDED readiness wait; only the shutdown wake-pipe write can end it, and no group notification is in its wait set. (2) The worker turn does the opposite: `worker_net_poll_slice_ms = 1` (`rt_worker_turn.c:109`), and the loop at `:147-160` unlocks, claims the lease, polls for 1 ms, relocks, and `continue`s while `rt_net_has_waiters_on_shard` is true (`:158-159`), so a shard holding one idle connection never reaches its park and burns ~1000 poll slices per second per shard; `++ctx->net_tick % 61U == 0U` (`:129`) adds a zero-timeout pass on top even when work exists. Neither figure is traced, so neither is visible. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:230-233`: "The wait runs under the bounded, traced budget of Section 8, and a holder that neither completes readiness nor releases the lease within it is a contract failure, not a slow poll", and `:667-669`: "a poll that cannot be woken by a group notification is not a legal wait state for a carrier of that group." | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Readiness and the fd registry + §8 (Epic 23b Wave D, net) | Every readiness wait runs under one declared, traced budget with no `-1` caller; the wait observes the group wake source, the shard's inbound source and shutdown alongside readiness; `net_poll_slices_total_by_shard` on empty readiness is reported next to throughput and a spin row is refused rather than printed. |
| RV2-DEBT-275 | **THE TRANSPORT PARK STATE IS A SHARD AGGREGATE WHERE THE CONTRACT NAMES THE WORKER GROUP, ON BOTH SIDES.** Publisher side: `rt_transport_prepare_shard_park` (`runtime/native/rt_transport.c:353-393`) is called by whichever single carrier is about to sleep (`rt_worker_turn.c:170`) and stores `RT_TRANSPORT_SHARD_PARKED` for the WHOLE shard after re-checking only the inbound queue -- it consults neither the shard's other carriers nor the poll lease, so one sleeping carrier marks the shard parked while peers run and while a lease holder sits inside a readiness wait. Producer side: `rt_transport_enqueue_locked` (`rt_transport.c:266-302`) publishes the record, loads `park_state`, and when it does not read `PARKED` takes the branch at `:296-301` -- `transport_wake_elisions++` and NO wake, NO credit. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:649-656`: "`PARKED` may be published only after every carrier in the shard's worker group has completed the group no-work handshake (Section 10). One sleeping carrier may not mark the whole shard `PARKED`. Every transport delivery into such a shard is an external producer for its worker group: after publishing the inbound record it must issue an eligible group credit, whether the aggregate transport state reads `RUNNING` or `PARKED`", and `:236`: "No peer publishes `PARKED` for a shard whose lease is held." A busy carrier therefore makes a non-empty inbound queue look served while every peer sleeps -- the exact shape §8 names. | Open -- the review noted no lane owns transport; this row assigns it | Runtime V2 §1 Readiness and the fd registry + §8 (Epic 23b Wave D, transport) | `PARKED` is published only after the whole worker group has completed the no-work handshake and never while the shard's poll lease is held; every transport delivery issues an eligible group credit regardless of the aggregate state; `transport_wake_elisions` is reported per shard and a sync-point stand shows a delivery into a shard with one busy and three sleeping carriers reaching a carrier, red on a negative control that restores the `RUNNING` elision. |
| RV2-DEBT-276 | **A RENDEZVOUS THAT LOSES ITS CANDIDATE DESTROYS THE VALUE AND THEN RE-SENDS A MOVED-FROM SOURCE.** In `rt_channel_send_inner` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c`) the send stages its value into a channel-owned park slot (`channel_stage_locked`, `:221-241`, whose `rt_value_move_init_detached` at `:233` CONSUMES `src` -- the function's own comment at `:291-295` says reading `src` a second time "would give one heap value two owners"), clears `task->resume_slot` at `:324`, and hands the slot to the popped candidate. When the candidate turns out dead, the recovery at `:340-346` is: `if (!channel_stage_into_ring_locked(...)) { channel_end_park_locked(ch_shard, ch, &staged); } ... continue;`. `channel_end_park_locked` (`rt_channel_lane.h:493-505`) calls `rt_value_drop_in_place_detached` on the slot's value, so the refusal path DESTROYS the value -- and then `continue` re-enters the loop with `staged_live` false and `src` already emptied, so the next pass stages a husk. The foreign-delivery failure at `:360-367` has the identical shape. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:264-267`: "A send that loses that test has taken no position: it joins the buffer if the buffer will take it, otherwise wakes the receiver it popped, unacked, and parks holding its slot; destroying the value is never a legal recovery." Both a silent value loss and a use of moved-from storage, on the default topology. **Closed 2026-09-03 (closeout step D7).** When the popped candidate is dead, the staged value stays in its slot -- `task->resume_slot = staged` and the loop continues: the next pass meets the next receiver, joins the buffer, or parks holding the slot; `src` is never re-read. Both shapes (same-shard dead, foreign delivery failed) fixed in `runtime/native/rt_async_channel_send.c`; the buffer is still tried first. Counters `channel_rendezvous_recoveries_dead_receiver` and `channel_values_destroyed_in_recovery` on the trace; the latter's only writer is the mutant `RV2_DEBT_276_NEGATIVE_CONTROL`, which restores the drop. Stand row `dead-receiver` in `internal/vm/testdata/channel_close_wins_modes.c` (`TestRuntimeV2ChannelCloseWinsOrders`): the receiver dies inside the staging window, the sender parks holding its slot, a later `try_recv` reads 7277 -- before, it read 0, the husk. | Closed | Runtime V2 §1 Channel wait queues + §7 (Epic 23b Wave D, channels) | The refusal path wakes the popped receiver unacked and parks holding its slot instead of destroying it, no path re-reads `src` after a stage consumed it, `channel_values_destroyed_in_recovery` is a hard zero and `channel_rendezvous_recoveries_by_kind` is reported; a stand cancels the popped candidate inside the staging window and shows the value delivered, red on a negative control restoring the drop. |
| RV2-DEBT-277 | **THE CHANNEL'S SINGLE-TRANSFER CLAIM HAS NO RETRY BUDGET, NO EXHAUSTION PARK, NO WAKE ON RELEASE, AND NO COUNTER.** A refused claim on the buffer's single transfer returns the operation to selection and nothing else: send at `runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c:388-393` (`reserved == RT_SLOT_CONTROL_BUSY` -> unlock, `pending_key = waker_none()`, `return 0`) and recv at `:534-541` (same shape), plus the silent `return 0` inside `channel_stage_into_ring_locked` when `rt_typed_fifo_reserve_push_locked` refuses (`rt_channel_lane.h:514-521`). Nothing counts the refusal, nothing bounds the number of retries, and the operation never parks on the channel's own key -- so an unbounded spin is indistinguishable from progress in every reported figure. The release side matches: `channel_stage_into_ring_locked` wakes only a parked RECEIVER after `commit_push` (`rt_channel_lane.h:531` -> `channel_wake_parked_receiver_locked`, `:439-452`); `rt_typed_fifo_abandon_push_locked` and `rt_park_pool_commit_take_locked` wake nobody, so releasing the claim that refused an operation notifies no one. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:256-261`: "A claim is refused, never queued; the refused operation returns to selection as a publication like any other, counting against the public queue's service bound. The retry budget names its exhaustion: the operation parks on the channel's own key, the release of the claim that refused it wakes that key, and shutdown terminates the retry rather than let it re-enter selection." **Closed 2026-09-03 (closeout step D6), redesigned from the `codex/rv2-channel-retry` patch rather than transplanted.** Budget 8 per logical operation (`runtime/native/rt_channel_retry.h`, mutant `RV2_DEBT_277_RETRY_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` = 9); the cores answer 3 = REFUSED with a cause (`rt_channel_sync.c`, ring push / ring pop / park take) and every refusal site counts it; exhaustion parks on the channel's own retry key (`WAKER_CHAN_SEND_RETRY` / `WAKER_CHAN_RECV_RETRY`, `rt_waiter_key.c`), every claim release wakes that key (`rt_channel_claim_released_locked`: commit push/pop, commit take/deliver, abandon push, value-bearing park release), and `rt_channel_close` drains four keys. What the patch lacked: a select is refused on a different arm per poll, so the state keeps a prefix ring of up to seven `{channel, direction, cause}` and an exhausted select subscribes to EVERY remembered arm, register-then-verify under each owner lane (`rt_select_channel_retry.h`, `SP_CHANNEL_SELECT_REFUSED_BEFORE_RETRY_REGISTER`); a `default` arm wins immediately, refusal or not (`LANGUAGE.md`: with `default` the task never parks). Stand `internal/vm/testdata/channel_claim_retry*.c`, five tests `TestRuntimeV2ChannelClaimRetry*` on the waiter gate: before, an eighth refused send returned to selection with `pending_key = none` for ever (`channel_retry_budget_exhaustions` had no writer); after, `refusals=8 republications=7 exhaustions=1 max_retries=8 woke=1`, and twelve mutants (budget 9, no wake, close draining two keys, select identity by stack address, only the eighth arm registered, `default` gated on a clean scan, no recovery reset ×2, no park-release wake, mixed handoff FIFO ×2, verify without the owner-lane predicate) each read red on their named line. | RV2-DEBT-284 | **OWNER CELLS SHARE CACHE LINES AND NOTHING PADS THEM OR ASSERTS THAT THEY ARE PADDED.** `rt_heap_accounting_cell` is four `_Atomic uint64_t` = 32 bytes with no alignment attribute (`runtime/native/rt_heap_accounting.h:15-20`), and `allocate_cells` (`rt_heap_accounting.c:14-28`) `calloc`s the worker array at that stride, so two carriers' cells share one 64-byte line; `main_cell` and `io_cell` are adjacent struct members (`rt_heap_accounting.h:29-30`) and `cold_cell` is a bare file-scope object next to `tls_heap_accounting_cell` (`rt_heap_accounting.c:7-8`). There is not one `alignas` or `__attribute__((aligned` anywhere in `runtime/native` (grep is empty). `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:178-179`: "cells of different owners are padded apart, asserted at compile time"; `:315-318`: "The padding rule above binds lane cells, pool heads, and free-list heads, and debug instrumentation may not change which cells share a line; it binds the target topology too, which also indexes one cell array by carrier, so this subsection is not free there either"; `:947-948`: "It also requires removing shared cache lines from the hot path." (Note for whoever fixes it: the earlier write-up said "the old block goes without an `rt_free`" -- that is wrong, `realloc` frees it itself; the defect is the stale registry row.) (2) The old block's release and the new block's allocation are both attributed to the calling lane whatever lane owned the original, and the block is grown in place from a lane that may not own it. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:318-323`: "A release issued away from the allocation owner is a remote release even inside one shard ... The rule is written over release, not `free`: growing a block in place is a release of the old block, so a reallocation issued away from the owner allocates on the issuing lane, copies, and remotely releases; only the owner grows in place." | RV2-DEBT-287 | **FIVE SCHEDULER TRACE WORDS ARE PLAIN `uint64_t` WRITTEN UNDER DIFFERENT SHARD LOCKS AND READ UNDER ANOTHER, WHICH IS THE MODE THE PER-COMMIT PERF GATE MEASURES IN.** `trace_sched_hash`, `trace_sched_events`, `trace_sched_local_pops`, `trace_sched_inject_pops` and `trace_sched_steal_pops` (`runtime/native/rt_async_trace.c:30-34`) are non-atomic file-scope `uint64_t` sitting between neighbours that are all `_Atomic uint64_t` (`:19-29`, `:35-38`). They are incremented at `:576-586` from `rt_trace_sched_record`, which every carrier calls from `pop_task_from_deque` under ITS OWN shard's lock (`rt_ready_queue.c:170`) -- different mutexes for different shards, and for one shard several carriers serialize but the reset at `:647-651` and the read at `:682-686` do not hold that lock. Concurrent unsynchronized read-write and write-write on plain objects is a data race and therefore undefined behaviour, and the numbers a traced build reports -- the numbers the per-commit performance gate reads its budget from -- come out of it. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:349-352`: "A carrier has no lock and this section creates none: carrier cells are published release and read acquire, and lane-owned cells are read under their lane, one at a time, by a reader holding no other"; `:362-365`: "A reported counter is admissible evidence when its owner is named ... A row may not publish a number whose writers share neither a lock nor an owner." Owner classification 2026-08-27: correctness/UB. | Closed 2026-08-28 (an owner per cell, in rt_sched_trace.{h,c}) Ruling Р8 (2026-08-27) fixes what these five words are and therefore what the fix must be: state a runtime decision reads is present and synchronized in EVERY build, reporting that only reports is switchable, and a debug invariant is a third thing armed in the gated builds. These five are read by the per-commit perf gate, so they are reporting -- which does not excuse the tearing: a reported counter is admissible evidence only when its owner is named, and a plain word several lanes write names none. The fix is an owner per cell, not an atomic per word. **CLOSED 2026-08-28.** The cluster moved to `runtime/native/rt_sched_trace.{h,c}` and the five words became one cell per owner. There are exactly two kinds of popper and each gets its own cell: a carrier, which writes only the cell at its own flat carrier index with no lock and none invented; and the runtime's control lane, which is every non-carrier thread draining shard 0 through `ready_pop`, all of them holding shard 0's lock across the record. So every reported number has writers that share a lock or share an owner. The cell is `_Alignas(64)` and padded to exactly one cache line with `_Static_assert` on both the size and the alignment, per the §1 padding rule; the array is `aligned_alloc`ed, so the padding holds at run time and not only in the type. Writers publish `memory_order_release`, readers load `memory_order_acquire`. `rt_trace_sched_record` keeps its name and signature, so no call site changed. WHAT THE DUMP REPORTS: one record per owner and no total. `SCHED_TRACE owner=carrier id=<k> shard=<s, or `none` before this owner has recorded anything> local= inject= steal= events= pop_mix= tier1_steal_denied= conn_owner_local= conn_owner_mismatch=`, one per carrier; the same shape under `owner=control id=0`; then, last, `SCHED_TRACE owner=runtime mode= seed= carriers=N owners=N+1 conn_owner_placed= unowned_pops= dropped_records=`. The runtime record is how a READER LEARNS THE OWNER SET: `owners` says how many owner records precede it, so a reader that counted fewer knows one went missing even when the write that lost it could not report itself, and `dropped_records` counts a short or refused write rather than discarding it (§1's write rule, for these records — the other six sites in RV2-DEBT-288 are untouched). No row sums across owners, because a sum over owners has writers that share neither a lock nor an owner; the reader adds the owner records up and knows exactly which set it added. The `owner=runtime` row is not such a sum: it is one owner's own three counts. AMENDED 2026-08-28 after integration review, which found the same defect twice over. The guard was keyed on four identifiers while this file's own `sched_trace_unowned_pops` and `sched_trace_dropped_records` sat at file scope in exactly the banned form, written from more than one owner's path and printed at the dump -- a guard its own file evades. It now refuses the SHAPE: rt_sched_trace.c declares no counter at file scope at all, whatever it is called, and every number the dump emits is loaded through a cell pointer, so a word that came from anywhere else cannot reach the wire. The stated rule was absolute where the code was not, so the code moved to meet it rather than the sentence being softened: a refused steal and a connection task run are the popper's own action (§1 enumerates a refused steal), taken inside the same pop under the same lock discipline, so `tier1_steal_denied`, `conn_owner_local` and `conn_owner_mismatch` moved onto the owner cell and are summed over the owner set by the reader; the runtime kept a cell of its own, padded apart like the rest, holding exactly what no carrier and no lane performed -- `conn_owner_placed` (the creator may own no cell), `unowned_pops` and `dropped_records`. That is the whole of the runtime exemption and it is checkable: nothing else in the file can hold a number. `events` is DERIVED from the three buckets at print time rather than kept as a sixth word, so an owner's total can never disagree with its parts. The cell records its own `shard_id` on its first pop, so the cell names its owner instead of the dump guessing from the topology; an owner that recorded nothing reports `shard=none`. The ordered FNV chain became `pop_mix`, a wrapping sum of a finalized per-pop mix, for a reason that is itself a finding (below): addition composes across owners, so the reader can fingerprint the whole run from owner-attributed cells, which an ordered chain cannot. MEASURED, red on the revert. Behavioural: with the runtime reverted and the tests kept, `TestRuntimeV2SchedTraceReportsAnOwnerPerCell` fails on `SCHED_TRACE record names no owner: SCHED_TRACE mode=parallel seed=0 local=7780 inject=22924 steal=3 ... events=30707`. DISCRIMINATED against the atomic-per-word fix the ruling warned about: with the same five words made `_Atomic` (fetch_add on the counters, CAS loop on the hash — race-free, and TSan-clean by construction), the gate is still red, and the structural row names the reason rather than an accident — `rt_async_trace.c still holds "trace_sched_hash": a scheduler pop counter at file scope is written by every carrier and owned by none, atomic or not`. COST OF THE OLD WORDS, measured on a stand of four threads recording 30,000 pops each while a reader dumps: the pre-fix code reports `events=58379` against 120,000 pops actually made — **it loses 51% of them** — and TSan halts on `data race ... Location is global 'trace_sched_events'`, write at `rt_async_trace.c:576` in `rt_trace_sched_record` against read at `:685` in `rt_sched_trace_dump`. The same stand on the fixed code reports exactly 30,000 per carrier with a 10,000/10,000/10,000 source split, `unowned_pops=0 dropped_records=0`, TSan clean, exit 0. PERF GATE BUDGETS: they do NOT need re-baselining. `TestRuntimeV2PerfControlLaneGate` asserts on TRACE_EXEC/TRACE_NET counters (`control_lock_acquired`, the tagged `ctrl_*` sites, `placement_adoptions`, `accept_owner_active_shards`), every one of which was already `_Atomic` and none of which this change touches; nothing here alters how often the control lane is taken. What the fix removes is the UB that made the whole traced process undefined while those numbers were being read, so the ceilings stand but the RECORDED HEAD VALUES are not evidence and want one re-measurement on the reference host to confirm they still land where §08-evidence says. Post-fix on the development host: lifecycle-control 6.002/req (ceiling 9.0), steady-state-control 8.177/req (ceiling 20.0), placement_adoptions=255, accept_owner_active_shards=8 — that host is not the CI reference host, so it is a smoke reading and not the baseline. What DOES change is the SCHED_TRACE numbers themselves: they stop losing increments, so `local`/`inject`/`steal`/`events` can only rise, and any budget keyed on them must be re-taken. The only such consumer is the `steal` column of `scripts/bench_native_net.sh`, now summed over the named owner set by a new `trace_owner_sum` helper instead of read off one ownerless row; its historical column was a lossy count and is not comparable. FINDING WORTH ITS OWN ROW: `SURGE_SCHED=seeded` does NOT reproduce which carrier runs which task. Two seeded runs of `TestMTSeededScheduler` divide the same 32 pops as 11/6/9/6 and 6/12/8/6 across four carriers. The old assertion `trace1.hash == trace2.hash` passed only because every pop in that workload comes off ONE FIFO inject queue, so the global chain fingerprinted the queue's order and not the schedule; per carrier there is nothing stable to compare. The test now compares the composed `pop_mix` over the owner set, which is what a seed actually reproduces — WHICH pops the run made, not who made each one. Whether seeded mode is supposed to fix the assignment is a question for the owner and is not answered here. GATE: `make runtime-v2-sched-trace-check`, wired into `runtime-v2-check`, running `TestRuntimeV2SchedTrace{ReportsAnOwnerPerCell,CellsAreOwnedAndPaddedApart,StandCountsExactlyPerOwner,StandUnderThreadSanitizer}`. `rt_async_trace.c` shrank from 666 to 547 effective LOC by exactly what moved; `rt_async_internal.h` 666 -> 659; `rt_async_state.c` unchanged at 526; new files 210 and 30. | Runtime V2 §1 Traces and counters + §10 (Epic 23b Wave D, tracing) | The five words become owner-named cells -- carrier-owned, published release and read acquire, or lane-owned and read under the lane -- with a TSan run over the traced build clean, and every reported scheduler row names the owner it was aggregated from. |

The tree already contains the correct shape one file over: `rt_io.c:52-58` loops on the returned `ssize_t`. A partial write on a pipe or a refused write on a full buffer therefore truncates a dump into something that still parses, with no counter saying it happened. `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:359-362`: "Each record is emitted by one `write` whose result is checked; a short or refused write is counted as a dropped record, not discarded". | Open | Runtime V2 §1 Traces and counters + §10 (Epic 23b Wave D, tracing) | All seven sites check the result and either complete the record or count it, `trace_records_dropped` is reported with its owner named, and a stand with a deliberately small pipe buffer shows the counter rising rather than a truncated dump. |
