| RV2-DEBT-291 | **A TASK'S SLOT IS STORED WITH A HEAP POINTER FOR AN ID, AND THE RUNTIME PANICS AT ONE MILLISECOND IN.** First read as a segment-growth race: `rt_task_slot_store` (`runtime/native/rt_async_state.c`) raised one sentence on both of its branches, so a field report could not say which fired. Split into two messages naming the id and the segment index, the branch that fires is "id past the table", NOT the NULL segment, and the ids it prints are HEAP POINTERS (`0x70f9bfea8c01`, `0x7b1280000`) rather than task ids -- `free_task` reading `task->id` out of an `rt_task` that was already freed. It is a use-after-free, and it is TWO defects that only show up together. (1) `apply_poll_outcome` (`rt_task_complete.c`) held no reference across itself. What keeps a polled task addressable during its turn is its RUNNING status; every arm ends that protection and then keeps using the pointer, and the yielded arm re-pushes through `ready_push_with_policy`, which BLOCKS on the owner shard lock and re-reads the task's status on the far side. (2) `task_release` / `task_release_lane_aware` (`rt_task_lifetime.c`) decided the free from an `atomic_fetch_sub` plus a SEPARATE, later `task_status_load` -- a decrement and a status read that are not one observation. A poller holds the raw pointer rather than a reference and takes one of its own, so a count that reached zero is raised back to one, completed and freed inside that window; the first thread's late status read then reads freed memory, sees `TASK_DONE` and frees a second time. Defect 1's pin ADDS exactly that resurrect-from-zero, which is why the pin alone made the panic worse (the lead measured a `free_task` signature about 1 in 40000 WITH the pin and 0 in 40000 without). Both close together: the pin, and a completion flag packed into `handle_refs` so the free is decided by one RMW with nothing read after it. | Closed 2026-08-28 (the pin, and one RMW for the free decision) | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle) | The PIN's half is proved DETERMINISTICALLY, and was not always: `TestRuntimeV2LifecyclePollOutcomePinProof` / `...NegativeControl` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_poll_outcome_pin_test.go`, `runtime_v2_pending`, `runtime-v2-lifecycle-check`) hold the yielded re-push at the EXISTING `SP_READY_REQUEUE_BEFORE_LOCK` -- after the unlocked pre-checks, before the owner shard lock -- and then do from the driver, in order, the two things an awaiting poll does to such a task: the wake a target that is neither WAITING nor DONE receives (`rt_task_poll`, `rt_async_task.c:220`), which hands the READY, not-yet-enqueued task to another worker that runs it to DONE; and the last-handle drop that same awaiter makes once it finds it DONE (`:244`). Both were read off a live report's FREEING stack rather than invented. Shipping build, `SURGE_SHARDS=1`, `SURGE_THREADS=2/4`, exit 0: `debt291 window: status=0 enqueued=0`, `debt291 completed in window: done=1`, `debt291 drop: completed=1 refs_after_drop=1` -- what is left after the last HANDLE is gone is the poller's own reference, which IS the rule. `-DRT_POLL_OUTCOME_PIN_NEGATIVE_CONTROL`, same three window sentences, `refs_after_drop=0` and `AddressSanitizer: heap-use-after-free ... READ of size 1 ... in task_status_load rt_async_internal.h:587 / ready_push_with_policy rt_ready_queue.c:270 / apply_poll_outcome rt_task_complete.c:517`, exit 1. Stable in SERIES, not once: the pair run 10 times end to end on 2026-08-31 (lane `lane-sur141` on `e34b7db8`, 32 cores) gave 10 PASS 0 FAIL, 34-37s a round -- `SURGE_STDLIB=$(pwd) SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 go test -tags runtime_v2_pending ./internal/vm -run '^TestRuntimeV2LifecyclePollOutcomePin' -count=1 -parallel=1 -p=1 --timeout 600s`. The release decision's half stays a campaign: `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleTaskFreeIsOneObservation` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_task_free_observation_test.go`, same tag and gate), an AddressSanitizer campaign over the timeout-cancel program, now TWO arms. Measured at `SURGE_THREADS=4`, 24 concurrent processes on 32 cores -- trunk `03379549`, 3 of 48 runs report `heap-use-after-free` in `ready_push_with_policy`; `-DRT_POLL_OUTCOME_PIN_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` (pin removed), 6 of 48 the same; `-DRT_TASK_RELEASE_SPLIT_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` (split decision restored, with the window held open), 3 of 48 report `heap-use-after-free` in `free_task` -- the reported symptom itself; both fixes in place, 0 of 96 and 0 of 80. **The pin arm was RETIRED from that campaign on 2026-08-31 (SUR-141) and replaced by the stand above.** It had gone from reporting in 10 aggregates of 10 at `97e82b63` to reporting nothing in 4 of 10 at `e34b7db8`, and a control that reports nothing correctly says the row proved nothing. The reason is the cancel-ordering fix, established from a captured report rather than inferred: the freed object is a CHECKPOINT task (`spawn_checkpoint_task_locked`, `rt_async_task.c:531`) and its freeing stack is `rt_task_poll` -> `task_release_lane_aware` -> `reclaim_task` (`rt_task_lifetime.c:146`), so BOTH sides of the race belong to one awaiting poll -- the wake at `rt_async_task.c:220` and the last-handle drop at `:244`. That poll can only meet a child mid-yield-re-push while the child is still live, and `e34b7db8` made a cancel reach the leaves before it makes anyone runnable again (`rt_task_complete.c:158-180`), so a cancelled awaiter is re-polled with its children already DONE and takes the `:222` branch instead. The arm encoded a schedule; the stand asserts the rule. |
| RV2-DEBT-292 | **THE SERVICE AGENT THE MODEL NOW NAMES DOES NOT EXIST IN THE TREE.** `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md` Section 1 (2026-08-27, ruling Р1): the threads serving a shard hold two roles never merged -- only a carrier executes task turns and owns private work, and a service agent "drives its transport, readiness, and timers, polling no task, and touching no carrier's private deque or run-next slot". A repository-wide grep over `runtime/native` for `service_agent`, `service agent` and `serving thread` returns nothing: every thread that serves a shard today is one of its carriers, and the readiness poll, the timer index and the transport pump are all entered from `rt_worker_turn.c` on the carrier's own turn. The consequence the model cares about is the coverage rule: with no non-carrier server, "at least one thread serving that shard -- carrier or service agent" degenerates to "a carrier", and every service obligation is discharged by a thread that could instead be executing. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D (task lifecycle), multi-carrier | A non-carrier serving thread exists, is named by the topology and recorded with it, and a stand shows a shard whose only carrier is inside a task still having its readiness and its due deadlines served; the steal and publication predicates refuse to hand that thread a task turn. |
| RV2-DEBT-293 | **THE 1 ms SERVICE BUDGET IS MEASURED BY NOTHING.** Section 1 makes it normative that what is ready, or past its deadline, "reaches the eligible class's queue with an eligible credit and a notification within a normative budget of 1 ms, measured from the earlier of the deadline's expiry and the readiness the shard's poll could have observed, never from when a thread happened to look". No counter in the tree names that interval: a grep over `runtime/native` for `service_publish`, `service_latency` and `publish_latency` returns nothing, and the trace surface (`rt_async_trace.c`) counts wakes, parks, sleeps and lock sites but no publication delay. A budget nothing measures is a sentence, not a bound -- and the harder half is the origin, since "the readiness the shard's poll could have observed" is not an observable in the tree today. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D, multi-carrier | The interval is measured from an origin the runtime can name, published as a counter and a maximum per shard, asserted by a stand that arms a deadline while every carrier is busy and requires publication inside the budget, and the bench rows report it in both reporting modes. |
| RV2-DEBT-294 | **NO STRUCTURE THAT CAN HOLD WORK NAMES ITS CLASS, WHICH THE MODEL NOW REFUSES.** Section 1: "Every structure that can hold work names its class -- the subsections below, and equally the fd registry and deadline index of Section 4, the ready queue, run-next slot and public queue of Section 8, and the class queues of Section 10 -- and one that can hold work and names no class is refused." Two classifications are meant: the obligation class (runnable / service / maintenance) and the eligibility class of Section 10. Neither exists as a declared property: a grep over `runtime/native` for `eligibility_kind`, `eligibility_class` and `obligation_class` returns nothing, and the closest thing in the tree is `TASK_PLACEMENT_CONNECTION` (`rt_scheduler_placement.c`), a placement tag on tasks rather than a class on structures. Until a structure declares its class, the safepoint invariant of Section 8 cannot be evaluated per class and the no-work predicate of Section 10 cannot read "every class queue its identity admits" -- both fall back to counting whether some worker is busy, which Section 8 already says is not enough. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D, multi-carrier | Every work-holding structure carries its class as a declared field, a compile-time or start-up check refuses one that does not, and the safepoint invariant and the no-work predicate are written against the declaration rather than against a hard-coded list. |
| RV2-DEBT-295 | **A SCOPE'S OWNER IS PINNED ONCE AND THERE IS NO QUIESCENT REPIN.** Section 1 (ruling Р6): "A scope belongs to its owner lane for its whole life and never migrates silently; the one transfer is a quiescent repin, admissible only under the serializer with no member live, no completion outstanding for a member, and no registration outstanding on the scope's wait key, re-registering every waiter on the destination lane before it publishes the new owner; a repin missing one of these is a contract failure, not a slow path." The tree has the first half and not the second: `rt_scope_enter` writes `scope->owner_shard_id` once at creation (`runtime/native/rt_async_scope.c:122`) and no path writes it again, so the ban holds by absence rather than by protocol, and the transfer the model now admits cannot be performed at all. `docs/runtime-v2-epics/08-tasks/09-scope-owner-lane.md:57-62` argues for the ban and is now the older text. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D, multi-carrier | The repin exists as a named operation with the four preconditions asserted, a stand drives it with waiters registered and requires every one of them re-registered on the destination before the new owner is published, and a negative control drops one precondition and goes red. |
| RV2-DEBT-296 | **`spawn` INTO A SCOPE THE TASK IS NOT A MEMBER OF IS ACCEPTED, AND THE ADOPTION IS PARTIAL.** Section 9, Structured Concurrency (`docs/RUNTIME_V2.md:742`, ruling Р6, a LANGUAGE contract change): "a task created outside a scope and scheduled inside one is adopted by it ... The adoption is partial, which is worse than silent membership: the child is never counted, so the scope does not join it, yet its cancelled result still raises the scope's fail-fast flag and cancels the real members. Semantic analysis rejects that `spawn` with a diagnostic of its own rather than change its meaning in silence." The runtime half is `rt_task_wake` (`runtime/native/rt_async_task.c:158-171`), which takes the control lock and writes `target->parent_scope_id = scope->id` for a live task whose id was zero -- the second writer RV2-DEBT-282 also names, here read as the language surface rather than as a lane defect. No diagnostic exists: `internal/diag/codes.go` ends its task block at `SemaTaskBodyNoValue Code = 3208`. The next free code is 3209 and three pieces of stopped work reserve it -- this refusal, the `@copy`-on-alias refusal of RV2-DEBT-264, and the scope-membership-at-creation refusal the L5 lane specifies -- so whichever lands first claims it in `codes.go` in the same commit and the other two take the numbers after it. **PARKED 2026-08-28 after two rework passes, with what was learned.** A refusal was written twice and both times the adversarial review found it wrong in BOTH directions. It refused a legal program -- a task created INSIDE the body and assigned to an outer `mut` binding -- with a message asserting a creation order the program contradicts; and it missed at least four spellings of the shape it exists to refuse: an alias (`let same = earlier; spawn same`), a synchronous helper (`fn schedule(t: Task<int>) -> Task<int> { return spawn t; }`), a call pass-through, and a tuple destructuring. Three of those spellings produce DIFFERENT runtime answers on the native lane, measured 3 runs of 3 each: one makes the task a full scope member, one makes it none. That three-way incoherence is the defect this row is about, and a refusal keyed to the SPELLING of the binding cannot close it. THE ROOT CAUSE THE PASSES ESTABLISHED: the premise both attempts were built on is not what the tree does. `async { ... }` lowers to a plain constructor call with NO registration, so 'membership is decided when the task is created' describes an intention rather than an implementation, and a diagnostic teaching it teaches a rule the compiler does not have. A sound refusal needs the question asked about the TASK -- which scope, if any, created it -- and the tree does not carry that fact anywhere a checker can read. ALSO ESTABLISHED, and it corrects this row: the runtime adoption in `rt_task_wake` MUST NOT be deleted on the grounds that the compiler refuses everything reaching it. A sync-helper program reaches it with a scope set, measured 3 runs of 3. Parked rather than continued because it is a LANGUAGE change outside the current wave's exit conditions, and because two passes producing two wrong refusals is the signal to stop and ask what the checker would need, not to send a third. | Closed (2026-09-01; RE-DERIVED 2026-09-02 on base `5b44cb0c` for the same reason as RV2-DEBT-282 -- the diagnostic contract row, the legal inner-creation/outer-binding control and the branch-merge row all passed again there, and the golden pair was regenerated on that base rather than carried) -- SEM3209 is now the dedicated refusal: `cannot spawn a task created outside the current scope`, with note `task was created here, outside this scope` at the creation expression and help `create the task inside this scope before spawning it`. The analysis tracks the task value rather than a binding spelling through direct use, aliases, synchronous helper return, helper-side spawn, tuple/nested destructuring and branch/loop merges. A task created inside the current scope remains legal even when assigned to an outer `mut` binding. The invalid/valid golden pair and exact note/help unit contract pin both directions. No syntax, adoption attribute or public scope identity was added; the runtime counterpart is closed with RV2-DEBT-282. | Runtime V2 §1 Scope accounting + §9 (Epic 23b Wave D, scope) | Done: the crossing is refused before lowering with SEM3209 across every named spelling, the legal inner-creation/outer-binding control remains green, and wake no longer writes provenance. |
| RV2-DEBT-297 | **A LIVENESS ROW REQUIRES A SCHEDULING SHAPE ITS PROGRAM DOES NOT FORCE, AND A QUIET MACHINE NEVER TAKES IT.** `TestMTChannelParkUnpark` (`internal/vm/mt_executor_test.go:1031-1034`) runs a ping-pong program at `SURGE_THREADS=2` with `SURGE_TRACE_EXEC=1` and fails when `channel_handoff_yield == 0`. That counter is incremented in exactly one place, `prepare_channel_send_yield` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c:18-29`), and its own comment says why: "The compiler emits this helper only when the ready path immediately reaches a recv suspend point." Whether a send's ready path immediately reaches a recv suspend is a scheduling outcome, not a property of the program, so the row asserts that a mechanism was EXERCISED rather than that a contract holds. On the dedicated runner `surge-bm` (cores 8-15 isolated, governor performance) `make runtime-v2-liveness-stress`, which runs it `-count=50`, fails all 50 with `channel_handoff_yield=0` beside `park_attempt=88002`, `park_committed=87995`, `worker_sleep=7552` -- the program ran, the workers parked and woke, and the fast path was simply never the one taken. The same row is green on the working machine even pinned to the same width (`taskset -c 8-15 ... -count=3`), so this is not a defect the machine reveals in the runtime: it is a row whose passing condition depends on the machine. The row's OTHER assertions are contract assertions and hold everywhere -- `channel_blocking_wait`, `channel_task_blocking_send`, `channel_task_blocking_recv` and `compensation_started` all zero, which is the real claim that an async channel does not pin a worker. | Closed (2026-08-27) -- the first branch was taken: the program now FORCES the shape instead of hoping for it, and the assertion is kept. A new phase runs eight times before the ping-pong: a receiver is spawned on a rendezvous channel and the runtime is given two yields to reach its park -- the construction the same program already uses twice for its cancel phases -- and only then does a sender run whose continuation is a recv, so the ready path is the one the send must take. A zero now means the fast path is dead or a spawned receiver did not reach its park across two yields, and both are defects rather than an interleaving. Measured where it failed: `-count=50` under `taskset -c 8-15` on `surge-bm`, the exact command `make runtime-v2-liveness-stress` runs, 50 of 50 green (`ok surge/internal/vm 245.461s`) against 50 of 50 red before. The four hard zeros are untouched | Epic 23b Wave D, multi-carrier | Done: the program forces the shape it measures -- a send whose continuation is a recv suspend, arranged so the ready path must reach it -- and the row keeps its assertion; `make runtime-v2-liveness-stress` is green on `surge-bm` for 50 of 50. |
| RV2-DEBT-298 | **THE CHANNEL ORDERS ADMISSION, NOT COMMIT, BECAUSE THE OWNER LANE IS RELEASED ACROSS THE ELEMENT MOVE.** Owner ruling Р5 (2026-08-27) promises owner-lane COMMIT order: "direct handoff does not overtake a write already committed in the ring, nor a sender the lane admitted earlier". `channel_stage_locked` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c:221-241`) acquires a park slot and reserves the delivery address under `ch_shard`'s lock, then `rt_shard_unlock(ch_shard)` at `:232`, moves the element with `rt_value_move_init_detached` at `:233`, and only then `rt_shard_lock(ch_shard)` at `:234` before `rt_park_pool_commit_deliver_locked` at `:236`. Admission -- taking the slot -- is serialized by the lane; the COMMIT is not. Two senders admitted in order A then B can commit in order B then A whenever B's element is cheaper to move than A's, which for a composite against a scalar is the ordinary case rather than a race that needs contriving. So the tree enforces the weaker of the two orders the ruling names, and the promise cannot be written into `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md` as a description of what ships. What is NOT established here is whether a receiver can observe the inversion: that depends on the order the park pool hands committed deliveries to poppers, and establishing it is this row's first job, before any fix. Distinct from RV2-DEBT-276 (a rendezvous that loses its candidate destroys the value and re-sends a moved-from source), RV2-DEBT-277 (the single-transfer claim has no retry budget) and RV2-DEBT-278 (the wait queues are a linear scan of the shard's waiter store), which are open against the same file for other reasons. | Open -- Section 7 now carries the promise with the gap named in the text, so what this row owes is the fix and its witness rather than the model sentence. The gap is TWO sites, not one. (1) The owner lane is released across the element move: `channel_stage_locked` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c:221-241`) acquires a park slot and reserves the delivery address under the lock, `rt_shard_unlock` at `:232`, moves the element at `:233`, re-locks at `:234` and commits at `:236`, so two senders admitted A then B commit B then A whenever B's element is cheaper to move. (2) A refill may serve a later candidate than the one that queued first (`:559-607`, and the skip-and-serve-the-next paths at `:572-580`, `:604-606`, `:626-636`), which the owner's 2026-08-27 sharpening of Р5 makes part of the promise: "a sender the lane admitted earlier" covers a sender that only queued on the send key. The ring half is kept today and cannot invert -- both send paths gate the direct handoff on the buffer being empty with nothing on its way in -- so this row is about the sender half alone | Epic 23b Wave D (channels) | A stand admits two senders in a known order with elements of deliberately different move cost, observes the order a receiver sees, and is red on the unfixed tree if that order can invert; the lane is held across the commit or the commit is ordered by the position taken at admission;. |
| RV2-DEBT-299 | **THE STANDARD LIBRARY DOES NOT COMPILE, AND HAS NOT SINCE 2026-08-14.** `SURGE_STDLIB=$(pwd) go run ./cmd/surge build stdlib/fs/fs.sg` is refused: `error SEM3199 stdlib/fs/fs.sg:467:12 cannot return a range over local 'out': the cursor walks storage this call frame frees`. `walkdir` builds a local array and returns `out.__range()` over it; SEM3199 arrived on trunk in `b5199277` ("refuse the three shapes the owner ruled out of the language", 2026-08-14) and refuses exactly that. The diagnostic even names the fix it wants -- return the array and let the caller iterate -- so this is a migration the language change owed its own standard library and did not get. It is not one module: the ownership corpus reports sixteen unrecorded compile failures, and eleven of them are `stdlib/http/*.sg` and `stdlib/json/*.sg`. **Nothing in `make check` compiles a stdlib module**, so six lanes of this wave reported `make check` green over a standard library that does not build; the only gate that reaches it is `runtime-v2-ownership-check`, which is itself red for this reason (RV2-DEBT-300). The `make check` half is `internal/stdlibgate`: it discovers every module under `stdlib/` from the tree and compiles each through a generated importing program all the way to MIR, in 6.5s, naming the module that failed; it runs untagged so `go test ./...` picks it up. Shown RED on revert of the `walkdir` migration: `stdlib module stdlib/fs does not compile: ... SEM3199 stdlib/fs/fs.sg:467:12`. **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-28 AND THE PREMISE IS GONE.** The SEM3199 refusal this row was opened on no longer happens: `walkdir` was migrated and `surge diag` over all 32 stdlib modules reports zero diagnostics, module for module. What remains true is that sixteen modules do not reach MIR under the ownership corpus's no-entrypoint profile, and that is RV2-DEBT-302's, correctly diagnosed there as a monomorphization use-site loss bisected to `21a816e9` and explicitly NOT a stdlib source defect. | RV2-DEBT-301 | **THE SYNC-POINT GATE CANNOT SEE A HOOK THAT HAS NO NAME, AND TWO SUCH HOOKS ARE LIVE.** `check_sync_points.sh` cross-checks its allowlist against the enumerators declared in `runtime/native/rt_sync_point.h` and against every `RT_SYNC_POINT(...)` call site, and it never looks at `rt_sp_name` in `rt_sync_point.c` -- the table `rt_sp_arm` searches to turn a name from the environment into a hook. A sync point declared in the header and missing from that table is invisible to the gate and aborts any stand that arms it by name. Measured at trunk `83dc74b1` by set-differencing the two files: thirty-seven enumerators, thirty-five names, and the two that are declared and unnamed are `SP_CHANNEL_LAST_RELEASE_BEFORE_FREE` and `SP_IMMEDIATE_ON_AFTER_PUBLISH`. The D6-tail lane hit this class on its own three enumerators and fixed those; these two predate it and remain. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D | `check_sync_points.sh` refuses a header enumerator that `rt_sp_name` does not carry, the two live gaps are named, and a stand arming each by name reaches its hook rather than aborting. |
| RV2-DEBT-303 | **A SHARED `&T` PARAMETER OF AN `async fn` IS COPIED INTO A HEAP BOX THAT NOTHING EVER FREES.** `emitAsyncRefParamBox` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_func.go:241`) is reached from `emitParamStores` for every non-mutable reference parameter of an async constructor: it calls `rt_alloc(sizeof(T), alignof(T))`, copies the REFERENT into the box, and makes the parameter local name the box, so the reference stays valid after the caller's frame dies. The box pointer is then packed into the task frame (`Pc0`) and travels through every yield. No leg frees it: the local is `[copy,ref]`, so MIR gives it no drop obligation; `emitSuspensionFrameReleaseBody` frees the frame storage and deliberately walks nothing; and the poll's terminal exits do not know the local is a box. Measured 2026-08-28 at trunk `03379549` with NONE of the channel-handle work applied: `async fn read_ref(x: &int)` called eight times definitely-loses 64 bytes in 8 blocks, stack `rt_alloc` <- the constructor, deterministic over three runs; the same tree with the D3b C1 lane on it gives the same figure, so the leak is trunk's and is neither caused nor widened by the handle flip. It became visible because `core/sync.sg`'s waiting tasks now borrow their primitive (RUNTIME_V2 section 11, no atomic on the lock path): eight lock/unlock rounds through `m.lock()`, `m2.lock()` and `s.acquire()` are 24 boxes, 192 bytes, which is the residue `TestRuntimeV2MutexLockUnlockValgrindBounded` pins exactly. Second-order and NOT settled here: the box is a bytewise copy of the referent taken with no retain, so for a `&Mutex` the task reads a channel handle nobody counted -- benign only because the box is also never released. Freeing the box without answering that question turns this leak into a double release. **CITATION UPDATED 2026-08-29: `emitSuspensionFrameReleaseBody` no longer exists.** The three-body release family was deleted when the frame gained a lifecycle word it answers from; the one release is now `rt_frame_release`, which walks a frame's members only when the frame says PACKED. This row's MECHANISM is unchanged -- the async ref-param box is still copied and still freed by nothing -- but the function it named as the site is gone, so read the row against `rt_frame_release` and `emitAsyncRefParamBox` instead. **CLOSED 2026-09-02 by deletion, on the terms the routing set.** Steps 4.1-4.4 of RV2-DEBT-307 promoted the borrowed place into the creator's frame (`StableActivationPlaces`, the resident fields) and `1ba55ecd` pinned the borrowing child to the creator's carrier by route and by credit, so a shared `&T` parameter is now stored as the pointer it is: `emitAsyncRefParamBox`, `shouldBoxAsyncRefParams` and the `async-ref-box` allocation site are gone (`emitParamStores` says why in place). Before: `TestRuntimeV2MutexLockUnlockValgrindBounded` pinned exactly 192 bytes in 24 blocks. After: the same program under `TestRuntimeV2MutexLockUnlockValgrindZero` reads strict zero (9.9 s, local, 2026-09-02); `TestEmitAsyncSharedRefParamKeepsCallerAlias` pins the constructor prologue free of `rt_alloc` with `store ptr %p0, ptr %l0` and the task built through `__task_create_affine`, and the alloc-guard census row `async_shared_ref_param` reaches `rt_frame_alloc` alone. The second-order question closes with the box: nothing is copied, so nothing is held uncounted. | Closed 2026-09-02 — deleted with RV2-DEBT-307 step 7 | Epic 23b Wave D (channel owner) | An `async fn` taking a shared reference leaks nothing: the box is reclaimed exactly once on every path the task can end on (return, cancellation, an abandoned frame, and a task constructed but never polled), and the ownership of what the box holds is stated -- a bytewise copy of a reference-counted referent either retains at the box and releases at its free, or the box stops copying the referent. `TestRuntimeV2MutexLockUnlockValgrindBounded` is rewritten to strict zero and renamed in the same change. **ROUTED 2026-09-02:** the 2026-09-02 ruling on RV2-DEBT-307 answers the second-order question this row deliberately left open. The box is not taught to retain; it is DELETED, at step 7 of that row's vertical, once a real borrow pointer into promoted address-stable storage replaces it. This row therefore closes with RV2-DEBT-307 and not before it: deleting the box earlier would hand the task a reference into a per-poll `alloca`, which is the double-release this row warned about wearing a different mask. |
| RV2-DEBT-304 | **THE `ValueOps` CLONE SLOT RETURNS `void`, SO THE ROLLBACK FAILPOINT P3 ASKS FOR CANNOT BE WRITTEN.** Section 6's P3 requires "a generated `ValueOps` failpoint [that] returns after initializing one child to prove rollback", and its success list requires that "a returned internal status restores the destination to `EMPTY`, drops its initialized child once, changes only the claimed entitlement to `DROPPED`, releases the pin, and leaves a sibling plus canonical `READY` result intact". Section 3 of `23-storage-model-and-typed-carrier-abi.md` says which operations may do that: "only an operation that returns an explicit internal status is locally rollback-capable". The tree's duplication slot is `typedef void (*rt_value_clone_init_fn)(void* dst, const void* src)` (`runtime/native/rt_typed_carrier_abi.generated.h`), and the take calls it through `rt_value_duplicate_detached`, which also returns `void` (`rt_value_ops.h`). There is therefore no status for a failpoint to return and nothing for `rt_far_task_take_result` to roll back from: a duplication either completes or does not return at all. Established 2026-08-28 while building P3's three missing sync-point rows; the three rows landed, this clause could not, and it is recorded here rather than left to be rediscovered a fourth time. Note what is NOT claimed: no defect has been observed. The clause is unimplementable against today's ABI, which is a different thing from being wrong. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-28 -- NOT OWED, AND WHAT IS OWED INSTEAD.** A local duplication that cannot finish is FATAL: the three crossing operations return a status because a crossing has a caller that can still answer, and a local `clone_init` does not, so an internal rollback would buy something no program can observe -- recoverable out-of-memory is explicitly outside this epic. The slot keeps its `void` return and the rollback failpoint is not owed. What the ruling DOES open, and what makes it the correct reading rather than the cheap one: the tree does not currently detect the failure at all. `internal/backend/llvm/emit_aggregate_ops.go:203` calls `rt_alloc` and tests nothing, while `rt_alloc` answers `NULL` on refusal without panicking (`runtime/native/rt_alloc.c:47,87,93`), so a refused allocation inside a duplication is a segmentation fault at an address the program never chose. Making the failure fatal means the generated body TESTS the allocation and panics naming the type it could not duplicate. Reopened under that description rather than closed. | Open (rescoped by ruling) | Epic 23b Wave D -- the allocation test in every generated move/copy/clone body | Either the clone slot becomes fallible (a status return, a generated failpoint that initializes one child and then returns it, and the take's rollback that empties the destination and retires only the claimed entitlement), or the owner rules the clause out of P3 on the stated ground that a duplication in this runtime does not fail recoverably — and P3's §6 text is amended to say so, since a criterion nobody can meet is worse than one that was never written. |
| RV2-DEBT-305 | **P3'S TWO BOUNDED CHILD-PROCESS CONTROLS DO NOT EXIST, AND THE LIFECYCLE HARNESS HAS NO CHILD PROCESS TO BUILD THEM IN.** Section 6's P3 requires that "bounded child-process controls cover user panic plus deterministic allocator `NULL` without attempting real OOM", with two success bullets: a child-process user-clone panic follows the terminal `panic -> exit(Error)` path and is never observed as `Cancelled` (and the test makes no destructor/unwind claim after process termination), and the allocator `NULL` control proves no result publication, no `Cancelled` and no retry spin while making no cleanup/balance claim. Neither exists. The lifecycle harness runs one process per mode and asserts on its exit code and stderr, which is the right shape for the panic control but gives the driver nothing to observe a terminated child WITH: the assertions would have to live in a parent that forks. The allocator half additionally has no injection point — `rt_alloc` has no deterministic failure switch, so "allocator `NULL`" would have to be produced inside the stand's own duplication rather than by the runtime allocator, which weakens what the row can claim and has to be stated in the row if it is done that way. Established 2026-08-28 beside the three deterministic rows that did land. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D, P3 tail (stand work; touches no production file and may run beside anything) | Two modes in the entitlement stand, each run in a forked child whose exit status and stderr the parent asserts: a `clone_init` that panics, proving the terminal exit and that no handle ever observed `Cancelled`; and a duplication whose allocation is made to fail, proving no result was published, no `Cancelled` was answered and no retry spun. Each states in its own comment what it does NOT claim after termination. |
| RV2-DEBT-306 | **THE VM LANE REFUSES ITS OWN STANDARD LIBRARY'S WRAPPING ARITHMETIC, SO TWO PARITY ROWS HAVE BEEN RED SINCE AT LEAST 2026-08-26.** `TestLLVMParity/random_pcg32` and `TestLLVMParity/hash_xxh64` fail on the VM lane with `panic VM1003: expected numeric, got biguint and int` raised inside `wrap_u64_shift_left` (`stdlib/random/random.sg:77:22`) and `wrap_u64` (`stdlib/hash/xxh64.sg:33:13`). The LLVM lane answers; the VM lane refuses, so the parity comparison reports a stderr mismatch rather than a differing value -- one lane produced a result and the other produced a panic. The shape is a binary operation whose two sides arrived as `biguint` and `int` in the VM's value model, which the VM's numeric check refuses to pair; the mask constants these functions apply are what widen one side. It is NOT RV2-DEBT-299: those programs compile and run, and the refusal is a VM-lane value-model answer, not a front-end one. Bisected to before this session -- red at `142d5f1a` (2026-08-26) and at every commit measured since, so it predates the wave that surfaced it. | Open | Not scheduled (VM lane, pre-existing; does not block the runtime-v2 migration and is recorded so a future red is not mistaken for a regression of this wave) | Both parity rows answer on the VM lane, and the mismatch that remains, if any, is a differing value rather than a panic. |
| RV2-DEBT-307 | **A BORROW CAPTURED BY AN ORDINARY `spawn` CROSSES A CARRIER BOUNDARY WITH NO DIAGNOSTIC, AND THE DEFAULT TOPOLOGY RUNS THE CHILD CONCURRENTLY WITH THE PARENT'S WRITE.** `docs/LANGUAGE.md` states the rule outright -- borrowed `&T` and `&mut T` are not allowed to cross WORKER-THREAD boundaries, and says why: it keeps the ownership model sound without a full cross-thread borrow analysis. The check exists but is keyed to the SHARD boundary: `SemaCrossBorrowCapture` (3165, `internal/diag/codes_crossing.go:92`) is reported from exactly one site, `classifyOnCapture` (`internal/sema/on_crossing_capture.go:190-194`), which is reached only through `checkOnCaptures` (`:144`) -- the `on` / `spawn on` crossing path. An ordinary local `spawn` goes through `registerAsyncBodyOwnership` (`:300`) and never reaches it. MEASURED 2026-08-28 at `a6acc31c` with `surge diag`, two programs differing in one condition: `let mut v: int = 0; let r = &v; v = 5;` is refused with `ERROR SEM3019 cannot mutate 'v' while it is shared-borrowed`; the same borrow handed to a task -- `async fn worker(x: &int) -> int { return *x + 1; }` then `let t = spawn worker(&v); v = 5;` -- produces ZERO diagnostics. The default topology is one shard with as many carriers as cores, so the child may be executing on a neighbouring carrier at the moment the parent writes. That is a data race the compiler permits, not a latent shape. The fix is two lines of defence and the explanatory document says so: the check must know the carrier boundary and a captured borrow must live for the task's whole life; AND the scheduler must pin a borrow-holding task to the parent's carrier so it is not stolen. An open question blocks the second half and must be answered before implementation: is the pinning TRANSITIVE (is the child of a pinned child pinned), and what happens to `blocking { ... }` inside a pinned task, whose body leaves for Tier 2 while the parent's borrow is still live -- the wrong answer there is a deadlock, a pinned child waiting for a carrier that is waiting for that child. Distinct from RV2-DEBT-041, which is a borrow escaping inside a union returned from a function, and from RV2-DEBT-178, which is the VM's incomplete pin walk from the other direction. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-28 on the blocking half.** Affinity is a function of the CAPTURE SET, not of the parent-child edge: a child is affine only when it captures something borrowed, so affinity is not inherited down the tree and is transitive through borrowing. A `blocking { ... }` body may not capture a borrow and is refused exactly as a crossing is. The deadlock this row was waiting on does not exist: submitting a blocking body PARKS the submitting task (`runtime/native/rt_async_blocking.c:314-327`) rather than occupying its carrier, so an affine task waiting on a blocking body leaves its carrier free for the work that will wake it. The row is unblocked and its shape is now known; it stays unscheduled because it is language work outside 23b's waves. **OWNER RULING 2026-09-02 -- THE ROW IS A MODEL GAP, NOT AN IMPLEMENTATION DETAIL, AND IT IS SCHEDULED AS ONE VERTICAL.** Two independent read-only reviews of the partial implementation established that carrier affinity alone never made the capture sound: an ordinary local place is a per-poll `alloca`, suspension packs live values into task state and a later poll unpacks them into NEW allocas, so a child holding the original pointer dangles after its parent suspends EVEN ON THE SAME CARRIER. The plan's earlier step -- prove affinity, delete `emitAsyncRefParamBox`, pass a real borrow pointer -- was therefore insufficient and is withdrawn. Four decisions replace it. (1) STORAGE: a narrow exception is granted in `23-storage-model-and-typed-carrier-abi.md` Section 7 for a compiler-proven carrier-affine child-task borrow whose referent is a parent place promoted to address-stable fixed-offset parent async-state storage. Promotion is PLACE-oriented, never type-oriented; no new category of heap-boxed locals is introduced and no async local is heap-promoted for merely living across an await. The invariant is that a place borrowed by a live carrier-affine child has ONE stable storage identity from the child's publication until its true completion. (2) SEMA: the pin is path-sensitive flow state. It is released only when the child is DEFINITELY complete on every reachable incoming path, merging as a may-be-live lattice (`ACTIVE + RELEASED -> ACTIVE`); a global mutation at a syntactic `await` is refused as a mechanism because it releases the pin for paths on which that await never ran. Handle move state and referent pin state stay different facts. (3) SCHEDULER: no new rule is created -- Section 10 of `RUNTIME_V2.md` already requires publication and notification addressed to the eligibility CLASS, so publishing an affine task into a worker-private deque under a generic group wake credit is an implementation violation of existing normative text, not a gap in it. Steal refusal after dequeue is a defence and not the correctness mechanism. (4) `main` OUTSIDE CARRIERS: resolved by giving the entrypoint a carrier rather than by refusing the program. `@entrypoint` executes as the runtime's synthetic root task on an initial carrier; `fn main()` stays `fn main()` and nothing on the source surface becomes `async`. Refusing borrow-capturing spawn from the entrypoint was rejected because `docs/QUICKSTART.md:555-564` already shows a synchronous `main` handing `&ch` to a spawned producer and consumer. The partial implementation preserved in the DEBT-307 lane is NOT to be committed: the reviews found two P0s in it -- generic group wake credit for an affine publication, and a non-path-sensitive `UnpinForTask` -- and both are mandatory parts of this vertical rather than follow-ups. | Closed 2026-09-03 -- the vertical landed in order: normative closure and the path-sensitive pin (`3dbbe374`..`8b12beb3`), promotion and resident storage (same), carrier-addressed publication with refusal by carrier and drain at exit (`1ba55ecd`, `479a75e6`), box deletion (`69a83039`), and the 2/4/8 acceptance (`TestRuntimeV2CarrierAffineAcceptanceMatrix`: five programs -- borrow spawn, the borrowed place read after the parent suspended and resumed, sixty-four yields on the carrier, a cancelled borrowing child, a by-value child that is NOT pinned -- at 2, 4 and 8 carriers, each read through the scheduler trace: `carrier_pinned` ≥ 1 where a borrow exists and 0 where none does, `carrier_shutdown_cancelled` 0, and the poll-site refusal never fired; plus the harness rows at 2/4/8 for addressed publication and the never-polled child at exit). Every clause of the acceptance column has a row it can be read off. | Epic 23b Wave D (language/sema + compiler storage + scheduler routing), ordered: normative closure, path-sensitive pin, promotion analysis, promoted storage, real borrow lowering, carrier-addressed publication, box deletion, 2/4/8 acceptance | The two programs above receive the SAME diagnostic; a borrow-holding task is provably not stolen from its parent's carrier AND is never published into a queue an ineligible carrier can consume from; the borrowed place is provably address-stable across a parent suspend; a join on only some paths provably does not release the pin; a borrow-capturing spawn from a synchronous `@entrypoint` compiles and runs on the root carrier; and no tree ever admits the borrow in sema while the lowered pointer is still a per-poll `alloca`. |
| RV2-DEBT-308 | **A FAR TASK CANCELLED BEFORE ITS FIRST POLL NEVER RUNS ITS BODY, AND ONLY THE DEDICATED MACHINE SEES IT.** `TestRuntimeV2FarTaskCallerCancel/cancel_after_publication_before_first_poll/shards_2` fails with `missing far-body witness at shards=2` -- the program answers `far-task-caller-cancel-e2e1-ok` and the far body's own marker never appears, so the body did not execute. MEASURED, and the two machines disagree completely: **81 red of 100** on the dedicated 16-core runner, **0 red of 3** on the 32-core development machine. It is not a flake there and not reproducible here. BISECTED ON THE MACHINE THAT SEES IT, 20 runs per commit: `142d5f1a` (2026-08-26) 18/20, `338c2aa7` 20/20, `20755cf6` 16/20, `48285f25` 11/20, `8e5393dc` 16/20, `a7fecd2b` 20/20. Red at every point measured, so it PREDATES this wave; the variation between commits is scheduling, not signal. **IT ALSO CORRECTS A CLAIM I MADE.** The twenty-gate aggregate was recorded as green on one run, and this row is in `runtime-v2-transport-check`. A row that fails four times in five will pass one run in five, so that aggregate result was a sample rather than a measurement, and the roster's greenness needs N runs on the machine that fails, not one. The shape to look for: a cancellation that lands after the far task is published but before its first poll, on a two-shard runtime, where the body is expected to run and leave a witness anyway. **CLOSED 2026-08-29 by the owner's ruling, and the row was the defect.** Publication does not promise a first poll: a cancellation that linearizes while the task is PUBLISHED and before STARTED withdraws the record and answers `Cancelled()` with the body never entered, and one that loses to STARTED becomes an ordinary cooperative cancellation the body observes. Both are legal outcomes of the same program. The row demanded the witness at two or more shards on the reasoning that the child reliably wins the race there; measured, it does not, and 81 red of 100 on one host against 0 of 3 on another is the row reporting the machine. The requirement is removed and the reason is written where it stood. The shards=1 direction survives, restated as what it actually is -- this program's own arrangement, whose failure means the row stopped exercising what it was written for, rather than a guarantee about the body. | Closed | Closed 2026-08-29 | The row is green over at least 100 consecutive runs on the dedicated machine, and the aggregate's own greenness is stated as a count of runs rather than one. |
| RV2-DEBT-310 | **A LIVE TRACE DUMP IS ASKED FOR AND NEVER WAITED FOR, AND THE STAND LOSES THE RACE ONLY UNDER LOAD.** `TestRuntimeV2NetWaiterTraceContract` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_net_waiter_contract_test.go`) sends `SIGUSR1` to the server at the halfway point of a twelve-request stream and then keeps driving the stream; the last request ends the program, and only afterwards does the test require `TRACE_NET reason=sigusr1` in stderr. Nothing synchronises the two, so the assertion is that the signal handler always beats the program's own exit. MEASURED ON THE DEDICATED MACHINE, and the two numbers are the whole point: **0 red of 20** running `runtime-v2-waiter-check` by itself on an idle host, and **1 red of 5** inside `make runtime-v2-check`, where nineteen other gates have just run. Failure text: `runtime_v2_net_waiter_contract_test.go:166: missing TRACE_NET reason=sigusr1 in stderr`. This is a STAND defect and not a runtime one: publication of a trace dump is not promised to precede an exit the program itself is racing towards, and the row demanded that promise -- the same shape as the far-cancel row closed on 2026-08-29. It also read its output buffers while the child was still writing to them, which is a data race in the test process on every failure path. FIXED in the same commit as this entry: the stand waits for the `TRACE_NET reason=sigusr1 ` line itself before continuing the request loop, bounded by a deadline that only decides how fast a genuine loss is reported, and the buffers are read under a lock. The prefix is the whole record because this run also sets `SURGE_TRACE_EXEC`, whose dump carries the same reason. **THE FIX EXPOSED A SECOND ASSERTION IN THE SAME ROW, and it was wrong in the same way.** With the dump arriving deterministically MID-RUN rather than whenever it happened to win, `io_poll_rebuilds != io_poll_calls` began firing: `rt_net_trace_poll_start_enabled` (`runtime/native/rt_net_trace.c:227-228`) increments those two on adjacent relaxed lines, so a live cut can land between them. Equality is a property of a QUIESCENT cut and the exit dump is one; the live dump is bounded instead by the number of lanes that can sit inside that pair, which is the shard count reported on the same line. Bounding it at one would have moved the flake from `sometimes the dump is missing` to `sometimes two shards polled together` rather than removing it. | Closed 2026-08-30 | Epic 23b Wave D (W8, the aggregate as a count) | The aggregate reports twenty of twenty on five consecutive runs at one tree on the dedicated machine, with this row inside it. |
| RV2-DEBT-309 | **THIRTY-ONE RUNTIME ENTRY POINTS ANSWER `NULL` ON REFUSAL AND GENERATED CODE STORES THE ANSWER UNTESTED; SEVENTEEN OF THEM ARE THE FILESYSTEM.** Established 2026-08-29 by classifying all 112 pointer-answering declarations in the LLVM backend's ABI list -- the only roster an entry point cannot hide behind, since a function not declared there does not link. Four classes came out of it: 6 tested by generated code, 72 that report their own refusal, 3 that answer NULL for a reason that is not a refusal, and these 31. The filesystem is 17 of them (`rt_fs_open`, `_read`, `_write`, `_seek`, `_close`, `_flush`, `_read_file`, `_write_file`, `_cwd`, `_metadata`, `_mkdir`, `_read_dir`, `_remove_dir`, `_remove_file`, `_file_metadata`, `_file_name`, `_file_type`): `fs_make_error` and `fs_make_success_*` answer NULL when `rt_tag_alloc` refuses, and the emitter stores that into the FsResult slot, after which the `compare` reads a discriminant through it. Sockets are 9 more by the identical route, and five carry their own answer (`rt_entropy_bytes`, `rt_argv`, `rt_heap_stats`, `rt_string_bytes_view`, `rt_term_read_event`). Confirmed live rather than theoretical: compiling `testdata/llvm_parity/file_rw_smoke.sg` emits `call ptr @rt_fs_open(...)` followed by a store and a read through the answer, with no test between them. The count is PINNED as a number in the emitter census, and a separate pin holds 25 more where a refusal is SWALLOWED rather than answered -- the bignum promotion path drops a failed limb allocation and `a + b` answers `b`, which is a legal value and cannot be caught by any test the emitter could write. Two pins, not one, so closing one family cannot be paid for out of the other. Not fixed with the allocation guard because the answer differs in kind: these are result-carrying types whose caller CAN be made to handle a refusal, where the guarded six are values whose caller cannot. Deciding that is a language question about what an `FsResult` says when the runtime could not build one. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-29 -- THE BOUNDARY IS 'VALID RESULT OR TERMINAL FATAL', AND IT IS WRITTEN IN THE MODEL RATHER THAN DERIVED.** An ordinary filesystem or network error stays a value of the result type; a refusal to allocate the result TAG or the error object is FATAL. `OutOfMemory` is NOT added to any result type -- a value reporting that a value could not be created needs the storage that was just refused, so it does not solve the problem it reports. All thirty-one entry points get that boundary stated in their own contract, and there is no third answer for generated code to store. The ruling deliberately does not rest on the storage model's Non-Goal about recoverable out-of-memory: it is now `docs/RUNTIME_V2.md`, section 'Refusal Of A Result Type's Own Storage'. | Open | Epic 23b Wave F -- the 31 entry points answer or stop | The 31 either test their answer or carry a refusal the program can read, and the pinned number moves to zero rather than being raised. |
| RV2-DEBT-311 | **A VALGRIND ROW SOMETIMES TAKES SIXTY TIMES ITS USUAL DURATION, AND THE HARNESS USED TO KILL THE ONLY EVIDENCE.** `TestRuntimeV2ChannelHandleValgrindZero/outlives_scope` inside `runtime-v2-heap-check`. MEASURED over the five runs of the W8 count on the dedicated machine, all at `45c54b22`, and the distribution is the finding: **182.01s (timed out at the 180s budget, RED), 3.01s, 43.48s (PASSED), 3.03s, 3.03s.** The four sibling rows of the same test sat at 2.94-3.03s in every one of those runs, so the spread belongs to this row and not to the host. On the development machine the row alone on a quiet host measured **0 red of 23**, the same two-machine disagreement as RV2-DEBT-308. **THE 43.48s RUN CORRECTS WHAT I WROTE FIRST.** From run 1 alone -- a timeout whose entire output was Memcheck's four-line banner, no witness, no leak summary -- I concluded the guest "did not run slowly, it did not move", and called it a HANG. Run 3 shows the row completing after fourteen times its normal duration and answering green. So what is established is a heavy-tailed stall that sometimes exceeds the budget, and what is NOT established is that anything is permanently wedged: a timed-out run cannot tell a lost wakeup from a long tail, which is precisely why it is not a measurement. The two have different fixes, and one run of evidence pointed at the wrong one. Note also that 180s is headroom and not tightness -- a green `runtime-v2-heap-check` takes 132s in total, and the failing one took 282s. **THE INSTRUMENT IS BUILT AND LANDED**, since the next occurrence is the only thing that can settle it: `runBinaryUnderValgrind` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_crossing_strict_census_e2e_test.go`) now photographs the process BEFORE the kill -- per-thread `/proc` state plus a CPU-tick delta that separates "every thread parked" from "a core spinning", and `vgdb --pid=N v.info scheduler`, which ptraces valgrind into answering even when every guest thread is inside a syscall and prints each thread's scheduler status and stack in the GUEST's own symbols, which a native debugger attached to valgrind cannot do. Verified live against valgrind 3.22.0, the version the self-hosted lane pins on both hosts. **BUILDING IT EXPOSED A DEFECT IN ITSELF, WHICH IS WHY THE PROBE HAS ITS OWN STAND.** vgdb finds valgrind's gdbserver FIFO under `TMPDIR`, and the helper hands the guest an explicit environment while a plain `exec.Command` inherits this process's -- so the first draft answered `no FIFO found matching pid N`, which reads as "the guest was unreachable" when the truth is "the probe looked in the wrong directory". `TestRuntimeV2ValgrindWedgeProbeReachesTheGuestUnderItsOwnTmpdir` starts valgrind under a TMPDIR of its own and requires guest thread state back; it is red on the revert with exactly that message. A MEASUREMENT HAZARD OF THIS SHARED WORKTREE was also proved: two iterations of an earlier rate arm reported a timeout after **2 seconds**, which was another lane's temporary valgrind budget landing in the working tree mid-count -- a loop that measures the tree measures whatever the tree is at that instant, so a rate stand for this row belongs in a worktree pinned to a commit. **MEASURED TO AN ANSWER 2026-08-30, AND IT IS THE ROW'S OWN TOPOLOGY.** 200 replays of the PREBUILT binary directly under valgrind on the dedicated machine, ceiling 600s, as an INTERLEAVED A/B on exactly one variable -- odd iterations unpinned (what the gate runs), even iterations `SURGE_THREADS=1` -- interleaved rather than blocked so that box drift cannot be confounded with the variable, and prebuilt so that a slow LLVM build cannot be mistaken for a slow run. **Unpinned: 4 of 100 ran >=10s, longest 163s. `SURGE_THREADS=1`: 0 of 100 ran >=10s, longest 1s.** Nothing exceeded the 600s ceiling in either arm, which independently confirms the correction above: the row is slow, never stuck. The mechanism is visible in the photographs the new probe takes. At 10s into a slow iteration, ~50 guest threads are parked in `futex_wait_queue` with exactly ONE running and the process burning 207 CPU ticks per 2s -- one core saturated, not a deadlock -- and `vgdb` puts the main thread in `rt_task_await` (`rt_async_task.c:401`) on a `pthread_cond_wait`. `envWithStdlib` does not pin topology, so the guest starts `sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN)` carriers; under valgrind every thread serialises onto one core, so the idle carriers' pre-park loops compete for the same serialised CPU as the one lane that has to finish the task. That is a mechanism which produces a heavy tail that RECOVERS, which is the observed shape, and it is invisible on a box with few cores -- which is why the development machine sees 0 of 23. Suspects previously listed by area (`9d710bcb`, `b303a213`, `3479954c`, `59b835aa`) are NOT implicated: the A/B moves the tail with a runtime environment variable while the binary is byte-identical, so no commit needs to be bisected for it. **THE ROOT IS NAMED BY LINE, AND PINNING WOULD HIDE IT.** The parallel lane read the same guest stacks and got the line; verified here independently against the source. The only running thread's stack is byte-identical across two separate occurrences: `read` -> `rt_transport_wake_drain` (`rt_transport.c:149`) -> `rt_transport_try_drain_one` (`rt_transport.c:356`) -> `rt_remote_spawn_drain_inbound_locked` (`rt_remote_spawn.c:414`) -> `rt_worker_main` (`rt_worker_turn.c:134`). `rt_worker_turn.c:132` takes `rt_shard_lock` and 134 calls the drain immediately; the `rt_lane_holds_shard` branch of `try_drain_one` then calls `wake_drain` WITHOUT releasing that lock, while a neighbouring carrier sits in `rt_shard_lock` from `rt_net_begin_poll_on_shard`. **`rt_transport_wake_drain` is a `for(;;)` around `read` that exits only on EAGAIN and has NO iteration bound, while its own caller does have one -- `RT_TRANSPORT_DRAIN_TURN_LIMIT` = 16 (`rt_transport.h:83`).** The bound the design placed on draining is bypassed one level below it: the outer loop counts messages, the inner one reads wake bytes without counting. The reading offered with that line was that a peer writes wake bytes faster than they are read, keeping the loop off EAGAIN and spinning under the shard lock. **THAT MECHANISM IS REFUTED, AND THE BOUND WOULD HAVE BEEN AN INERT FIX.** Checked against the source rather than carried forward, the loop cannot spin, for two independent reasons. (1) `rt_lane_holds_shard` is exact -- `rt_lane.c:106-129` sets the thread-local immediately after `pthread_mutex_lock` and clears it immediately before `pthread_mutex_unlock` -- so that branch really does hold the shard mutex, and all three `rt_transport_wake_write` call sites (`rt_transport.c:314`, `321`, `462`) sit on paths holding that same shard's lock (`rt_transport_enqueue_locked`, `rt_transport_shutdown_wake_all`). No peer can put a byte into the pipe while the drainer is inside the loop, so "a peer writing faster than they are read" is a state the lock discipline forbids. (2) Independently of the lock: line 311 writes a wake byte only when `park_state == PARKED`, and a shard whose worker is spinning is RUNNING, so `enqueue_locked` takes the elision arm instead (`transport_wake_elisions++`, line 324) and writes nothing at all. The loop therefore drains at most the pipe's standing content -- 65536-byte capacity against a 64-byte buffer, so at most 1024 reads -- then breaks on EAGAIN. Unbounded in SHAPE, bounded in BEHAVIOUR, and nowhere near 180s. The cheap self-check that catches this without any of the above: Rule 13 wants a stand that goes red on the revert, and no such stand can be written here, because nothing can keep this loop off EAGAIN. **A root you cannot write a failing test against is not the root.** **THE PHOTOGRAPH NAMES A DIFFERENT LOOP, AND THE LINE NUMBER IS WHAT DISCRIMINATES.** The frame is `rt_worker_main` (`rt_worker_turn.c:134`) in all five occurrences. Line 134 is the TOP-OF-TURN drain -- the one that already carries `RT_TRANSPORT_DRAIN_TURN_LIMIT`. The unbounded `limit == 0` drain is line 136, inside the inner `while`. A thread stuck below 136 would be photographed at 136; being photographed at 134 five times out of five means the thread enters a FRESH OUTER TURN each time. So the loop that spins is the outer `for(;;)` at `rt_worker_turn.c:127`, and `read` is merely the one syscall each turn makes, which is why a sampler lands there every time. The rest of the photograph agrees: one runnable thread, ~49 parked at `rt_worker_turn.c:183`, one core saturated, self-recovering. **So `SURGE_THREADS=1` is the MEASUREMENT that isolated the variable, NOT the fix, and pinning the row would still hide a shipping-code defect that hurts any multi-carrier program.** The earlier framing of this as an owner question of pin-vs-budget stays withdrawn, for the reason above rather than the one first given. **SURVIVING HYPOTHESIS, NOT YET MEASURED.** The outer turn re-enters only while `worker_next_ready` (line 135) keeps yielding an id -- otherwise the thread sinks into the inner `while` and parks at 183 like its peers. The arm that burns a whole turn for no progress is `rt_worker_turn.c:195-199`: `get_task` returns NULL or the task is already `TASK_DONE`, so the turn unlocks and `continue`s having done nothing. A stale or repeatedly re-queued ready id produces exactly that -- a hot loop ending when the race resolves, which is a heavy tail that recovers. THE EXPERIMENT THAT SETTLES IT is cheap: two `vgdb` samples ~2s apart on one wedged process, reading `ctx->net_tick`, which line 129 increments once per outer turn. A large advance confirms the outer loop is turning and closes out any remaining reading of a single stuck call; no advance refutes this entry and sends it back to the inner loop. A counter on the 195-199 arm then separates "spinning on dead ids" from "doing real work". Nothing should be written against this row until that sample exists. **THAT HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED, AND THE PHOTOGRAPHS ALREADY TAKEN REFUTE IT -- NO NEW SAMPLE WAS NEEDED.** The probe stated its own decision rule: a sample landing inside task-execution frames refutes the dead-id reading. Three of the five photographs in `/root/dist/slow.txt` contain exactly that, and they were read for the running thread only the first time. Threads are inside `rt_task_poll` (`rt_async_task.c:220` and `:223`) below `__surge_poll_call`, blocked in `wake_task_with_policy` (`rt_task_park.c:94`) and `rt_task_entitlement_begin_take` (`rt_task_entitlement.c:84`); other threads are inside `mark_done` (`rt_task_complete.c:303`) blocked on `rt_control_lock` (`rt_lane.c:59`). Real user tasks are being polled, woken and completed throughout the stall, which is what "spinning on dead ids" says cannot be happening. **THE SECOND CORRECTION IS TO THE PARK COUNT, AND IT CHANGES THE SHAPE.** "~49 parked at `rt_worker_turn.c:183`" is true of ONE photograph (iter 45: 20 threads at 183). In the other four, 19 to 21 threads are blocked at `rt_worker_turn.c:132` -- `rt_shard_lock` -- which is a CONVOY on the shard mutex, not parking. So the stall is neither a wedge nor a spin on nothing: it is lock contention. The dedicated machine reports `nproc` = 16, the default topology is one shard by N carriers, and every carrier's turn takes shard 0's lock at 132 while `mark_done` takes the process-global control lock. Under valgrind, which runs exactly one guest thread at a time, that convoy is the heavy tail, and it explains every measured fact at once -- one core saturated, self-recovery at 43.48s, `SURGE_THREADS=1` at 0 of 100, sibling rows flat, and a development box with fewer cores seeing 0 of 23. **A NAMED COST ON THAT CRITICAL SECTION IS FIXED IN THIS COMMIT, AND THE SCOPE CLAIMED FOR IT IS SMALL.** `rt_transport_try_drain_one` and `rt_transport_drain_inbound_locked` called `rt_transport_wake_drain` whenever the inbound queue was empty -- **including when the pop removed nothing, which is what an idle worker turn does every time** -- so every turn of every carrier spent a `read(2)` inside the shard's critical section to learn that a pipe nobody wrote to is empty. Skipping it is safe by the lock discipline already established above: a wake byte is written only under that shard's lock and only immediately after a push (`rt_transport.c:314`), so a byte can exist only while the queue is non-empty, and whoever removes the last message owns the drain. A caller that removed nothing cannot be holding one. Stand: `empty-queue drain reads no wake pipe` in `TestRuntimeV2TransportSpineAcceptanceRows`, which also pins that the pass which DOES own the byte still reads the pipe exactly once, so the fix cannot degenerate into never draining; the negative control `-DRT_TRANSPORT_NEG_DRAIN_EMPTY_QUEUE` restores the old call and is red with `transport-spine-check: empty-queue drain read the wake pipe under the shard lock`. **WHAT THIS COMMIT DOES NOT CLAIM.** It shortens the contended critical section; it is NOT measured to close the row, and one syscall is not the whole of the convoy. **THE NAMED EXPERIMENT IS RUN, AND THE OUTER TURN IS CONFIRMED BY A NUMBER.** This entry asked for two `vgdb` samples ~2s apart reading `ctx->net_tick`, which `rt_worker_turn.c:129` increments once per outer turn, and said nothing should be written against the row until that sample existed. It exists. A slow iteration of the `547fc568` arm was caught live at 380s -- iteration 33 of the interleaved A/B in `/root/ab2`, which went on to be killed at the 600s ceiling with `rc=137` -- and sampled from outside: **`net_tick` = 56,253,144, then 56,747,951 about five seconds later. That is ~99,000 outer turns per second, and 56.2 million turns cumulative in 9.5 minutes.** A healthy replay of this same binary is 2 seconds. So the outer `for(;;)` at `rt_worker_turn.c:127` is turning at full speed; every remaining reading of a single stuck call is closed out; and the description's standing hypothesis that the 3-minute budget is simply too small is refuted with a number rather than an argument, because nothing that performs 56 million turns is waiting for more time. The process burned 200 CPU ticks per 2s throughout -- one core saturated, unchanged from the earlier photographs. Artifacts in `/root/live311`. **THE CONVOY IS GONE AT `547fc568`, AND THE TAIL IS NOT.** Eight `v.info scheduler` photographs spanning 25 seconds of that same process give a census that does not move between them: 23 blocking workers parked at `rt_async_blocking.c:108`, **21 threads in `VgTs_Init` that never start**, the main thread in `rt_task_await` (`rt_async_task.c:401`), one io thread at `rt_worker_turn.c:99`, exactly ONE carrier parked at `rt_worker_turn.c:183`, one thread inside `mark_done` blocked on `rt_control_lock` (`rt_task_complete.c:303` -> `rt_lane.c:59`), one thread inside `mark_done` in `futex_wake` under `rt_done_cv_broadcast_after_done` (`rt_task_complete.c:381` -> `rt_done_cv.c:15`), and one `VgTs_Runnable`. **Not one thread is at `rt_worker_turn.c:132`.** The shard-lock convoy this entry last corrected its shape to is ABSENT here, so `547fc568` did change the mechanism, and the row stalls anyway. That is the third root this entry has had to withdraw, and the discriminator was the same each time: the census of the other N-1 threads, not the running one. **THE RUNNABLE THREAD IS EXECUTING TASKS, NOT REPEATING ONE CALL.** Its top frame differs in every sample -- `rt_scheduler_placement.c:38` (below `wake_task_with_policy` <- `wake_task` <- `rt_task_poll` at `rt_async_task.c:220` <- `poll_user_task` <- `poll_task` <- `rt_worker_main:207`), then `rt_async_waiter.c:5`, `rt_ready_queue.c:140` and `:129` (below `worker_next_ready` <- `rt_worker_main:135`), `rt_remote_spawn.c:408`, `rt_lane.c:81`, `rt_async_state.c:76`. That is a whole worker turn -- pop, poll, wake -- executed end to end, over and over. So `worker_next_ready` yields an id on essentially every turn, which is precisely the condition this entry already identified as what keeps the thread out of the park at 183. **WHAT IS STILL NOT NAMED, AND THE TWO EXPERIMENTS THAT NAME IT.** A pop per turn requires a push per turn, and the obvious candidate is refuted by reading rather than left standing: the re-wake at `rt_async_task.c:220` CANNOT double-push, because `wake_task_on_shard_locked` returns 0 without enqueueing for any task that is DONE, RUNNING, or already enqueued (`rt_task_park.c:52`). The identity of the task, or pair of tasks, cycling through the ready queue is therefore unknown, and no fix should be written until it is named. (1) Sample or count the popped `id` and `task->kind` on the `rt_worker_turn.c:195-199` and `:200-220` arms during a slow run; that says which task is polled 99,000 times a second. (2) `21 threads in VgTs_Init` is recorded here as an OBSERVATION and explicitly not as a mechanism: a photograph of a HEALTHY 2-second replay is the control that separates "this runtime's normal startup bookkeeping" from the thread starvation it superficially resembles, and it costs one sample. Asserting starvation before taking that control would repeat exactly the borrowed-mechanism error this entry and RV2-DEBT-312 have each already made once. **THE A/B IS RUNNING, AND ITS INTERIM READING DOES NOT FAVOUR THE FIX.** `/root/abrate2.sh 547fc568 200`: interleaved odd/even against the parent `d5ba88a1`, prebuilt and separately verified binaries, each arm in its own worktree pinned to its commit per Global Rule 17. At 37 of 200 the count is **new 4 slow of 19, old 1 slow of 18**. n is far too small to conclude anything at a ~4% rate, but the tail has plainly not vanished, and it is the NEW arm that hit the ceiling. One caveat stated rather than buried: that iteration was already 380s in and unrecoverable before any probe touched it, and only its classification as slow is clean -- its 600s duration is the ceiling and the repeated gdb stops together. **THE PUSHER IS NAMED, AND IT IS MONTHS OLDER THAN EVERY SUSPECT.** This entry has said since the `net_tick` measurement that a pop per turn requires a push per turn and that no fix should be written until the pusher is named. It is `rt_task_poll`, which registered a join waiter for every target kind EXCEPT `TASK_KIND_CHECKPOINT` and left `pending_key` invalid for that one case. An invalid `pending_key` is not "no park needed" -- it is the yield path: `emit_async.go:190` branches to PendBB on a 0 return, `rt_async_yield` finds no valid key and reports `POLL_YIELDED` rather than `POLL_PARKED` (`rt_async_poll.c:304`), and `apply_poll_outcome` pushes the awaiter straight back onto the inject queue (`rt_task_complete.c:484`). So `checkpoint().await()` did not wait for its checkpoint: it re-entered the ready queue on every turn and asked again, which is the pop's exact pair. Usually the checkpoint finishes within a few of those turns and the busy-yield is invisible; in the tail it does not, and one core saturates while the carriers behind it sit unstarted -- every measured fact of this entry, including the self-recovery. Nothing about a checkpoint needed the exemption: `mark_done` ends every completion with `wake_key_all_with_policy(join_key(id))` (`rt_task_complete.c:379`) and tests no kind. Fixed in `fd054b88`. The exemption dates to `25350bd1` (2026-01-05), months older than all four suspects the issue listed by area, so a bisect over them would have found a commit that moved the PROBABILITY rather than the one that introduced the defect. **RULE 13 IS SATISFIED BY A TEST AND NOT BY A RATE.** `fd054b88` shipped with a rate as its only stand, which answers with a probability and costs hours to read. Now that the mechanism is named by line the question is asked directly, in `TestRuntimeV2AwaitingACheckpointRegistersAJoinWaiter` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_checkpoint_await_registration_test.go`), on `runtime-v2-waiter-check`'s roster and therefore inside the aggregate. Two halves. `registers` builds a C stand over `runtime/native/*.c` with `-DRT_TEST_SYNC_POINTS`, has a task await a checkpoint PINNED in `TASK_WAITING`, and requires both that `SP_TASK_POLL_AFTER_JOIN_REGISTER` is reached and that the entry is really in the target shard's waiter store. The target is pinned rather than spawned because a live checkpoint is two turns from done -- `poll_checkpoint_task` yields once and then succeeds -- so an ordinary one may be DONE before the awaiter polls it, `rt_task_poll` would answer from its DONE fast path having registered nothing, and the stand would report whichever it drew. `no-kind-exemption` reads `rt_task_poll` and refuses ANY branch on the target's kind between the cancel gate and `prepare_park`, so the next kind added to this runtime cannot acquire the same exemption the same way. Measured in both directions: green on `fd054b88`, and on a revert of the `rt_async_task.c` hunk both halves are red, the behavioural one with `awaiting a checkpoint did not register a join waiter` and `joiner status=1`, which is READY -- the awaiter sitting in the ready queue instead of parked, which is the requeue itself. **THE A/B SEPARATES THE ARMS.** `/root/abrate3.sh fd054b88 200`, interleaved against the parent `e1e24cf2`, prebuilt and verified per arm: at 111 of 200 the count is **new 0 slow of 55, old 5 of 54**, and old's `iter 108` ran to the 600s ceiling and was killed with `rc=137` -- the first iteration in any arm of any of these campaigns that did NOT recover on its own. | Root named (`checkpoint().await()` registered no join waiter, `25350bd1`) and fixed in `fd054b88`; Rule 13 stand landed and red on the revert; the A/B reads 0 slow of 55 new against 5 of 54 old at 111/200 | Epic 23b Wave D -- W8 cannot report the aggregate green while this row is counted red | The A/B to 200, then `/root/w8fix.sh`, already queued behind it: the rate of RV2-DEBT-312's `http-owner` red over 40 runs, then **W8 as a count of runs at `fd054b88`**, which is this row's and the wave's exit. |
| RV2-DEBT-312 | **ONE HTTP CLIENT OF EIGHT NEVER GETS ITS RESPONSE, ONLY AT EIGHT SHARDS, AND WITHOUT VALGRIND.** `TestRuntimeV2HTTPOwnerLocalBehavior/shards-8` in `runtime-v2-http-owner-check`: `client 1 read response: read tcp 127.0.0.1:16052->127.0.0.1:16625: i/o timeout`. FOUND BY THE W8 COUNT, which is the point of making W8 a count -- **it is a SECOND intermittent row in the aggregate, not the one that was being chased**, and one exit code would have reported the roster green in four runs out of five. Measured across the five runs at `45c54b22` on the dedicated machine, and the row is otherwise metronomic: shards-8 took 2.38, 2.37, 2.38, 2.37 seconds and then **12.40s and failed**, while shards-1 and shards-2 passed in all five. So the W8 aggregate stands at **3 green, 2 red of 5**, and the two reds are different gates. It matters that this stand DOES pin topology (`SURGE_SHARDS` and `SURGE_THREADS` are both set to the shard count; only `SURGE_BLOCKING_THREADS` is left alone), because that rules out the tempting reading that the class is simply "rows that do not pin". **THE MECHANISM PREVIOUSLY BORROWED FROM RV2-DEBT-311 IS WITHDRAWN, BECAUSE IT WAS REFUTED THERE.** The reading was: more shards means more transport traffic, and the unbounded wake-drain loop at `rt_transport.c:149` spins under the shard lock for as long as a peer keeps the pipe non-empty, so a lane that owes a response does not get the lock in time. That loop cannot spin -- no peer can feed the pipe while the drainer holds the shard lock, and a RUNNING shard elides the wake write entirely; see 311 for the full refutation. So this row is back to having NO named mechanism. What 311 does still lend it, once 311's own outer-turn hypothesis is measured, is a SHAPE worth testing here: a worker burning outer turns without parking starves the lane that owes the response. That is a HYPOTHESIS and not a measurement -- unlike 311, no guest stack has been read for this row, because its first occurrence published nothing, and borrowing an unmeasured mechanism from a neighbouring row is exactly what went wrong the first time. What makes it worth stating is that it is falsifiable in one step: this row is not under valgrind, so it costs a rate stand rather than a serialised replay. **THE FIRST OCCURRENCE TAUGHT ALMOST NOTHING, FOR THE THIRD TIME.** The stand runs its fixture with `SURGE_TRACE_EXEC=1`, but `runtimeV2AcceptServer.fail` killed the server before reading its buffers, so the failure printed an empty stdout and an empty stderr -- the trace that would name which lane never answered is written by the process itself and had no chance to. Same shape as RV2-DEBT-310 and RV2-DEBT-311. FIXED IN THE SAME COMMIT AS THIS ENTRY, in the shared helper rather than in one stand: `fail` now signals `SIGUSR1` and waits for the live dump before killing, the buffers moved to `lockedBuffer` because they are now read while the child still writes, and the report carries a note distinguishing the outcomes that an empty section cannot -- dump arrived, fixture exited on the signal untraced, or **no dump within the budget while the fixture was still alive**, which is itself the finding, since the dump is drained at a safepoint and silence means no lane reached one. `TestRuntimeV2AcceptFixtureAnswersALiveTraceRequest` pins the mechanism against a healthy fixture and is red on the revert. | Open | Epic 23b Wave D -- W8 counts this red alongside RV2-DEBT-311 | A measured rate for shards-8 on the machine that fails it and the live trace of a failing occurrence read; if that trace shows the wake-drain spin, this row closes with RV2-DEBT-311's fix and its stand, and if it does not, it is its own defect and gets its own. |
